This file holds the **design content** --- candidate schemes, working hypotheses, and the consistency sweeps run against them. The parent keeps the **control surface**: status, open decisions, recommended order, verification, progress.

where its crossings are abstraction it is **invisible to fiction by design**, so breaking one is an **authoring** event and never an act in the world. **Then amended the same day by [multi-abstraction wrapping](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28)**, **settled 2026-08-29** --- a node in two abstractions raised *which boundary carries the relation?*, and **the question dissolved**: abstractions are partial, so **an abstraction may omit but may not contradict**, and no route is obligatory. **A chain is an independent referent whose *identity* is not an aggregation over legs** (legs still compose its extension), **referenceable from graphs holding none of its legs** (it always has legs *somewhere*) and usable as a terminal --- `CoarseEdge -[JustifiedBy]-> DetailEdge` --- which **answers [EA-10](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-10)'s *terminal of an edge* as leg-to-*chain*.** **Identity is a consistency affordance, not a representation constraint:** atomicity at the mutation site, **reconciliation** across the lattice, **atomic consistency rejected.** **Still open:** discriminator row 5, and **chain-terminal edge versus reified justification node** (against [EA-7](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-7)'s precedent). **Two more closed 2026-08-29, by work rather than by subtraction.** **The mixed pair reads *different legs*** --- no absorption, so chain membership is an **explicit authoring act** and no comparison mutates anything; the price is [item 10](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s assign/strip operations, specified here and built elsewhere. **And the unenforced hint is tolerable for *absence*, not for *wrongness*** --- a missing id makes a reasoner reinvent the match and fail openly, a wrong one makes it answer confidently about a relation it never saw --- **a hazard that is [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)-era rather than P8-added**, and item 8's to own. **Two items left the open list 2026-08-29, both by subtraction rather than by work.** **Chain self-intersection is *structurally impossible*, not forbidden** --- a crossing port is degree-2 and terminals are port-qualified, so a leg is a port-plus-direction and single-use forecloses a repeat; **no owner and no guard are owed**, only a falsifier (*a port admitting two crossings in the same direction*, which PQ-14's interior fan-in is not). **It deletes rather than resolves *the only case that would resurrect a per-leg identifier*: nothing does.** And **port coarseness was uncited, not unchecked** --- [PQ-14](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) has held it since 2026-08-10. **Not open: what hosts a chain-terminal edge.** Nothing fixes it; a reader finds the record through the chain's reverse index, as a reader of a node already does. **What that leaves is a requirement rather than a question** --- the index must cover referencing hosts and not only leg-holding ones, widening clause 5's index, priced [with the argument](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28). **One finding recorded there and deliberately not acted on:** two of the three grounds for EA-7's closure of *containment between edges* have moved, and this proposal leaves the re-open to whoever owns the row. **Still true:** **no corpus case has been *worked* against any of it** --- LC8 supplies the topology and is itself unworked --- its question row in the parent is **not yet written**. ~~its motivating demand is **unwitnessed in the corpus**~~ **--- struck 2026-08-29: the corpus search was run and the demand *is* witnessed.** Under [AB-54](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#settled-register) membership is **shard residency**, and EA-C1's `SolderedTo`, EA-C2's `Secures` and C6's candle-`Under`-span each put one relation's legs in two abstractions' shards, so **P8's ground moves from *symmetry* to *attestation*.** Full finding, its negative case, and the method failure that made the first run report empty, at [what it does not resolve](#what-it-does-not-resolve). **It amends no other row** --- [EA-8 needs nothing from it](#what-ea-8-already-booked-and-what-p8-discharges), having booked this obligation itself, and the citation runs upward. **P7 is skipped, not missing:** it is reserved for the `ludicCache` reducer. **--- Iteration 1 elected a [Prototype](#status-1) 2026-08-30 and booked as [Channel E](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order); **[CE1/CE1a](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order) landed the same day and [item 8](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires) closed with them, so the checklist is *ten of ten*.** **Read that as the discovery work being finished, not as P8 being settled** --- the tier is still Prototype, the field is built, and the evidence it exists to buy has not been collected yet.** *Prototype is a licence to build, not a promotion* --- the proposal stays provisional as design, and the tagged rollback set is one row |

## Proposal P1: single-host objects + hosting at the containing node

~~**Status: adopted provisionally as [H1](#working-hypotheses), 2026-08-05.** Recorded first as an unjudged proposal, then promoted --- **not** to a decision, but to a *working hypothesis whose job is to ground further design*. Every answer derived from it is tagged `H1-dependent` so the rollback set stays known; see [Working hypotheses](#working-hypotheses) for the tagging test, the falsification cases, and the named rollback triggers.~~ **Status: withdrawn 2026-08-07, along with H1 --- see [H1's status line](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) for the falsification.** Left in place, struck rather than removed, per this plan's supersession rule: the clause table above argues against this proposal's own numbered items by number, and cannot be read without them. It touches AB-4, AB-8, AB-9, AB-16, AB-17 and the AB-21 identity question, so it is written up here rather than inside any one row.

### The proposal

1. **Remove multi-host capability for Objects.** An Object has exactly one host. (Features may remain multi-host --- see the note below.)
2. **Give Objects a direct lookup of placement data on their `Meta`**, rather than a variable set of adjacency rows.
3. **Spanning things are modelled as parts.** "Tie rope to rock" then "drop rope into abyss" improvises **two rope parts**: one stays in the cliff-top room tied to the rock, one goes into the abyss.
4. **The whole is hosted at the node high enough in the containment tree to contain all of its parts.** The rope object itself hosts in the **Area** containing both rooms, with part connections (likely two-way) to its parts.
5. **Migration is automatic and bidirectional.** Haul the rope up, all parts in one room, and the rope migrates down from Area into that room.

### What it buys (assessed against code, 2026-08-05)

| Gain | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| **Every threatened invariant survives** | `computeEndStateRoomDiff`'s end-state scrub stays correct; adjacency stays single-valued; `MutationKernelTransferStep`'s singular `toHostId` needs no widening. |
| **The contract clause is vindicated, not stale** | "At most one room per `OBJECT#`" becomes **true as written** --- the design moves to meet the contract instead of the contract being rewritten. Materially cheaper than the widening path, and it retires the three-way disagreement recorded under [Multi-host presence](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#multi-host-presence-what-is-actually-true-today). |
| **The relational-edge problem dissolves** | Rope tied to rock is an ordinary **same-host** edge between the cliff-top *part* and the rock. This was the sharpest structural objection to multi-room ropes; it needs zero new machinery. |
| **Presentation is correct by construction** | Each room's `graph.objectIds` holds the part actually present, so no room renders a whole rope it only partly contains. |
| **The drift class genuinely shrinks** | A single-valued host cannot exhibit multi-room drift. `repairObjectPlacementDrift`'s multi-room scrub branch becomes **structurally impossible**; only field-vs-graph lag survives. A deletion, not a relocation. |
| **Improvisation is reused unchanged for parts** | A part is an Object; minting one is minting an `OBJECT#` into `ASSET#IMPROVISATION`. Only the composition edge is new (already tracked as gap 1). |

### What it costs --- concentrated almost entirely in the host containment tree

| Cost | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| **Area is further away than "parked"** | `EphemeraMembershipHostId = EphemeraRoomId \| EphemeraCharacterId` ([`ephemeraPositionAdjacency.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraPositionAdjacency.ts)), and **there is no `EphemeraAreaId` in `baseClasses.ts` at all**. Area exists only WML/asset-side (`AREA#downtown`, `AREA#region`). This introduces an ephemera-side identity, not merely a deferred host kind. |
| **Containment is authored downward, with no upward index** | `StandardAreaData` carries a `positionGraph` listing its members, so "Area contains Room" is nodes in the Area's graph. **Nothing maps room -> area.** The rule needs the upward direction on every part move --- structurally the same missing thing as the parked reverse-edge-reference index. |
| **Is it a tree at all?** | Area membership is authored blueprint **merged at participation order**, so containment may be **asset-stack dependent** --- while an object's host must be world truth, not perspective-relative. `AREA#region` beside `AREA#downtown` also suggests nesting depth. And two rooms with no common Area leave the LCA undefined without a world root. **This is the load-bearing unknown for P1.** |
| **Migration is an invariant needing transactional cascade** | Every part move recomputes its whole's host, which may move the whole, which may cascade to wholes-of-wholes. `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once, up front**, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered --- so plan time must walk composition *upward* to pre-lock every host the cascade might touch. |

### What it changes conceptually

- **Two kinds of hosting, needing a name.** Room-hosting means *physically there*; Area-hosting is *bookkeeping*. Area-hosted objects presumably never surface in affordances --- state it, or someone writes compose that surfaces them.
- **It makes AB-9 acute rather than dodging it --- and [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) escalates that from acute to routine.** With all parts back in one room, the whole **and** its parts sit in that room's graph: "you see a rope, a rope end, and a rope end." Description must pick a level, unavoidably. C5 shows this is not a transient state during manipulation but the **permanent steady state of anything anchored**: a chain mounted at one end and attached to a chest at the other sits in the cell's graph as three nodes indefinitely. Level selection is therefore a default read-path concern, not an edge case.
- ~~**Migration-down and reabsorption may be the same event (AB-17).** Coiling the rope is all-parts-co-located, which is both "migrate the whole into the room" and "dissolve the parts."~~ **Falsified 2026-08-05 by [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends).** Co-location and load-bearing are independent: the chain's parts are all in one cell *and* still carry the relations anchoring it, so the whole migrates down while the parts must persist. **P1 collapsed two conditions into one** --- AB-17's criterion was already written as a disjunction ("no part is separately referenced **or** separately positioned") and H1 clause 5 tests only the second. The two events stay separate; **AB-17** carries the split criteria. Struck rather than softened, per this plan's rule on recording supersessions.

### What it does not resolve

- **How parts *connect* is out of P1's scope.** Parts plus a whole give membership, not structure. **This is AB-26, and it is not a sequence** --- connections are adjacency-mediated edges ("the clifftop end connects to the shaft section by way of the 'into the abyss' exit"), which branch. P1 and AB-26 are complementary, not competing: P1 decides *where the whole is hosted*, AB-26 decides *how the parts relate*.
- **Multiple parents (AB-13) is fine**, incidentally: each whole computes its own LCA independently, so an object being part of two wholes creates no conflict.

### On the two sub-proposals

**`Meta` direct lookup:** single-host makes the reverse index single-valued, collapsing a variable row set into one field --- a genuine simplification of *shape*. It remains a dual write with the same atomicity discipline and the same "`positionGraph` wins" conflict rule, so it **shrinks** the drift class rather than removing it.

**Features stay multi-host:** this is **forward-looking, not preserved.** Features are not graph nodes at all today (the node `tag` union is `'Character' | 'Object'`). It is consistent with their documented narrowing --- never membership-moved, never a relation subject, therefore no transfer semantics to get wrong --- but it describes a capability yet to be built.

**And the phrasing may be wrong, not just unbuilt (added 2026-08-05, see AB-30).** "Features stay multi-host" presumes Features are the kind of thing that *has* hosts. If a wall between two rooms is better modelled as an **edge** than as a node, it is not multi-host --- it is a non-member, and the sub-proposal is answering a question that does not arise. Note the asymmetry this sub-proposal currently carries unremarked: H1's entire thrust is **removing** multi-host for Objects, while this clause **preserves** it for Features. That is either a real distinction with a reason, or the clause is describing the problem in the vocabulary H1 just abandoned. AB-30 decides which.

---

## Working hypotheses

**Why this section exists (2026-08-05).** This plan's governing posture has been that *deciding is premature* --- there is useful discussion left in most of these questions, and closing them early would foreclose it. That posture is unchanged. What changed is the recognition that a question can also be under-discussed for the opposite reason: with no grounding assumption, downstream rows have nothing to argue *against*, and the discussion stays abstract rather than premature.

**A working hypothesis is the middle move.** Adopt a candidate explicitly as a *test of how well it grounds further design*, tag every answer derived from it, and keep the derived set visible so it can be rolled back as a unit. If a hypothesis dies, the paper trail says exactly which answers die with it and where to branch from with a different candidate. **This is not a decision, and a hypothesis surviving is not evidence it is right** --- only that nothing has yet killed it.

### The tagging test, and why it is the point

Mark a row **`H1-dependent`** only if its answer is **derived from** the hypothesis --- it would not make sense, or would be answered differently, if the hypothesis were withdrawn. Do **not** mark a row merely *compatible* with it.

| | Example | Tag |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Derived from** | "Part-connections need an Area-level graph" --- only holds because P1 hosts the whole at the Area | `H1-dependent` |
| **Merely compatible** | AB-26's "adjacency *licenses* an edge, never derives one" --- true under framing (a) as well | untagged |

**The distinction is not bookkeeping; the count is a measurement.** A tag applied to everything makes rollback total, which is the same as having no trail at all. Applied honestly, the size of the dependent set says how much work H1 is actually doing: a small set means the hypothesis was cheap and most of the design stands on its own; a large one means it is load-bearing and the lock deserves more scrutiny than it has had. Record that count at [Phase 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order)'s consistency pass, where the set is finally known.

### H1: single-host objects, hosting at the containing node

**Status: Withdrawn 2026-08-07, superseded by [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice).** [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) falsified H1 clause 1a --- a part and a multi-host whole are jointly unsatisfiable the instant one object occupies both roles, which C11 shows happens with no contrivance. H1 clauses 4 and 5 are entailed by H1 clause 1b, which was already under sustained pressure and never independently defended; with 1a gone and no clause of 1 surviving, the conjunction as a whole no longer stands. H1 clause 2 was never tested. **Left in place below rather than deleted --- the falsification is itself load-bearing evidence, per this plan's supersession rule.** The six-row `H1-dependent` set is rolled back per the mechanics recorded [below](#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07); see H3 for what carries forward.

**Statement:** [Proposal P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node), in full.

#### H1 is a conjunction, and its clauses are tested separately

Adopted 2026-08-05 after "H1 is untested against a serious attempt" turned out to be **too coarse a statement to be true or false.** [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) is a passed discriminating test --- of *one clause*. Tracking per-clause is what makes the next tests focused rather than another all-purpose scenario, and it makes the untested surface visible instead of averaged away.

**They are also not independent, which the first version of this table got wrong.** Corrected 2026-08-06: **H1 clause 1 applied to *wholes* generates H1 clause 4, and H1 clause 4 generates H1 clause 5.** Enforce single-host, decompose a spanning thing into parts, and the *whole* still exists --- if it is a graph node it needs a host, and its parts are by construction spread across rooms. So "where does the whole live" must be answered, and H1 clause 4 is that answer. Then, since the host is defined as *the node containing all parts* and parts move, the host must change: **H1 clause 5 is not a feature, it is what maintaining H1 clause 4's invariant consists of.** Three of the five clauses are therefore one commitment with two entailments, and testing them as peers would have over-counted H1's independent content.

| # | Clause | Tests run | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1a** | **Parts** have exactly one host | ~~implied by C7's decomposition result~~ --- **[C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine), 2026-08-07** | ~~**Nearly free.** Follows from H1 clause 3: a part spanning two rooms would be decomposed further, since the criterion *is* "a distinct locus is needed here"~~ **Falsified 2026-08-07.** C11's `StringSpan` is a part of the string *and* a whole of its sub-spans **at the same moment**, so 1a and a multi-host reading of wholes are **jointly unsatisfiable** --- the same object takes both rules. The justification was also never a derivation: *decompose further* has **no base case**, since extent can always be cut again. **The "hold even 1a loosely" caveat below was the whole objection and was carried forward as a hedge.** With 1a gone, **no clause of 1 survives** |
| **1b** | **Wholes** have exactly one host | **AB-32's same-mechanism requirement** (2026-08-06) --- an argument, not a scenario | **Survives, at a stated price.** Single-hosting is compatible with scale-invariance **only if reference is traversal-shaped as the clean rule** ([three positions](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs)). Still what generates H1 clauses 4 and 5; see [the non-positional-whole alternative](#candidate-drop-h1-clause-1b-and-the-whole-chain-with-it), now narrowed to extent. **[P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) supplies the structure position 2 needs**, and changes 1b's price without settling it. **Under sustained pressure from four directions as of 2026-08-06 --- [tallied in one place](#the-motivation-stated-plainly-2026-08-06), with the six-row rollback set named so the call can be made with the number in view.** Still not withdrawn |
| **2** | Placement is a direct lookup on `Meta` | **none, and nothing is queued** | **Unexamined.** The most conspicuous gap in the register --- this clause has attracted no scrutiny at all across the whole design conversation |
| **3** | Spanning things are modelled as **parts** | [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) --- discriminating, competitor **failed** | **Passed.** Framing (a) cannot represent Ariadne's thread; H1 can. The strongest result available from a discriminating test --- not survival, but the alternative breaking |
| **4** | The whole hosts at the node containing all its parts | none --- *used* in C5, C6, C7, never *stressed* | **Untested; entailed by 1b --- and it acquired a cost 2026-08-06.** AB-25 is its test, and rollback trigger 1. **New charge from [PQ-7](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings):** hosting the whole away from its referrers is what breaks the same-host locality `removeObject`'s assert-and-throw depends on. Under multi-host + ports the node sits in every room that references it and the guarantee holds; under this clause room B references a port whose node is at the Area, and **nothing local can enforce dissolve-before-remove.** Downstream of AB-36 either way, but only this clause makes it unavoidable |
| **5** | Migration is automatic and bidirectional | [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) --- a worked operation, 2026-08-06 | **Untested, and now with nothing to test *if* multi-host + ports wins.** C9 coils a three-room rope by rewriting port egress: **no whole-migration occurs**, because the whole is present wherever its ports say. The queued concurrent-migration and thrash cases would target machinery that does not exist --- **third queued test set to dissolve rather than run.** Still entailed by 4, so it survives exactly as long as 1b does |

**Credit where it is due and no further: H1 clause 3 is not uniquely H1's.** AB-8's framing (c) proposed decomposition before P1 existed, and H1 adopts it --- so a (c)-with-multi-host variant passes C7 identically, which is why C7 earns no `H1-dependent` tag. **C7 vindicates decomposition, which H1 contains.**

~~**But that line of reasoning yields a cost reduction on H1 clause 1.** Parts are local by construction, so nothing needs multi-host and H1 clause 1 weakens from *remove the capability* to *nothing uses it*.~~ **Half wrong, corrected 2026-08-06.** The argument holds for **parts** and fails for **wholes** --- and the whole is precisely the object whose parts span. Only half the objects were checked. What survives is now **H1 clause 1a**; what the argument missed is now **H1 clause 1b**, which is not free at all. The surviving half is still worth having: P1's claimed gains --- compile gate, end-state planner, singular `toHostId`, per-room compose --- only require that no object *is* multi-present, so they are satisfied by 1a plus whatever answers 1b.

**Two things still separate unused from removed** for H1 clause 1a: the contract's "at most one room per `OBJECT#`" is only *true* if enforced rather than merely unexercised, and Features sit outside the decomposition story entirely (AB-30). **Hold even 1a loosely:** it follows from the decomposition *rule*, not as a theorem --- a lazy decomposition could leave a part spanning.

**And that last sentence was right, 2026-08-07, in a stronger sense than it was meant.** It was written as a caution about lazy implementation; [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) shows the rule has **no base case at all**, so the theorem does not exist to be approximated. **Recorded as a process finding, not only a technical one:** this is the second time a stated reservation in this plan was carried forward for days and then treated as settled by the reasoning built on top of it (the first was AB-16's tag). A caveat that nothing acts on becomes an assumption.

#### Three positions on reference, and what H1 clause 1b actually costs

**Raised 2026-08-06 as the sharpest pressure H1 clause 1b has taken, and the first arriving from a *requirement* rather than a scenario.** The requirement (now on **AB-32**): *reference and perception must reach a spanning whole and a local whole by the same mechanism.* If "the thread in room B" and "the flashlight on the table" resolve by different machinery, that difference propagates into every consumer.

**The argument as first put:** you can have **one scale-invariant processing rule** or **single-hosting**, not both --- because a single-hosted spanning whole cannot be a node in each room it passes through, so reaching it needs a second path, and a second path is an epicycle. **Substantially right, and it turns out there are three positions rather than two.**

| Position | Reference mechanism | Price |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1. Multi-host the whole** | Local lookup everywhere --- the thread is a node in A, B and C; the flashlight is a node in A | **Not free, and this cost was missing from the original framing.** [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) showed one node cannot record *where along* the thread anything attaches, so segments are **still** required --- room B then holds both the thread node and its local segment, two nodes for overlapping things, and "pull the thread" in B has two plausible bindings. Every mutation writes N graphs. **Substantially re-priced 2026-08-06 by [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)** --- see below |
| **2. Single-host, traversal as the *clean* rule** | Local nodes **plus their reachable ancestors**. Zero hops for an undecomposed flashlight (the node present *is* the whole); one hop for a thread | **Breaks the dichotomy.** A loop that sometimes iterates zero times is one mechanism, not two --- differing hop count is not an epicycle. Cost is that it **inverts clean and fast**: today's `graph.objectIds` lookups become the *optimisation*, needing a stated validity condition. Legitimate [dirt](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) by that section's own test --- a lookup returning what the traversal would return is "the clean answer, computed faster" |
| **3. Single-host, lookup as the clean rule** | Local lookup, plus a separate path for spanning wholes | **This is the position the argument kills**, and correctly: the second path is not an optimisation of the rule but a *different rule for a subclass* --- an epicycle in the precise sense |

**The narrowed dichotomy, which is the durable form:** ~~scale-invariance or single-hosting~~ --- **you can have single-hosting or lookup-as-the-clean-rule, not both.** Scale-invariance survives single-hosting only if the clean rule is traversal-shaped.

**And the plan is currently sitting in position 3 without having chosen it.** [Part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger) was introduced as the answer for spanning wholes while local lookup remained the unexamined norm for everything else --- **two paths, never reconciled.** So the epicycle this argument predicts is not a future risk; it is already in the design as written, and was introduced the moment the spanning case got its own answer.

**Position 1 was re-priced 2026-08-06 by [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings), and the table above is now understated in its favour.** The charge against it was C7's: one node cannot record *where along*, so segments are still required and room B holds two overlapping nodes with an ambiguous binding. **Presence ports answer that without single-hosting** --- room B holds the thread node plus a **presence-port reference**, the presence port names exactly which segment, and the where-along record lives in the rope's interior graph. Multi-hosting under presence ports is also cheaper than priced: the rope is *listed* in two rooms but there is exactly **one rope graph**, so nothing interior is duplicated --- closer to a presence index than to real multi-hosting. **H1 clause 1b therefore takes a new hit: not a falsification, but its competitor got materially cheaper.** The narrowed dichotomy below is unaffected; the choice *within* it shifts.

**Position 2 acquired a structural realisation the same day: [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation).** `positionGraph` stopping at host boundaries is the clean traversal model; `positionCache` is the expanded denormalization that makes it fast. **That is what position 2 was describing without having a mechanism for**, and it is the first thing in this design to *pass* the [clean/dirty](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) test rather than be excused by it.

**One reason position 2 is cheaper than it first sounds:** C7's derived-presence question already pushes *description* toward traversal independently --- something must work out that a thread passes through room B. If description traverses anyway, reference traversing too is one mechanism serving both rather than a new cost.

#### Candidate: drop H1 clause 1b, and the whole chain with it

**Recorded 2026-08-06 as a candidate, not adopted --- and narrowed the same day to *extent only*.** Follows directly from the entailment above: if 1b generates 4 and 5, then declining 1b removes all three.

**Scope correction, [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it).** As first written this candidate was derived **entirely from C7**, an extent case, and generalised without being checked against a containment case sitting in the same document. Applied to a flashlight it is destructive: decomposition would make the whole non-positional, so the flashlight leaves the room's graph, affordance compose describes three components, and --- because the referent catalog reads the same `objectIds` --- **"pick up the flashlight" stops grounding**. Looking closely at a thing would make it unreferrable, while fictionally nothing happened.

**So the candidate holds for extent and inverts for containment:** extent makes the *whole* non-positional, containment makes the *parts* non-positional. Positional status looks like a property of the **composition kind**, not a global rule. ~~which is AB-16's distinction surfacing positionally~~ --- **that inference was withdrawn 2026-08-06 and must not be re-drawn from here.** This paragraph is a *scoping decision about this candidate*: the candidate breaks containment, so it is confined to extent. Reading the resulting asymmetry back as evidence that the two part kinds differ is circular, because the asymmetry is what this paragraph just imposed. See **AB-16** for the full withdrawal. Everything below is therefore scoped to **extent wholes**; the LCA machinery it would remove is correspondingly confined to **scattered containment** rather than eliminated. **Untested premise:** that containment and extent are cleanly separable --- a ship's rigging may be both.

**The observation that motivates it: nothing reads the whole's host.** P1 states Area-hosted objects presumably never surface in affordances. [Part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger) establishes that the whole is never catalog-visible and is reached by **traversal from a part**. Description reaches it the same way. So neither the reference path nor the description path consults H1 clause 4's host --- **it is bookkeeping nothing reads.**

**The alternative.** The whole becomes a **non-positional record**: a `Meta` row carrying the member set and the part-connection graph, *not* a node in any position graph. Parts remain graph nodes; the whole is structure **over** them. It then has no host, and H1 clauses 4 and 5 do not arise.

**This is already a listed option, not a new invention.** AB-4 asks whether the level-crossing relation is membership with an Object host, a new edge kind, **or something that is not an edge at all** --- and the third option is exactly what makes the whole non-positional. It has been in the row since it was written.

**What it would buy:**

| Gain | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| **P1's gains survive untouched** | All four --- gate, planner, `toHostId`, compose --- require only that no object *is* multi-present. A whole that is neither a graph node nor an adjacency row cannot be multi-present at all |
| **AB-25 deflates** | It exists almost entirely to serve H1 clause 4, and is currently both H1's critical path **and** its only live rollback trigger. It would not vanish (AB-30's walls may still need containment answers) but would stop being what H1 lives or dies on |
| **Trigger 3 evaporates** | No migration means no cascade, no concurrent-migration race, and no thrash --- three queued test cases dissolve rather than needing to be run |
| **No `EphemeraAreaId` needed for this purpose** | P1's largest single unbuilt dependency was an ephemera-side Area identity, required so wholes had somewhere to host |

**What has to be true for it to work --- and both are real questions, not formalities:**

1. **Composition must live in the record, not in position-graph edges.** ~~If wholes can themselves be parts of bigger wholes, this collapses.~~ **Decider corrected 2026-08-06 --- the original made the requirement/representation conflation this plan has just named.** Wholes-nest is **answered yes** (AB-32) and the candidate **survives it**: a record-based whole holds a member set of ids, an id can be another whole's, so nesting is free and hosts recurse to leaf parts. **The real decider, as a requirement: must a relation ever attach to a *whole* rather than to one of its parts?** Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes and this collapses. Every case so far says no; "the place setting is on the table" is the untested shape. **The candidate still hinges on AB-4** --- it is that row's third option carried to its consequence --- but by way of AB-32.
2. **Lifecycle needs an owner.** With no host, what cleans up a whole when its parts are all deleted? Orphan-sweep shaped, and the objects lane has precedent (delete-only repair, the S1 compensating delete), but it is currently the whole's host that would make it discoverable.

**Clause-targeted tests this framing suggests**, replacing the habit of building one elaborate scenario and hoping it stresses something:

- **H1 clause 1a** --- hunt the corpus for a spanning thing that **resists decomposition**. If none exists, 1a holds and is close to free. AB-30's wall is the nearest candidate and is already excluded from the decomposition story, which is suggestive.
- **H1 clause 1b** --- the decisive question is not a scenario but **AB-4**: does composition live in the record or in position-graph edges? Record means [the whole can be non-positional](#candidate-drop-h1-clause-1b-and-the-whole-chain-with-it) and 1b, 4 and 5 all go; edges means wholes must be graph nodes and the chain stands. **Answer AB-4 before running any H1 clause 4 or H1 clause 5 test** --- three queued cases target machinery that may not need to exist.
- **H1 clause 2** --- dual-write drift: does the reverse index stay single-valued across a failed transaction, and does the parent index from [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger) land on the same `Meta` row, making it a multi-writer hotspot?
- **H1 clause 4** --- AB-25's three sub-questions, of which the sharpest is that Area membership is **asset-stack dependent**: if the LCA is perspective-relative then an object's host is not world truth, which is disqualifying rather than expensive.
- **H1 clause 5** --- concurrent migration and thrash (both queued), plus AB-31's fission, where **one event migrates two wholes in opposite directions**.

**Why it was promoted rather than left open.** Two arguments, of unequal strength. The weaker one, stated first and repaired: *the route non-uniformity means any single consolidated representation beats the status quo.* True about the inconsistency, but **symmetric** --- it argues equally for consolidating on multi-host. What actually selects H1 is that the five sites are not symmetric in **cost**: under H1 the membership compile gate, the end-state planner, the relational host map, the singular `toHostId`, and per-room affordance compose all become **correct as written**, no changes at any of them; under framing (a) every one needs widening. Five sites that independently decided single-host is weak evidence that single-host is natural and strong evidence that widening is expensive.

**What it buys the design, beyond the code sites.** It predicts a specific answer to AB-1 rather than dissolving it. Extent stops being its own axis (the rope is parts plus a whole, so AB-8 collapses into AB-4/AB-26), and the functional-state aggregate plausibly shares the same *carrier* --- a generator-and-door system hosts at the Area by the same rule --- while the **relation kind** differs, since a generator is not *part of* a door. That is AB-1's "one substrate, distinct types above it" option, arrived at from a mechanism rather than chosen from a list.

**What it does not buy: net simplification.** H1 retires AB-8 and buys **AB-25**, which is not close to answered and is a bigger commitment than the proposal's wording suggests --- `EphemeraAreaId` does not exist ephemera-side at all, no room -> area index exists anywhere, and containment may not be a tree. It also makes **AB-9 acute** rather than deferrable (a rope and two rope ends in one graph; description must pick a level), and AB-9 is the scope-controlling row. Forcing AB-9 is arguably good. It is not a reduction, and the promotion should not be defended as one.

### What counts as a falsification case (learned the hard way, 2026-08-05)

Three cases in, two of the constructed ones had failed as tests --- one dissolved because it was not a test at all, one passed by acquiring dependencies --- while the corpus was meanwhile producing genuinely hard *scenarios* that H1 sailed through. **The diagnosis matters more than the individual cases: representational adequacy is nearly free, and it is not what H1 claims.**

**The tell is the tag count.** [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer) --- rope, candle, impromptu timer, about as baroque as play gets --- added **zero** `H1-dependent` rows. Everything it established is equally true under framing (a). **A case a competing model passes identically has no selective power over the choice between them.** It was measuring composition-in-general, which was worth doing, and was not a test of this hypothesis.

**Why "can it represent X" keeps passing.** Any sufficiently expressive graph model can express almost any situation --- there is always another node and another edge available. Fictional complexity is not structural difficulty. Meanwhile **H1's load-bearing claims are mechanism claims**: *the whole hosts at the node containing all its parts*, and *migration is automatic and bidirectional*. Nothing run so far has required the LCA to be **computed** rather than asserted to exist, or a migration to be **planned** against `computeStepSequenceFootprint`'s once-only footprint.

**Restated 2026-08-07 against [the five axes](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to), which supply the positive half this section lacked.** *Discriminating* now means **discriminating on a named axis**: two proposals differing is not yet a test, two proposals differing where the difference is **visible to a named audience** is. This subsumes the faithfulness clause below rather than replacing it --- an unfaithful state is one some audience gets a wrong answer from. **And [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) adds a mode no axis covers:** a case can discriminate by showing a proposal **inconsistent with itself**, with no consumer involved at all. Rare, decisive, and not to be forced into an axis.

**So a case earns its place by being at least one of:**

| Kind | Test |
| --- | --- |
| **Discriminating** | H1 and framing (a) give **different** answers. If both handle it the same way, it is a composition case, not an H1 case --- file it as corpus, not as falsification. **Two clauses, and the second was added after the first proved insufficient. (1) Show the competing model's answer step by step; do not assert it.** "Framing (a) handles this trivially" turned out to be wrong on inspection --- (a) needed the same derivation H1 did. **(2) Then check whether the resulting state is *faithful*, not merely reachable.** H1 clause 1 alone still produced a wrong verdict on [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread): the steps were shown, (a) came out cheaper, and the reason it was cheaper is that it **cannot represent the object** --- one node, no notion of where along the thread anything attaches. **An alternative that produces a wrong state cheaply is not winning.** Both failures were the same habit: evaluating the alternative on the dimension that is easy to count rather than the one that decides. |
| **Mechanism** | The LCA rule must actually run, and the resulting migration must be plannable in one footprint pass. |
| **Interaction** | H1 meets shipped machinery built assuming single-level objects --- carry closure's boundary-edge classification, the orphan sweep, Coyote's bulk clear, the eviction ladder. |

**Falsification cases --- run these before dependents accumulate.**

| Case | What would kill H1 |
| --- | --- |
| ~~**Born-spanning things** (river, cable run, wall)~~ **--- retired 2026-08-05, it was not a test** | Two faults. **(i)** [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) already absorbed it: a river's parts are permanently separately-positioned, so the whole stays Area-hosted and migration-down simply never fires. A rule returning a stable answer for a stable thing is not vacuous --- `computeEndStateRoomDiff` returning `changed: false` on a no-op is not evidence against it either. **(ii)** The three examples were **not one category**: a river and a cable run are ordinary Objects with extent that H1 handles unstrained, while a **wall** is structurally different and was grouped in on the surface property "multi-room from creation." That misgrouping is now **AB-30**, which is a design question rather than a falsification case. |
| **"Pull the rope" while the rope is Area-hosted** *(replacement, 2026-08-05)* --- **run same day; passed conditionally** | The concern: P1 says Area-hosted objects presumably never surface in affordances, but a spanning rope is Area-hosted **exactly when a player most needs to refer to it**. **Resolved by [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger)** --- the locally-present part takes the grounding and a parent index walks up to the whole, so the whole never needs to be catalog-visible and P1's affordance clause stays true as written. **Conditional on** AB-6 resolving to stored, AB-15's naming seam having a workable answer, and the parent index being built. |
| ~~**Rope state (iv), re-coiling**~~ **--- run 2026-08-05 via [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends), a sharper instrument** | Tested whether migration-down and reabsorption are the *same event* (AB-17). **They are not.** Re-coiling changes co-location and load-bearing simultaneously and so cannot distinguish them; C5's chain holds co-location fixed while the ends stay load-bearing. **Outcome: a sub-claim of P1 falsified, H1 itself untouched** --- nothing here bears on single-host objects or hosting at the containing node. Rope state (iv) still belongs in the corpus for its own sake; it is no longer load-bearing as a falsification case. |

| **One end of the rope held by a character who walks away** *(added 2026-08-05; run the same day as [C7, Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread))* | **Outcome, after one reversed verdict: evidence *for* decomposition, and not discriminating on hosting.** ~~First reading: H1 is more expensive than framing (a) --- (a) adds a room to a container set, H1 mints a span-part per crossing --- so H1 takes damage.~~ **Withdrawn same day.** That compared *operation cost* without checking whether the resulting state is **correct**. Under (a) the thread is **one node**, so a bystander grabs the entire thread rather than a middle, and three attachments on three hosts have **nothing recording where along the thread each sits** --- a container set is unordered, and the route cannot be recovered from exits because a labyrinth has multiple paths. **(a) cannot represent Ariadne's thread at all**; its cheapness is the cheapness of not modelling the object. The case therefore discriminates between **one node and parts**, not between multi-host and single-host --- and conceding parts lands in AB-8's framing (c), which H1 embodies. **What survives independent of the reversal:** lazy minting is circular (see the description-before-reference constraint), and both framings need the same presence derivation. Original framing below, kept because its AB-25 consequence stands. **Discriminating and mechanism at once.** Under framing (a) the rope is simply present in two hosts and nothing special happens. Under H1 the whole needs an LCA of a room and a **character** --- and `EphemeraMembershipHostId` is `Room \| Character`, so characters *are* hosts. The containment tree therefore has to admit characters as **interior** nodes (room contains character contains held object), which AB-25 does not contemplate, being framed purely as Room/Area. **The consequence to check before going further:** character navigation is the highest-frequency mutation in the system and routes through the same `commitStepSequence` as object take/drop (BD-36), so a walker carrying a rope end re-hosts the whole rope. Navigate must then pre-lock every whole whose part the walker carries --- feasible with the parent index, but it makes the commonest operation in the game structurally more expensive and exercises **rollback trigger 3 on ordinary movement** rather than on contrivances. |
| ~~**Carry closure meets a composition edge**~~ *(queued --- H1 clause 3; closed 2026-08-07, see [Recommended order](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order))* | **Interaction.** Pick up a chest with a rope tied to it, the far end in the next room. [`boundaryEdgeOutcomes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) classifies incident edges `dissolve` / `carry` / `defer` --- three outcomes, corrected 2026-08-07 from a four-outcome citation (`error`) this plan had been carrying that does not exist in the shipped classifier --- **is a composition edge a boundary edge, and which verdict?** This plan has three times found that discipline anticipating cases it was not written for (AB-28, AB-29, [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends)). **Closed by trace rather than by running this scenario**, per Recommended order --- H3 clause 4 plus C11's clause-2 finding compose to answer it without a new worked case. |
| **Concurrent migration** *(queued --- H1 clause 5)* | **Mechanism.** Two characters holding opposite ends, both moving in the same instant. Each transaction recomputes the whole's host from its own view and locks a **different** footprint. Whether the retry sees a coherent state is a `MultiKeyUpdate` question rather than a modelling one --- and it is the kind of thing that is cheap to check now and expensive to discover in production. |
| **Migration thrash and its narration** *(queued --- H1 clause 5)* | **Mechanism, and partly a product question.** A character pacing across a room boundary re-hosts the rope on every crossing. Nothing happened fictionally --- but the presentation kernel narrates on host change. If each crossing narrates, the design is visibly wrong to a player; if it does not, something must suppress it, and that suppression rule does not exist. |

**Rollback triggers, named up front so "dead end" is not decided by mood:**

1. **AB-25 resolves as *containment is not a tree and cannot be made one*** --- H1 then has no node at which to compute a host, and no amount of downstream cleverness recovers it. This is H1's critical path and its most likely cause of death.
2. ~~**A born-spanning case reads wrong** under permanent Area-hosting.~~ **Retired 2026-08-05 --- it was not a test** (see the falsification table above). **Replaced by: the whole becomes unreferrable while Area-hosted.** If "pull the rope" cannot bind to the rope in the state H1 exists to model, the design has moved the difficulty rather than solved it. **Recorded as a weakening, not a win:** retiring a badly-built trigger leaves H1 *less* falsifiable than this section originally claimed, and the replacement is a genuine substitute only if it is actually run. **Run 2026-08-05 and passed conditionally** --- see [part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger). **This trigger is now spent**, so trigger 1 (AB-25) is the only live one remaining, and H1's falsifiability now rests almost entirely on it. Weigh that when Phase 2 reaches AB-25: it is both H1's critical path and its last remaining test.
3. **The migration cascade proves unplannable** --- every part move recomputes its whole's host and may cascade to wholes-of-wholes, while `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once**, up front, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered. If plan-time upward walking cannot pre-lock the affected hosts, H1 is unimplementable on the shipped kernel regardless of how well it models the fiction. **This trigger got *more* likely on 2026-08-05, not less.** [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) shows parts persist through migration rather than dissolving, so dragging a chained chest out of a cell and back cycles the whole up to the Area and down again with the part structure intact --- **a high-frequency operation on a structure that is not changing.** Had reabsorption coincided with migration-down, cascades would at least have been rare events at decomposition boundaries. They are not; they are ordinary movement. Weight trigger 3 accordingly when Phase 2 reaches AB-25. **Candidate escape recorded 2026-08-07, deliberately not claimed:** [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat) makes the cascade deferrable, so the footprint need not cover it --- same problem, same shape, same fix. **Not claimed because a briefly-wrong *host* changes what is in a room**, which is a much harder thing to be eventually consistent about than a briefly-wrong door. Argue it on its own; do not let it be inherited.

### Part-mediated reference: how H1 answers its own replacement trigger

**Proposed and assessed 2026-08-05.** Written here rather than inside a row because it touches AB-6, AB-11, AB-13 and AB-15 at once --- same reason [P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) is written up separately.

**Defect found 2026-08-06, and it is in this scheme's *framing* rather than its mechanics.** As introduced, part-mediated reference was the answer **for spanning wholes**, while local `graph.objectIds` lookup stayed the unexamined norm for everything else --- **two reference paths, never reconciled.** That is [position 3](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs), the one an epicycle argument correctly kills: a second path for a subclass rather than an optimisation of one rule. **The fix is not to discard the scheme but to promote it**: make traversal the *clean* rule for all reference, with zero hops in the local case (an undecomposed flashlight's node *is* the whole), and let today's lookup be a fast path with a stated validity condition. Everything below then describes the general rule rather than a special case.

**The scheme.** In any room where "pull the rope" is a sensible thing to do, **a part of the rope is present** --- an end, a span. That part takes the identification-grounding, because it is what is locally there. It carries a **parent index** naming the whole it belongs to. Reference walks *up* that index to the rope object, which then accumulates the state of its parts across every room in order to evaluate what pulling does.

**Why this is the better answer, not merely an answer.** The alternative was to make Area-hosted wholes surface in room affordances, which would require compose to reach up the containment tree and would drag non-present things into every room's catalog. Under part-mediated reference, **P1's "Area-hosted objects never surface in affordances" stays true as written.** The whole is never in a catalog; reference reaches it by traversal rather than by presence.

**What it costs, checked against shipped machinery:**

| Step | Status |
| --- | --- |
| Part is catalog-visible in its room | **Works today, unchanged** --- the part is an Object node, so `roomObjectCatalogForCharacter`'s `positionGraph.objectIds` scan already finds it |
| Grounding binds "rope" to the part | **The one real gap.** See the naming seam below |
| Part -> whole traversal | **New**, a `Meta::Object` parent field. Cheap, but a new dual write with the usual atomicity and drift story |
| Whole -> all parts, all hosts | **New**, and it forces AB-6 (below) |
| "Pull" applied across hosts | **Shape exists.** `MultiKeyUpdate` already folds N effects into one `transactWrite` |

**It answers AB-11 with the cheap option.** "Pull" is not a new step kind --- the whole **expands** into per-part steps, which is precisely Synthesize's Expansion sub-role, already growing one ungrounded change into an ordered sequence for carry closure and `sameHost` repair. **The kernel's step vocabulary does not grow.** That is the outcome AB-11 exists to determine, reached by a scheme that was not designed to reach it.

**Three consequences it forces, recorded so they are owned rather than rediscovered:**

1. **AB-6 resolves toward *stored*.** *(Contested 2026-08-07: [AB-1](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register)'s state facet pulls the same row toward **derived**, and [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat) is the candidate that dissolves the conflict --- settling is neither stored nor derived, but persisted-in-progress. Recorded here so the stored leaning is not read as unopposed.)* Evaluating the pull needs every part and its host known **before** the walk begins, because `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once, up front. A *derived* member set cannot supply that without already knowing where to look.
2. **The parent index is AB-25's missing upward direction, one level down.** AB-25 records that containment is authored downward with no room -> area index; this is the same gap at object scale. **Distinct from** the reverse *edge*-reference index the plan has flagged three times --- different index, same missing direction.
3. **The naming seam, and it is the step that does not fall out of existing machinery.** The catalog matches on `normalizedShortName`. The player says "pull the **rope**"; what is present is a part. If the part's short name is "rope end," the whole's name is not in the catalog and grounding has nothing to bind to. Candidates: parts alias the whole's short name, a two-phase match consults the parent index, or the catalog widens to include reachable wholes. **This is AB-15 arriving concretely** --- unqualified reference should bind to the authored level, and under H1 the authored level is exactly the thing not locally present.

**And one smaller consequence: make the parent field plural from the start.** Multiple parents (AB-13) means a part belonging to both a rope and a knotted pair, where "pull the rope" is ambiguous. The ambiguity can be deferred; the field shape should not be, since widening it later is the more expensive order.

**Trigger 2 therefore passes --- conditionally, and the condition is the point.** The trigger asked whether the whole stays referrable while Area-hosted. It does. But it passes *because* AB-6 resolves to stored, AB-15's binding question has a workable answer, and the parent index gets built. **H1 survives by acquiring three dependencies, not by needing nothing** --- a normal outcome for a hypothesis doing real work, recorded as conditional so a later reader does not read "passed" as "free."

**The failure mode this section is designed against is sunk cost, not error.** Conditional adoption goes de facto permanent once the dependent set is large enough that rollback means redoing fifteen answers --- at which point H1 survives on accumulated cost rather than merit, and this section documents a decision nobody is willing to act on. **The mitigation is ordering, not discipline:** run the two falsification cases *next*, while the rollback set is still near-empty. The tagging is insurance for what accumulates afterwards, which is when insurance is worth buying.

**Rollback mechanics.** IDs are stable and never reused, so withdrawing H1 does **not** renumber anything: every `H1-dependent` row returns to **Open** with a note naming H1 as what invalidated it, exactly as [Phase 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order) already does for rows a later answer invalidates. The branch point is this section.

#### Rollback mechanics, executed 2026-08-07

H1 withdrawn (above). The six `H1-dependent` rows --- **AB-6, AB-8, AB-9, AB-11, AB-15, AB-17** --- are reverted to Open in the [Open decisions](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) table, each with a note naming H1 as what invalidated the derivation and pointing to H3 where a row's answer plausibly carries forward under the new hypothesis rather than starting from nothing.

### H3: ports as the boundary mechanism, decomposition as a modeller's choice

**Adopted 2026-08-07, in place of H1.** Built from pieces the corpus had already produced independently, none invented for this hypothesis. **Audited against the full corpus 2026-08-07**, the same day it was adopted, following H1's own discipline of testing per clause rather than as one undifferentiated bet. H3 clauses 4 through 9 below are new relative to the three-piece sketch H3 started from --- two are positive findings the first audit pass surfaced (4, 5), two are gaps that pass found and folded in rather than leaving implicit (6, 8), and one (9) was found the same day by [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork), a case built specifically to test H3's clause table for what a corpus of mostly-static cases had not yet exercised: a mutation performed *after* an improvised decomposition, and its reverse. One requirement the audit found (AB-4, held-and-present) is **not yet folded in** --- see below.

| # | Clause | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **Ports are the boundary mechanism.** A whole owns its own interior graph with a root node; crossing into or out of it happens only through numbered, single-use ports bound at the host boundary | **Locked.** [Provisional P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings)'s two shape claims were already [locked](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) independently of H1's fate; H3 promotes what was already load-bearing rather than proposing it fresh. [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) showed H1's old clauses 4/5 (whole-migration) dissolve entirely under this scheme --- there is no whole to migrate, only ports whose egress changes |
| **2** | **Whole and part are roles relative to a level, not kinds.** The same object is a part of one whole and a whole of its own parts simultaneously, at every level of a chain | **Established.** [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s four-level chain --- `StringSpan` is part of `String` and whole of its sub-spans at once --- is what falsified H1's clause 1a; this is the positive content of that finding. A foreclosed misreading of the locked frame's existing vocabulary ([AGENT.concepts.md](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports)), not a change to it, per C11's own closing note |
| **3** | **Decomposition is a modeller's choice, not a requirement with a base case.** An undecomposed span and a decomposed one must both be legal and must give the same answers; nothing in the fiction may depend on which was chosen | **Established.** C11 Finding 2 demoted H1's old clause 3 (spanning things *are* parts) from requirement to option. Lets a modeller give a spanning object a different, finer mutation surface in one location than another (a rope tied to a tree in room B, plain elsewhere) without the choice being load-bearing for correctness --- a granularity knob, exercisable on demand per [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends)'s and [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s third decomposition trigger, not enforced by the model |
| **4** | **Grounding is invariant under decomposition depth.** A room referencing a whole's port names the whole, not whichever interior part decomposition happens to have produced | **Settled by construction, folded in from the audit.** Follows from H3 clause 1 plus [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in)'s naming-seam finding under AB-15 --- "the room's node **is** `OBJECT#ROPE`." Confirmed against [C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it), the case that broke under H1's non-positional-whole candidate: "pick up the flashlight" grounds identically whether or not the flashlight has been decomposed into battery/casing/bulb, because the room only ever sees the port |
| **5** | **Ports bind any relation kind crossing a boundary, not only composition.** Directed relations (generator *powers* door) and participation-without-parthood (door *attached to* the power system, not part of it) cross a port the same way a `part` edge does | **Requirement stated 2026-08-07 from C12; first ordinary-case exercise found the same day.** [C14](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c14-the-tray-its-cups-and-how-many-times-it-gets-picked-up) Finding 5: `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer`'s `defer` verdict (checked against shipped code --- three outcomes, `dissolve`/`carry`/`defer`, not the four this plan had been citing) has never had a resolution path, because relational edges are same-host-only and there was no representable third option. A minted whole's port supplies one --- a string tied to a carried-away tray survives as a live cross-host binding instead of being forced to dissolve or block. Still **open as a mechanism check** for C12's directed/causal case specifically; C14 exercises the ordinary-relation case, not that one |
| **6** | **Membership is a DAG: a part may belong to more than one whole at once, each dissolving independently.** | **Open, folded in from the audit as a hard requirement.** [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer) forces this, not merely permits it: the shared span is one part, the rope and the impromptu timer are two unrelated wholes, and the timer must dissolve (candle burns out) while the rope persists. No mechanism yet for how a part's port set records which of several wholes currently claims it, or how one whole's dissolution leaves a sibling whole's claim untouched. **Cross-note, 2026-08-07:** if consequence propagation for this clause runs through [P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat) (per AB-40(b)), this is precisely the **diamond** P4's dataflow reading already flags as a known glitch source --- a shared node reachable by two paths can be visited short-path-first and briefly report a value that was never true. Not a new risk H3 clause 6 introduces; a known P4 cost this clause is now a concrete instance of |
| **7** | **Reabsorption is legal only when nothing exterior still depends on the part being dissolved.** No relational edge external to the part may still reference it, and for hosting-reabsorption specifically, all of the part's ports must already share a single egress host | **Settled, gathered from shipped machinery.** The first condition is [`removeObject`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/AGENT.md#known-limitation-deferred)'s existing assert-and-throw (`RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError`), not newly designed. The second is [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in)'s criterion --- previously AB-17's clause, gathered here rather than left scattered across C5/C9/AB-17. **Clarified 2026-08-07, checking the rope's fourth state against this clause directly: legal does not mean automatic.** H3 clause 3 already establishes decomposition as a modeller's choice, not a requirement; the symmetric reading is that reabsorption is the same --- once a fully-coiled rope satisfies both conditions, dissolving its parts back to one node is *permitted*, not forced. A rope could sit indefinitely as three co-located, connected parts without ever formally reabsorbing, exactly as a place setting could stay undecomposed indefinitely. **This corrects the "mandatory" framing** the rope's fourth state was originally scoped under (an H1-era assumption); nothing in H3 requires it |
| **8** | **The port record reserves a field for apprehension scale, undecided.** Whether a whole surfaces at the level of a given part or only by traversal from it is a real per-(object, port) fact | **Open, deliberately deferred, folded in from the audit.** [C10](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c10-the-moonbase-computer-system) and [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine) both raised this and neither resolved it (perspective- and position-relativity, respectively). H3 does not decide the policy here, only that the port record should have a place to carry the answer later --- same shape as [PQ-2](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s "a local endpoint is optional on a port's exterior end" |
| **9** | **A part crossing its whole's interior-graph boundary as a live, independently-manipulable object --- in either direction --- is unaddressed.** Neither a step kind for detaching a named part and relocating it to an unrelated host, nor one for the reverse (adopting a foreign object as a new part of an existing whole), nor a description rule for a whole that has shed exactly one member, is named anywhere in H3 clauses 1--8 | **Open, both directions checked and found missing, not merely under-specified.** [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork) --- take the fork, then put it back --- traced both operations against H3 clauses 1--8 and found neither has a path: taking has no step kind (**AB-40**, opened there) and no rule for what the remainder says about itself; putting back additionally lacks even an ordinary destination, since "inside another object's interior graph" is not a host kind any existing or proposed step targets. **This is the sharpest concrete direction the corpus has produced since H3 was adopted** --- everything else folded in so far was either already solved (4, 5 partially) or a policy deferred on purpose (8); this is a mechanism nobody has proposed at all |

**Not yet folded in: AB-4, held-and-present non-exclusivity.** [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s thread can be held by a character and spatially present in a room at the same moment --- flagged there as "the gap underneath the whole case." Ports address multi-*room* presence; they say nothing about simultaneous non-spatial (held) and spatial (present) relations on the same object, and no clause above closes it. Recorded here rather than silently assumed away, since this plan has twice already found a caveat that sat unacted-on treated as settled by later work (AB-16's tag, H1's "hold even 1a loosely"). Not a clause because there is no candidate mechanism yet, only the requirement.

**Folded in 2026-08-09, and it needed no new mechanism.** [The register's third general finding](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register) --- relation kind and endpoint granularity are independent axes --- means containment kinds take **qualified endpoints** like any other kind. So Theseus's graph holds `Theseus -Held-> thread:1` and the room's holds `thread:2 -In-> room`, with the thread's own graph binding port 1 to its skein and port 2 to a span. Both true, of **different parts**, in different graphs. **The requirement was never for a mechanism that permits a contradiction** --- it was for one that stops manufacturing a contradiction out of a thread collapsed to a single referent, which is [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s own *where along* defect appearing as false exclusivity. **Note what actually did the work:** removing the ghost link. While membership was containment-in-a-collection, *held* and *present* were two claims on one exclusive slot; once both are ordinary edges of declared kinds, there is no slot to contend for. **`Held` and `Worn` are new register kinds** opened by this, and `Held`-versus-`In` exclusivity is an open per-kind-pair question there.

**What H3 is not yet, beyond the above.** A restatement of H1's clause-by-clause structure is now largely done (this table), but the nine open PQ-questions under P3 (PQ-4, PQ-6 through PQ-9, PQ-12, PQ-13, PQ-1's record split) are unresolved and now sit *under* a working hypothesis rather than a provisional --- PQ-13 in particular ([C11 Finding 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#finding-3-pq-13-is-the-fractals-base-case-not-a-calibration-question)) was already flagged as needing a decision before anything downstream, and H3's promotion makes that more urgent, not less.

**Tagging convention carries forward unchanged.** Mark a row `H3-dependent` under the same test as H1's: derived from, not merely compatible with. A row that was `H1-dependent` and whose answer survives under H3 for the same reason gets re-tagged `H3-dependent` with a note; one that doesn't reverts to plain Open.

### Candidate H2, not adopted: AB-12's privileged-level reframe

**H1 died 2026-08-07 without H2 being the replacement --- see [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice).** H2 remains what it always was, AB-12's separate privileged-level reframe, still not promoted, still available if H3 dies in turn.

Named here so the next load-bearing candidate is explored through this mechanism rather than re-inventing one ad hoc. **AB-12** --- everything is an Object at *some* level --- has the same shape as H1: it would dissolve rows rather than answer them, and it is currently recorded as "do not treat as settled" with no way to explore it conditionally. **Deliberately not promoted yet:** two hypotheses in flight at once would make the dependent sets overlap, and a row tagged both `H1-dependent` and `H2-dependent` cannot be rolled back as a unit --- which is the whole mechanism. Promote H2 only after H1 resolves, or in place of it if H1 dies.

## Proposal P2: separate the storage shard from the containment relation

**The whole-has-its-own-graph half was locked 2026-08-06 --- see [Locked frame: parts and ports](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06).** What is locked is the *shape*: a whole is a graph with a root node, and part relations are edges inside it. The shard-versus-relation reasoning, the `positionCache` clauses, and AB-33's expensive reading (a global edge space) are **not** locked and are still argued below as written.

**Recorded 2026-08-06 as a proposal, not adopted, and deliberately *not* promoted to a working hypothesis** --- H1 is still in flight, and two hypotheses at once break the rollback mechanism for the reason [candidate H2](#candidate-h2-not-adopted-ab-12s-privileged-level-reframe) records. P2 is written up here rather than inside a row because it touches AB-2, AB-3, AB-4, AB-9, AB-16, AB-30 and AB-32, and because it reframes what [the three reference positions](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) were arguing about.

### The conceptual error it starts from: hosting creates a phantom edge

Membership is expressed by **containment in a collection**, not by an edge. [`ephemeraMeta.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraMeta.ts):

```typescript
export type EphemeraPositionGraphData = {
    hostId: EphemeraMembershipHostId;
    nodes: EphemeraPositionGraphNode[];
    edges?: EphemeraPositionRelationalEdgeData[];
}
```

`hostId` is a **scalar sibling** of `nodes` --- the host is not in its own node list --- and `EphemeraPositionRelationalEdgeData`'s `from`/`to` are both `EphemeraObjectId`. So every node carries an implicit *contents-of* link to its host that is **not merely unlabeled but untypeable**: the edge could not be written into `edges` even without a kind, because one endpoint has no node to be.

**Three consequences, and the first is the strongest evidence.**

1. **The phantom is already materialized in storage, with the label slot designed out.** [`ephemeraPositionAdjacency.ts`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/ephemeraPositionAdjacency.ts)'s `EphemeraPositionAdjacencyRow` is `{ EphemeraId: containedId, DataCategory: 'POSITION#' + hostId }` --- an edge record with a from, a to, and **no third column**. The link was recognized as a link and then flattened into a two-column index. That is a stronger claim than absence: it shows the shape was chosen, not overlooked.
2. **There is no containment relation kind anywhere.** `HostRelationalEdgeKind = 'On' | 'Under' | 'Against' | 'Custom'` --- support and adjacency, no `In`, no `Contains`, no `PartOf`. The system cannot currently say "in" about *anything*. So `part-of` would not be displacing a competitor; it would be the first relation of its family, and **AB-4's whole question is being asked in a vocabulary that has no word for the answer.**
3. **It is the third angle on a defect already recorded.** [`ludicGraph/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/AGENT.md) notes "No root concept --- and a rooted graph already exists next door," with `CarryClosureFragment = {rootId, members, edges}` as the shape the class has no root to lend. **A host node is that root**, which makes this **AB-3**'s pre-registered trigger, arriving from the hosting side rather than the closure side.

### Objects will be hosts --- and that makes the phantom urgent rather than hypothetical

`EphemeraMembershipHostId = EphemeraRoomId | EphemeraCharacterId`, whose own doc comment reads *"v1 eligible membership hosts (room + character inventory; **OBJECT# / AREA# later**)."* **Read that as a roadmap, not a limit.** This game is in Zork's lineage; "put the lantern in the box" is not an optional feature, so object-hosting is coming.

**And object-hosting built on the phantom model walks straight into [position 3](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs).** If `OBJECT#` joins the union as currently shaped, `BOX` gets its own `positionGraph` and containment is expressed by **graph nesting** --- phantoms all the way down, one per level, none labeled, none traversable as an edge, and "what is in the room" becomes a recursive read across N shards. That is single-hosting with lookup as the clean rule: the position the same-mechanism argument kills. **So the sequencing matters more than the limitation did** --- every nesting level added first is another site to unwind later.

### What is actually wrong: one construct doing two jobs

The host union is **not** arbitrary, and the reason is worth stating before proposing to change it. Room and Character are both **things that move while retaining their contents** --- a character walks between rooms with an inventory. That is a real sharding justification, and it is exactly why a pick-up-able box belongs in the union too. **The roadmap is coherent *as storage*.**

The problem is that the same construct also carries the containment *relation*:

| Job | What it needs | Fractal-compatible? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Storage shard** | A boundary that travels with a mobile container | Yes --- physical, and traversal should not see it |
| **Containment relation** | A labeled, traversable, nestable link | **No** --- a collection cannot carry a kind |

**Separate them and both get better.** Shard by mobility, keeping `MultiKeyUpdate`'s `transactWrite` unit intact; express containment as labeled edges within whatever shard holds them.

### The proposal, in three clauses

1. **`positionGraph` is strictly constrained not to extend into nodes that are themselves hosts.** The graph stops at each host boundary. **Already the documented fractal** --- [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#fractal-ludic-graphs-container-scale-and-edges) has `Area.positionGraph -> Room.positionGraph -> Container graph`, with "Object `OBJECT#` / Area hosts deferred until needed." This clause promotes a description to a constraint.
2. **`positionCache` is a denormalized read structure** holding, *as useful to optimization*, more expanded graphs that show the deeper structure across multiple host nodes. **Not documented anywhere today** --- this is new design, not recall.
3. **Edges in a `positionGraph` may address a specific *part* of a node, without that part being listed as a node in the host holding the edge.** "Rope" is a node in the room's graph; `rope: rope-end` is an endpoint of a `tied to` edge in that same graph.

### Why P2 clause 3 is the prize: it decouples decomposition from membership

This is the mechanism the design has been missing, and it clears four open items at once:

| Open item | Under part-addressed endpoints |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-9 made acute** --- "you see a rope, a rope end, and a rope end" | **Dissolves.** One node; the ends are endpoint qualifiers, not members. Nothing to select between at the membership layer |
| **[C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)** --- room B holds both a thread node and a segment, "pull the thread" ambiguously bound | **Dissolves.** One node plus part-qualified edges. The cost [position 1](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) was charged disappears |
| **[C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends)** --- three nodes in the cell's graph, permanently | **Refined.** One chain node and two edges addressing `chain: end-a` / `chain: end-b`. The "permanent multi-level steady state" was an artifact of parts needing to be nodes |
