| **[C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it)** --- improvising interior detail unhosts the whole | **Dissolves structurally.** Part-qualified structure is added; the flashlight node never leaves the room |

**Four cases, one mechanism, and none of them was what it was designed for.** P1 never did that. Weigh it accordingly --- but see the caution below, because one of the four is doing less work than it appears.

### Where P2 clause 3 risks recreating the very defect it fixes

If `rope: rope-end` appears in an edge and parts are nodes nowhere, then **part existence is asserted by reference** --- an endpoint naming something with no declaration. That is structurally the same defect as an unlabeled host link, one scale down.

**The reading that avoids it, and it is the one this plan adopts for the write-up:** `rope: rope-end` is an **address into the rope's own graph**. The rope hosts its parts exactly as the box hosts its contents --- same mechanism, and consistent with objects becoming hosts. The room's edge then reaches *across* a host boundary **by address rather than by node**, P2 clause 1 still holds because the room's graph contains no part-node, and part identity has a home. **So "objects will be hosts" turns out to be load-bearing for the part story as well as for `put lantern in box`** --- a second, independent argument for it.

**The unresolved half, and it is the next topic rather than a gap to paper over.** P2 clause 3 as stated cleanly handles the **within-host** case: the rope is a node in the room, and the tie attaches to one of its ends. The **cross-host** case is not settled --- if Ariadne's thread hosts once, an edge in room B addressing one of its segments has an endpoint outside room B's graph, which is exactly what P2 clause 1 forbids traffic in. **Making addressing work in all directions is the open problem**, and the candidate on the table is **ports**: a host object exposes named ports living on its *boundary*, whose job is to connect outside references to inside addresses. Recorded as **AB-34**; not yet assessed.

### Considered and rejected: flatten instead of nest

Raised and withdrawn the same day, kept because the reason is the load-bearing one. **The alternative:** let the room's graph hold `box`, `lantern`, and the `In` edge between them --- nesting expressed as edges *within* one graph rather than as graphs within graphs. It gets uniform traversal with no cross-shard hop.

**It is worse than what exists, and mobility is why.** Under host-nesting, picking up the box moves one shard and its contents come along free. Under flattening, every pickup **migrates N nodes and edges** out of the room's graph into the character's. It optimizes the read path by breaking the write path that host-binding exists to make cheap. **The middle path keeps shard-mobility *and* gets uniform traversal, by paying for reads in the cache rather than in the structure.**

### The invariant that keeps P2 clause 2 honest, with local precedent

[`packages/mtw-wml/documentation/schema/README.md`](../../../../../packages/mtw-wml/documentation/schema/README.md) records this codebase backing **out** of denormalizing child data upward --- "an enormous source of unnecessary complexity. Avoid the temptation" --- replaced by **selectors** computing condensed views from the source-of-truth tree. Different subsystem, and the analogy is limited: that was denormalization into *authored source*, not a runtime cache. But the discipline it landed on is exactly what P2 clause 2 needs.

**Pin it as a rule, not an aspiration: the write path never touches `positionCache`; it is invalidate-and-rederive only.** Otherwise the cache becomes a second truth and the phantom returns as a coherence problem --- the same failure one layer up. Tracked as **AB-35**.

### How it relates to H1, and to the clean/dirty test

**P2 does not decide H1, and does not compete with it.** P2 clause 3 is orthogonal to single-hosting: it changes what parts *are* (addresses, not necessarily nodes), which changes what H1 clauses 1b, 4 and 5 **cost** without settling them. H1 clause 1a and clause 3-of-H1 (spanning things are parts) survive unchanged.

**What P2 does supply is the structural realisation of [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs)** --- traversal as the clean rule, lookup as the optimisation. `positionGraph` is the clean fractal; `positionCache` is the deliberate dirt. **That makes it the first thing in this design to *pass* [the clean/dirty test](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) rather than be excused by it:** a cache returning what the traversal would have returned is provably "the clean answer, computed faster," not a model change wearing dirt's clothing.

### What it does not resolve

- **Cross-host addressing** --- AB-34, the ports question, and the immediate next topic.
- **Cache invalidation and derivation scope** --- AB-35.
- **Whether the host link becomes an actual edge, or the host merely becomes a node** --- AB-33. The proposal argues the relation should be labeled and traversable; it does not settle the representation, and that is deliberately the same discipline AB-4 / AB-32 just imposed on composition.
- **Lifecycle of a whole with no host** is untouched --- still open on [the non-positional-whole candidate](#candidate-drop-h1-clause-1b-and-the-whole-chain-with-it).

---

## Provisional P3: ports as host-boundary bindings

**Its two shape claims were locked 2026-08-06 --- see [Locked frame: parts and ports](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06).** The heading and preamble below are left as written, per this plan's supersession rule; read "provisional" as applying to the **calibration** (PQ-4, PQ-8, PQ-9, PQ-12, PQ-13, and PQ-1's record split), not to the existence of an interior-owned boundary binding. The exclusion list in that section is authoritative about which is which.

**Recorded 2026-08-06 as a provisional, deliberately ahead of its open questions.** This is **AB-34**'s candidate answer, worked out far enough to be argued against. **Promoted 2026-08-07 to the core mechanism of [H3](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)**, in place of H1. The nine questions below are recorded **as questions**: several have an obvious-looking answer and a leaning is noted where one exists, but **no leaning here is a decision**, and they are to be worked rather than assumed --- H3's promotion raises their stakes rather than settling them.

### The requirement, stated independently of any hosting model

**This is what makes ports worth designing before H1 resolves.** Whether wholes are multi-host, single-host, or hosted at an Area, there is a **common requirement to address relations and links across a containment boundary, in both directions:**

- A whole at a **non-local** level --- relations to the rope *as a whole* must jump **out** of the room's graph. Part relationships especially: either they cross a level, or composition needs an entirely separate graph system.
- A whole at a **local** level --- addressing its parts means reaching **in**, to things contained not in the room but inside the rope's own parts list.

Both directions are the same problem, and no hosting decision removes either.

### The scheme

Numbered **presence ports** act as ingress/egress borders, turning a cross-`positionGraph` connection into something traversal can parse. Worked example, taking multi-host presence for the moment (see [the consequence for H1](#consequence-ports-re-price-the-three-positions-table) --- that assumption is not neutral):

- A rope is present in `ROOM#A` and `ROOM#B`.
- `ROOM#A`'s graph holds a **`tied to`** edge between a rock and **`OBJECT#ROPE:1`** --- port 1.
- `OBJECT#ROPE`'s own `positionGraph` holds: a **root node** for `OBJECT#ROPE` itself; a **`part`** edge from the root to `OBJECT#RopeEnd1`; and a **`tied to`** edge from **port 1** to `OBJECT#RopeEnd1`.
- Traversal composes those into an edge from an object in `ROOM#A` to a part inside `OBJECT#ROPE`.
- **Interior structure:** `part` links from the root to every part, plus **sibling links** between parts expressing how they connect.
- **Candidate `present` edge kind:** "this part is part of the object's presence in this room."

**Two properties fall out, and they are the design's stated goal:** looking at the rope alone tells you **(a)** how its parts are connected and **(b)** where and how they connect externally.

#### Ports are single-use, and that is not a detail (clarified 2026-08-06)

**Re-opened 2026-08-10 as PQ-14, from [C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c22-the-ships-wheel-and-the-lighthouse)'s multiplicative port fan-out --- read this section as the standing argument *for* single-use, no longer as a settled property.** The pressure is scalability under ordinary fiction, not a representability failure, and **the two payoffs argued below may survive a narrower relaxation** than the prohibition states: both rest on a port having one *egress*, not one *interior edge*. See PQ-14 for what must be worked and in what order. **And read the two payoffs below as *consequences of* the clarification, not *grounds for* it** --- this section was written to correct a misreading of what ports are, and the reverse-index and plan-time-footprint benefits were noticed afterward. They are real; they were never weighed against a fan-out alternative, because no such alternative was ever on the table to weigh.

**A crossing port records *one* ingress/egress between two position graphs --- it is not a general-purpose named interface with fan-out.** If `ROOM#A` connects to both `RopeEnd1` and `RopeSpan1`, that is **two crossing ports**, not one crossing port carrying two interior edges. An earlier reading of this section treated ports as reusable boundary interfaces; that reading is wrong and several assessments made under it needed correcting.

**The consequence that changes the most: on the egress side, a port can tell you which position graph it exits to.** So a whole's own graph **enumerates its referrers** --- the port list *is* the reverse index, stored exactly where it is needed. That is why **PQ-6, the blocking item, largely dissolves**, and with it PQ-7's surviving residue.

**Why that lands harder than convenience:** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs **once, up front**, and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered. A port-carrying graph can compute its own footprint at plan time --- open the rope, read its ports, and every host the operation touches is known. Fission therefore **cannot** silently release a port, because the port names `ROOM#A` and obliges the operation to include it. *A host may not release an externally-referenced port* becomes locally enforceable, which is exactly what it lacked.

**And it narrows the encapsulation argument, which this plan had overstated.** Ports make **interior repartitioning** non-breaking --- coiling rebinds port 1 from `RopeEnd1` to the root, entirely inside the rope. They do **not** make **identity changes** non-breaking: when fission turns the rope into `ROPE-upper` and `ROPE-lower`, an external edge naming `OBJECT#ROPE:1` must be rewritten to name `OBJECT#ROPE-upper:1`, because the object's identity changed --- which is true with or without ports. See the correction on **AB-31**.

#### The port record: split, complementary, with the interior authoritative (settled 2026-08-06)

**This is [PQ-1](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s answer.** It resolves the question the [single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) clarification opened, and it avoids the trade that question appeared to force.

**The shape --- two halves holding *different* facts, each stored where it is read:**

| Half | Lives on | Holds |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Egress list** | the interior graph, as a new top-level element | port number -> outbound host: `[{ port: 1, host: ROOM#A }, { port: 2, host: ROOM#A }, { port: 5, host: ROOM#B }]` |
| **Ingress list** | ~~the exterior graph's **node**~~ **`positionCache`** (corrected 2026-08-06, below) | which of that object's ports are live *here* |

**Why this dodges the drift trade.** PQ-1's fork was "stored once in the whole" versus "split halves," where split meant two records asserting **the same** fact. These assert **complementary** facts, so the expensive read stays local: `ROOM#A` answers *what is here and how is it connected* without opening the bag. Opening it is required only to learn *which part* port 4 lands on, which is a detail-level question and exactly the thing that should cost a hop. **That is [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) working as advertised, with the zero-hop case genuinely zero hops.**

**Two defaults the shape enables:**

1. **Failed addressing coarsens rather than dangles.** An edge to `OBJECT#BAG:4` where 4 is not a legal port --- or where no interior edge leaves port 4 --- resolves **identically to an edge to `OBJECT#BAG`**. *If the chain of addressing fails, the last successfully addressed host is the default target.* "Tied to the bag's strap" degrades to "tied to the bag," which is fictionally acceptable almost always. **Per-hop:** each graph resolves one hop and coarsens locally; a failure does not unwind a whole chain.
2. **A port with no exterior edge is a pure presence indicator**, when the interior carries a `present` link from that port. Better than an optional-endpoint field, because **the absence of an edge *is* the signal** --- no second record shape. [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) in a corridor: the room's THREAD node carries port 7, the thread's graph has `port 7 -> present -> ThreadSpan3`. Present, attached to nothing.

**Authority: the interior egress list wins.** It is the half that must be complete for plan-time footprint computation (`computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once), and the half whose lifecycle invalidates ports.

**Scoped 2026-08-22, and the scoping is recorded here because this sentence is the one that got generalised.** **What it decided was *egress list versus ingress list*** --- two denormalizations of one topology --- **and the ingress list was removed from truth in the reversal immediately below**, so the contest no longer has two sides. **It was never adjudicating the interior against the *exterior edge*, which is not a denormalization at all but primary structure in the room's own graph.** Read as *the interior wins over everything*, it produced a citation chain (premise 12 -> locked-frame clause 2) in which clause 2's *"a binding **the interior owns**"* --- ownership of the binding --- was taken as authority over every field on it. **Of the two reasons given above, only the second survives as an authority argument:** lifecycle invalidation is genuinely interior. **Footprint completeness argues for the egress list being *local*, not for it *winning*** --- a denormalized copy is equally local and equally complete, and authority only decides a disagreement. (As shipped, [`computeStepSequenceFootprint`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/computeStepSequenceFootprint.ts) reads no port record at all; it takes hosts from step fields and an injected resolver.) **The scoped form is normative in [`positions/AGENT.contract.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.contract.md#port-records-field-scope-and-the-conflict-rule)** (carried there 2026-08-23 from the ports plan's premise 12, on that plan's close)**:** interior scope is existence, `portId`, lifecycle, `kind` and the interior edge; the port-to-host pairing and the exterior label defer to an exterior reference **when one exists to disagree with**.

~~**The ingress list is therefore a denormalization inside truth, and that is accepted deliberately** --- a materialized view in `positionGraph` rather than in cache, on the `Meta`-placement precedent, with the `positionCache` alternative rejected because the room's local read is too hot to depend on cache warmth. The drift obligation is tracked as PQ-11.~~

**Reversed 2026-08-06, one exchange after being settled --- the rejection reason did not survive inspection.** The room's local read was the justification, and **the room's own edges already carry what a coarse read needs**: `boulder -TiedTo-> ROPE:4` is in the room's graph and names the rope, so "a rope is tied to the boulder" renders with no ingress list at all. The list's unique contribution is knowing which ports are live here *including presence-only ones* --- **a where-along fact, and detail costing a hop was the deal.** The thing put in truth to protect the hot path turns out not to be on it.

**The strength argument, which is the general form.** A denormalization earns a place in truth by what it saves. The ingress list is **weak** in exactly the sense that matters: per record it surfaces information from **one** item, saving one read of an object already known to be present. The membership node is **strong**: it saves scanning every object in the world.

| | Saves | Verdict |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **The node** --- rope is present in Room A | scanning all objects --- **N:1** | strong; **stays in `positionGraph`** |
| **The ingress port list** --- ports 1, 2 live here | one read of an object already known --- **1:1** | weak; **belongs in `positionCache`** |

**So [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in)'s rule --- *present iff at least one port egresses here* --- is a consistency invariant between the egress list and membership, not a derivation any reader performs.** A far smaller obligation than the drift repair it replaces: **PQ-11 is retired.**

**And there is a principled reading, not merely a tidier one: ingress-in-truth was [position 3](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) growing back.** Materialising a lookup answer into truth so the common case need not traverse, while the uncommon case traverses, is *two rules where one would do* --- the shape this plan already caught itself in once, by accident. An analogy rather than an identity, but close enough to record, because it is the second time the same instinct produced the same defect.

**What the reversal costs, and why it is already covered.** A room's graph alone can no longer tell whether `boulder -TiedTo-> ROPE:4` names a live port --- that needs opening the rope. **Which is precisely the coarsening default's job:** it resolves to `ROPE` and renders "tied to the rope." The two decisions now support each other, which they did not before: with the ingress list in truth, a stale entry was a **phantom** the coarsening rule said nothing about. That asymmetry was the ugliest corner of the rejected branch and it goes with it.

**One rule that must not be inferred away.** The coarsening default is a **read-side resolution rule only**. It does **not** license relaxing BD-33/35's write-side assert-and-throw --- `removeObject` still refuses to drop a node an edge references, and a caller still owes an explicit dissolve first. The tempting inference is that robust reads make the write check unnecessary; they do not, because **a silently-coarsening world accumulates imprecision nobody notices**, which is the silent-wrong-answer class this plan has repeatedly chosen against. A smaller instance of the same: coarsening turns "pull this strap" into "pull the whole bag," correctly most of the time and silently always.

**Chaining falls out for free (PQ-5).** An interior graph addresses its own nodes' ports exactly as a room does, so a bag's interior edge from port 4 may target `ROPE:2`. No extra machinery.

#### What a port number is not (added 2026-08-06)

**Written because this section has now generated two misreadings of the same family, each of which propagated into assessments before it was caught.** The section says clearly what a port *is*; it said nothing about what a port number is **not**, and the negative turns out to be the load-bearing half.

A port number is a **boundary slot on the host object**, allocated by that host, meaningful only as one end of one crossing. It is **not**:

- **A name for the interior node behind it.** `OBJECT#ROPE:1` does not identify a part. The part is an ordinary nominal id --- `OBJECT#RopeEnd1` --- and the port merely has an edge to it. The two worked examples in this section are already symmetric on this point: `port 1 -[TiedTo]-> OBJECT#RopeEnd1` and `port 1 -[SolderedTo]-> OBJECT#BatteryCase`.
- **A reusable public interface.** One crossing port records **one** crossing; two connections to the same host are two crossing ports. See [Ports are single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).
- **A fan-out point.** A crossing port has exactly one interior edge and one exterior referrer.
- **Evidence about the interior at all.** Numbering is a property of the *boundary*; whether the interior identifies its parts positionally or nominally is a separate question (**PQ-9**), which the port answers neither way.

**The error this last bullet prevents actually happened.** The retired **AB-16** briefly held that extent parts are identified by *where* and containment parts by *what*, citing `ROPE:1` --- reading the boundary slot as the part's name. It was the last surviving argument for that row, and it was an artifact of this section.

#### A port is a scale boundary, not a relay (2026-08-06)

**The two edges joined by a port need not carry the same kind, and usually will not.** This reverses the leaning recorded on **PQ-1 (original)** and changes what a port is understood to be.

**The rope made matching kinds look necessary, and that was an artifact.** `boulder -TiedTo-> ROPE:1` and `port 1 -TiedTo-> RopeEnd1` agree because a rope end is *the same kind of thing* as a rope --- homogeneous extent, where a part is a smaller instance of the whole. Under homogeneity a port looks like an identity function, quietly relaying one relation inward.

**A heterogeneous case breaks it immediately.** A power cord run into a flashlight: the exterior edge is `PowerCord -[ThreadsInto]-> OBJECT#FlashLight:1`, and the interior edge is `port 1 -[SolderedTo]-> OBJECT#BatteryCase`. Both are true, neither is a copy, and they describe the connection at the level each graph operates on. **They are two relations at different scales, joined by the port** --- so a port is a **scale boundary at which the description changes**, not a relay.

**Consequence for storage: each side stores its own kind, and nothing is duplicated.** The earlier leaning ("the kind lives inside only") rested on avoiding a two-writer problem across two shards. **That premise is gone** --- if the kinds legitimately differ there is no shared fact to disagree about, and the consistency worry evaporates for the same reason the ingress list's did: complementary, not redundant. **No new mechanism is needed either:** the exterior edge already carries `HostRelationalEdgeKind`, and the interior edge is an ordinary edge in the flashlight's graph.

**Consequence for coarsening, and it is better than tolerable --- it is lossless at the level being read.** Under matching kinds, coarsening felt like degradation: *tied to the rope's end* -> *tied to the rope*. Under heterogeneous kinds there is no degradation at all: `PowerCord -ThreadsInto-> FLASHLIGHT:1` coarsens to `PowerCord -ThreadsInto-> FLASHLIGHT`, which reads **"a power cord threads into the flashlight"** --- unchanged and still correct. **Because the exterior kind was never about the interior part, coarsening loses only the interior fact, which was the detail by construction.**

**Qualified 2026-08-07 --- "lossless at the level being read" was stated without naming the reader, and it is only true for one of them.** Under [the five axes](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to), coarsening is an **apprehension** mechanism: a player told "a power cord threads into the flashlight" has lost nothing. An **LLM** asked whether the strap can be untied, or what the cord is soldered to, has lost precisely the fact it needs --- and reasoning is lossless by design where apprehension is lossy by design. **So coarsening on the reasoning path is a defect, not a graceful degradation.** The likely fix is small and is *not* being adopted here: coarsening stays a read-side rule for **description**, and the reasoning path opens the bag, paying a hop on a path that already expects to pay for detail. Recorded as a defect rather than resolved, since coarsening is calibration on the [not-locked list](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-is-not-locked-and-must-not-ride-in-on-this) and this is a frame rather than a decision.

**Sharpened later the same day, and the diagnosis moves: "coarsening" names *two* operations, and they have opposite requirements about silence.**

| Operation | Situation | Is there a finer fact? | Must it announce? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Failure recovery** --- the case this section was written for | `OBJECT#BAG:4` where 4 is **not a legal port** | **No.** The address was bad | **No.** There is nothing to announce |
| **Atomic fact** --- split out 2026-08-26, and it was never failure recovery | Port 4 is legal and **no interior edge leaves it**. A **presence port** is this case by construction: it is a terminal, so it never has the partner edge a crossing has | **No** --- and that is a correct answer about the world, not a broken address | **No.** Announcing would assert a finer fact that does not exist |
| **Deliberate summarization** --- the case the reasoning axis exposes | Port 4 is valid and the reader chose not to traverse | **Yes** | **Yes** --- otherwise the omission is silent, which is the reasoning axis's one hard requirement |

**So the repair is not "reasoning always opens the bag."** It is that the summarized form must **say that a finer fact exists**, exactly as [a span reports a snag without reporting where](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07). Cheaper than a mandatory hop and it fixes the actual defect, which was silence rather than compression. **The two operations should get different names before either is implemented** --- one word covering a case that must be quiet and a case that must not is how the wrong one gets built. **Answered 2026-08-25 from conversation, and the discriminator is structural: [PR-15](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register).** *Passing through* a port is not a stored shape but a **derived relation holding exactly when a PARTNER edge exists interior-side**, so **a coarse read must announce iff a partner exists** --- and a **presence port never has one**, making `kind === 'Present'` the atomic case by construction. **The names this section asked for follow from the branch rather than needing to be invented.** **One correction it forces on the table above:** the failure-recovery row reads *"where 4 is not a legal port, **or no interior edge leaves it**"* and those are **two different things** --- **quiet-because-broken** and **quiet-because-nothing-finer.** The first is an error, the second is a correct atomic fact, and only the first is failure recovery. ~~**A third row is owed here.**~~ **Paid 2026-08-26**, when PR-15 graduated: the row is split above, failure recovery keeps only the broken address, and the atomic case gets its own row with `kind === 'Present'` named as its structural discriminator. **Why one word covered both for three weeks is worth keeping:** the two cases agree on every *observable* in this table --- no finer fact, no announcement --- and differ only in **what a reader may conclude from the silence**. A table of behaviours cannot separate them; only a column for the reason can, and the reason is the thing the third row adds.

**No compatibility matrix --- and this is an existing decision applying, not a new one.** The tempting next step is to police coherence across the boundary (is *threads into* compatible with *resting on*?). **Do not.** That is a small version of the compile-fictional-physics-into-deterministic-structure move **AB-19 already rejected**: an abstraction's job is to be the right-sized packet of context for an LLM step, not to replace it with a lookup. Cross-boundary coherence is authoring's and improvisation's responsibility; reasoning about consequences is the LLM step's.

**Two rules that follow.**

1. **The port is the unit of lifecycle, not either edge.** With independent kinds the halves can diverge under dissolution --- sever the exterior edge, leave the interior one, and a port is soldered to a battery case while exiting nowhere. Rather than defining that state, **releasing the port takes both halves.** Consistent with [single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).
2. **Do not "optimize" the rope.** Someone reading rope data will see `TiedTo` on both sides and conclude the kind is stored redundantly. **It is coincidentally equal, not redundant.** Build nothing that assumes matching and nothing that assumes differing.

### The strongest argument for ports is not the traversal one

Direct addressing (`OBJECT#ROPE/OBJECT#RopeEnd1` written into the room's edge) would also satisfy traversal. **What ports add is encapsulation** --- and the payoff lands squarely on the two hardest lifecycle rows, neither of which ports were designed for:

| Row | Under direct addressing | Under ports |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **AB-17 reabsorption** | Coiling the rope dissolves `RopeEnd1`; every external edge naming it must be found and rewritten across hosts | Rebind port 1 to the root. **Nothing outside the rope changes.** One internal edit |
| **AB-31 fission** | The member set repartitions and external references dangle --- which is the question the row opened with | Ports rebind; external references survive by construction |

**The port is the object's published interface; its parts are the implementation.** Interior refactoring being non-breaking is exactly what those rows were failing to get.

#### A third argument, on a third axis: the port is the summarization seam (2026-08-07)

**Both arguments above are on one axis each --- traversal is [positioning](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-five-axes-what-the-graphs-flexibility-is-in-service-to), encapsulation is mutation --- and this section chose between them. It missed a *reasoning* argument that was present in the original sketch and never developed.** The scheme's stated goal was that *looking at the rope alone tells you how its parts connect and where they connect externally*; that was written as a convenience and is worth considerably more than that.

**The mechanism: a relation on an interior part crosses the port and is visible at the whole, unexpanded.** A thread snagged on something in one room --- the snag is a relation on a constituent, it ports up, and **reading the span alone already reports that a snag exists.** Where and how is retrievable and **omittable**. That is the [no-silent-omission](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#correction-same-day-reasoning-wants-no-silent-omission-not-losslessness) property delivered structurally: the summary never hides the *existence* of a fact, only its detail.

**Why the third axis matters more than a stronger argument on the first two.** Ports already win positioning and mutation, so another argument there changes no ranking. This is the first developed argument for ports that a **different audience** would make, and under the axis frame that is the kind of evidence that actually moves a decision.

**The honest competitor check, per [the falsification discipline](#what-counts-as-a-falsification-case-learned-the-hard-way-2026-08-05).** A flat representation *can* answer "is there a snag" --- with a reverse index over the constituents, which is **AB-36**. But that is a special-purpose index built to recover what nesting supplies structurally: **a second mechanism for a subclass, which is [position 3](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs)** in the precise sense this plan already rejected. The flat form is rescuable only by an epicycle. **Stated rather than asserted**, because "the alternative obviously cannot do this" is the habit that produced two reversed verdicts here.

**Worked example:** [C7's nesting question](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#the-nesting-question-run-2026-08-07).

### The root node is required, not incidental

The scheme needs `OBJECT#ROPE` to be a node in its own graph, because `part` edges must emanate from the whole. **That is AB-3's pre-registered trigger firing a third time** --- once from composition (where the trigger was written), once from hosting ([AB-33](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation)), now from ports. At which point `CarryClosureFragment = {rootId, members, edges}` is simply what a ported object's graph looks like.

### Consequence: ports re-price the three positions table

**The "assume multi-host for the moment" framing is not a neutral simplification.** [Position 1](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) was charged with C7's objection: one node cannot record *where along*, so segments are still required, so room B holds both a thread node and a local segment with "pull the thread" ambiguously bound.

**Ports answer that objection without single-hosting.** Room B holds the thread node and a **port reference**; the port names exactly which segment; the where-along record lives in the rope's interior graph where it belongs. And multi-hosting under ports is cheaper than the plan ever priced it --- the rope is *listed* in two rooms but there is exactly **one rope graph**, so the interior is not duplicated. That is closer to a presence index than to real multi-hosting.

**So H1 clause 1b takes a new hit --- not a falsification, but its competitor got materially cheaper.** The narrowed dichotomy stands; the choice *within* it shifts toward the branch that keeps lookup-as-the-clean-rule.

**A second consequence, on AB-25 and AB-26:** sibling links living inside the whole's graph give the part-connection graph a home **below** the rooms rather than above them. AB-26 no longer needs an Area-level carrier, which deflates AB-25 further --- Area was largely being summoned to host things above rooms. See **PQ-8** for the loose end in that claim.

#### Candidate: perceptual links as port chains through the containment hierarchy (2026-08-10)

**Recorded as a candidate answer to [C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c22-the-ships-wheel-and-the-lighthouse), kept out of the case per Phase 0's no-solutions rule.** Filed under P3 rather than as a new proposal **because it is ports doing their declared job across several boundaries**, not a rival to anything.

**The scheme, on C22's setup.** A `Shown` edge runs from `ROOM#Bridge`'s root to an **egress port**; that port lands on the `ROOM#Bridge` node inside `AREA#Boat`'s graph and continues to an egress port there; *that* port lands on the `AREA#Boat` node inside `AREA#Bay`'s graph and terminates at `FEATURE#Lighthouse`, hosted there. **Three edges, three graphs, two ports.**

**What it fits, and the list is long enough to be the main argument for it.** Locked **clause 2** (boundary crossings are interior-owned bindings). **[Port-as-scale-boundary](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06)**, genuinely rather than nominally: the facts differ per level --- *you can see it* at the bridge, *where the boat lies relative to it* at the bay --- so the chain is not a relay. **[The summarization seam](#a-third-argument-on-a-third-axis-the-port-is-the-summarization-seam-2026-08-07)**: reading the bridge's graph reports that something is shown without expanding it. **AB-17's and AB-31's rebinding payoff**, which is the scheme's best property --- when the boat relocates to `AREA#NearShore` and the lighthouse is present there too, the egress **rebinds** rather than dissolving and re-deriving, and the decision is made in the one graph that holds both the boat and the lighthouse. **[P4](#proposal-p4-settling-dataflow-and-narrative-heat)**: *actively tracked rather than searched* is iteration 1's stored-and-settled model, with boat movement as the impulse.

**One thing to state loudly, because the design has already produced this misreading twice:** the chain will **look** redundant --- possibly the same kind on every hop --- and someone will try to collapse it. That is [*do not "optimize" the rope*](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06) exactly: coincidentally equal, not redundant.

##### The fan-out cost, stated precisely because it is the scheme's real risk

**Crossing ports are [single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06): one crossing port records one crossing, no fan-in and no fan-out.** That property is what makes the port list a reverse index and what makes footprints computable at plan time --- and it is also what makes this scheme's cost multiply.

| Scope | Ports required |
| --- | --- |
| One room, one landmark, one nesting level | 2 (one per boundary crossed) |
| **R rooms, L landmarks, one level** | **R x L** chains, and **R x L ports on `AREA#Boat`'s boundary alone** --- every pair needs its own slot |
| A chain crossing **d** boundaries | **d** ports, one per crossing |
| **A boundary splitting a world of N participants into k inside and N-k outside** | up to **k x (N-k)**, maximised at k = N/2 --- **N squared over 4** |

**Two separate growth statements, and conflating them understates the risk.** **(i) In world size it is quadratic**, since perceptual links are pairwise. **(ii) In *depth* it is exponential** --- a containment hierarchy with branching factor *b* and depth *D* has on the order of *b^D* leaves, so the pair count goes as *b^2D*. **Nesting is the multiplier**, which is exactly why this cost arrives with C22 and not before: every earlier port case had one boundary.

**And the load concentrates rather than spreading.** Ports pile up on **mid-level** boundaries, where both the interior count and the exterior count are large. A leaf room carries few; `AREA#Boat`'s boundary carries the product of everything inside by everything outside it that anything inside can perceive.

**The mitigation direction, named and deliberately not designed here: relax single-use for perceptual kinds.** The natural saving is **coarsening at the boundary** --- the boat holds **one** `Shown` port to the lighthouse and its rooms share it, since *whether the boat can see the lighthouse* is genuinely a property of the boat's position rather than of each room. That collapses `R x L` to `L` at the boat's boundary. **But sharing a port is fan-in, which single-use forbids** --- so the coarsening and the relaxation are one change seen from two sides, and it cannot be adopted by halves.

**The register predicted this, which is the strongest reason to take it seriously rather than treat it as an exception.** [Its first general finding](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register) is that *soundness of a mechanism is a property of the relation kind, not of the mechanism*, and it names **single-use** in the list of mechanisms to re-check against each new kind. So *single-use holds for containment and spatial kinds and may not hold for perceptual ones* is the predicted shape, not a special pleading. **Single-use is [locked-with-a-lower-bar](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-is-not-locked-and-must-not-ride-in-on-this) --- re-openable on a case rather than on a preference --- and C22 is a case.** **Done: re-opened as PQ-14, 2026-08-10, and the framing above is deliberately the pessimistic one.** PQ-14 records two things this subsection overstates: the relaxation may be **narrower than it looks** (single-use's payoffs rest on one *egress*, not one interior edge, so interior fan-in may leave both intact --- what it threatens is the port-as-unit-of-lifecycle rule, giving the candidate condition *shared port only where lifecycle is shared*), and a **modelling answer may make it moot** (originate the `Shown` edge at the level where visibility is actually determined --- the boat --- and `R x L` collapses to `L` with single-use untouched). **Work the modelling answer first.**

**What must be measured before relaxing anything**, on P4-iteration-1's build-to-measure discipline: actual landmark counts per level, actual nesting depth in play, and whether the concentrated mid-level boundaries are reached at all. **This is the first design in the initiative whose port count scales multiplicatively**, and the choice between paying it and complicating ports should be made against numbers rather than against this table.

### Open questions (AB-34 sub-questions; ids stable, never reused)

~~**None of these is settled.**~~ **Corrected 2026-08-10: most of these are open, but not all.** Where a row has been settled, retired, or decided as a requirement it says so in its own status cell --- PQ-1, PQ-5, PQ-11. Elsewhere, leanings are recorded where one exists, explicitly as leanings.

| ID | Question | Leaning, if any |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **PQ-1** | **Which graph owns the port record --- and with it, the relation kind?** Widened 2026-08-06 by [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06): a single-use port is not an indirection, it **is** the relationship, so "where does the kind live" is a special case of "where does the record live." **Answered the same day --- see [The port record](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06).** Split into **complementary** halves rather than duplicated ones (egress list on the interior graph, ingress port numbers on the exterior node), with the **interior egress list authoritative**. ~~Ingress list accepted as a denormalization inside truth; `positionCache` branch rejected because the room's local read is too hot to depend on cache warmth.~~ **Reversed the same day: the ingress list goes in `positionCache`**, because the room's edges already carry what a coarse read needs, and the list is a **weak** (1:1) denormalization while membership is a **strong** (N:1) one. **PQ-11 retired as a consequence** | **Settled provisionally, after one reversal.** The original sub-question (which side carries the kind) follows from the split; kept below because its reasoning about one-hop resolution is what made the answer acceptable. **Split 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the authority half is *locked* (locked-frame clause 2), not provisional --- only the ingress denormalization's location is, and it rests on AB-35** |
| **PQ-1 (original)** | **Where does the relation kind live?** In the sketch, `tied to` appears on **both** the room-side edge and the interior port edge --- same kind, two shards, two writers, and no rule for which wins on disagreement. That is the drift class this codebase already fights (`repairObjectPlacementDrift`, "positionGraph wins"). Splitting one relation across two records is unavoidable, since the endpoints genuinely live in different shards; **the question is which record owns the kind.** Options: (a) outside only, (b) inside only, (c) both, with a stated winner | ~~**Inside only**, to avoid two writers across two shards.~~ **Reversed 2026-08-06 --- see [A port is a scale boundary](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06).** The question assumed the two edges carry **the same** kind, which is an artifact of reasoning from a rope: homogeneous extent makes a part the same kind of thing as its whole, so the port looks like an identity function. **Heterogeneous cases differ legitimately** (`ThreadsInto` outside, `SolderedTo` inside), so **each side stores its own kind and nothing is duplicated** --- complementary facts again, no shared claim to disagree about. What survives from the original reasoning is the one-hop point: room-local description resolves locally, and detail costs a hop |
| **PQ-2** | **Is `present` a distinct edge kind, or a variant of a port?** Mostly redundant --- a part referenced from a room's graph is present there by virtue of the reference --- **except in the case that matters:** a mid-thread segment lying across a corridor floor, attached to nothing, trippable over. [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) in a pass-through room. Presence without attachment is real. **Sharpened 2026-08-06 under [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) into one record shape with one field varying:** a port's exterior end is *a graph, plus optionally a local endpoint*. **Attachment** = graph `ROOM#A` + endpoint `boulder`. **Presence** = graph `ROOM#A`, no endpoint. Better than "variant" --- it is the same record. **And it reaches further than this row was scoped for: it answers half of AB-6.** If a whole's ports enumerate its presence, presence is **stored, in the whole** --- a carrier neither of that row's two options (stored member set versus derived predicate over edges) contemplated | **The same record, with an optional exterior endpoint.** Flagged for AB-6 rather than settled here |
| **PQ-3** | **Do presence ports collapse when the object is co-located, and re-apply when it spreads?** Proposed as part of the scheme | **Do not collapse in the model; collapse in the projection.** Collapse-and-reapply is a *triggered state transition*, and this plan has repeatedly found triggered transitions are where the cost hides (migration cascade, reabsorption, thrash). If presence ports always exist and merely all point at the same room when coiled, there is no transition to get wrong. The desired collapse --- not listing twenty ports for a coiled rope --- is a render concern, which is the [clean/dirty](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) split already adopted. **Flagged as a leaning with a known counter-view.** **Largely dissolved 2026-08-06 under [single-use ports](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06): there is nothing structural to collapse.** A coiled rope has one presence port; spread across three rooms it has three. The count tracks reality with no transition to trigger. **The residual redundancy is exactly the [C5](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c5-the-chain-mounted-at-both-ends) case** --- load-bearing parts persisting while co-located, so N ports all name the same room --- **which is a render concern, where the leaning already put it.** Strengthened rather than changed |
| **PQ-4** | **Port identity and reuse.** Numbered ports invite it: release port 1, mint port 1 again, and a stale external reference silently rebinds to a *different* attachment. **AB-21**'s identity obligation, one layer down | Allocate monotonically and never reuse, **or** key ports by what they bind rather than by ordinal. Not chosen between. **Second candidate weakened 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10):** keying by what a port binds makes port identity a function of the interior node, which is [the foreclosed reading](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) as an allocation scheme --- and **PQ-9** already reached that conclusion about naming on 2026-08-09 without it reaching this row. **Third candidate proposed 2026-08-10 --- a compact pseudo-random id (`OBJECT#ROPE:k7m2q9`) --- and it is the strongest of the three.** **(a) It makes the foreclosed reading unavailable rather than forbidden.** An opaque token cannot be read as naming the interior node, and it also cannot be read as an **ordinal** --- a second misreading numbers invite that [the negative statement](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) does not even list. `ROPE:1` and `ROPE:2` look ordered and countable, which is the shape of the retired **AB-16** error. Monotonic allocation prevents reuse by *discipline*; a random id prevents it by *construction*. **(b) It removes a write from the allocation path.** Never-reuse cannot be `max(existing) + 1`, which reuses after release, so monotonic needs a durable high-water mark --- a read-modify-write on a path where [`computeStepSequenceFootprint`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/manipulation/kernel/computeStepSequenceFootprint.ts) runs once and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered. A random id is mintable at plan time without reading anything. **(c) The argument this row has never made: merge.** Per-whole monotonic counters are unique *within* a whole and collide on **union**. **AB-17**'s reabsorption and **AB-31**'s fission both union two port sets, where `ROPE-A:1` and `ROPE-B:1` are a silent rebinding --- exactly the failure this row names, arriving by an operation neither existing candidate was tested against. **(d) Precedent, not new machinery.** Ephemera ids are already `TAG#${uuidv4()}` ([`handleApiObjectsChange`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/objects/handleApiObjectsChange.ts)), and that call site already threads `deps.uuidFactory ?? uuidv4`, so the test-determinism obligation is discharged by a shipped pattern. **(e) The scope that lets it be compact is set by locked-frame clause 2:** a port is only ever reached as `OBJECT#ROPE:<id>`, never looked up without its host, so uniqueness is needed only within one whole's port list including the merged case. Roughly 50 bits (ten base32 characters) holds collision probability below 1e-9 at a thousand ports per whole; a full v4 uuid is an order of magnitude more id than the scope needs. **What it does not buy, stated so it is not oversold.** **PQ-10 is untouched** --- `OBJECT#ROPE:k7m2q9` still passes `isEphemeraObjectId` under a `:` separator, so the matcher fix is owed either way --- and **PQ-14 is orthogonal**. **The one real cost is authored ports:** **PQ-9** keeps the door open for an authored port (a lantern with a hook), and a random id gives an author no handle, so either authored ports carry names while improvised ports carry random ids --- two schemes in one slot, a shape this plan has repeatedly regretted --- or authored ports lose their handle. **That is the part to decide rather than assume.** Legibility is a smaller cost than it looks: the worked examples already write `OBJECT#ROPE` for something that is a uuid in production. **And it closes this row's link to AB-21 rather than merely echoing it:** that row's north wall needs a **nominal** identity so two *descriptions* reach one thing, whereas a port is never referred to by description --- which is exactly why an opaque id is right here and a `stableKey` is right there **Decided provisionally 2026-08-13, from conversation, on CC1b's sanctioned first exit** (*"take a provisional keying with the choice named and a rollback line"*) --- **not a closure of this row.** **The keying is the compact opaque id, and the decision ratifies three arguments this plan made separately rather than making a fresh one:** this row already grades it *"the strongest of the three"*; the back-sweep already weakened *key-by-what-it-binds* for making port identity a function of the interior node, **the foreclosed reading**; and **PQ-9**'s numbered lean was already undercut on its own axis, since an opaque id makes the interior-node reading *unavailable* where an ordinal only makes it wrong. **Two of the three converge on a row that did not know it**, which is evidence rather than an echo. **Separator correction, and this row is where the error lives: the candidate is written `OBJECT#ROPE:k7m2q9` throughout, using the `:` that PQ-9 rejects.** Read `#`. **This is a second instance of the tranche-C finding** --- PQ-4 and PQ-9 not knowing each other --- **in the opposite direction from the one tranche C recorded**, and it means this row's *"PQ-10 is untouched --- `OBJECT#ROPE:k7m2q9` still passes `isEphemeraObjectId`"* is an artifact of its own typo: **under `#` the nested form throws instead of passing**, which is the whole point of the separator finding ([verified 2026-08-13](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)). The matcher obligation survives, but as *a port-aware discriminator that must run before any tagged-id predicate*, not as a silent-pass fix. **What the decision buys and what it leaves: the *reuse* half of this row is dissolved by construction rather than answered** --- there is no ordinal to recycle, so *release port 1, mint port 1 again, stale reference silently rebinds* cannot arise. **The *authored ports* half is explicitly NOT decided**, and must not be read as decided by the phrase *opaque by default*: this row already isolates it as *"the part to decide rather than assume,"* and *names for authored, random for improvised* is the **two-schemes-in-one-slot shape this plan has repeatedly regretted**. **A third path is proposed here and deliberately left undecided: separate identity from legibility** --- an authored port carries an **opaque id and an optional authored label**, one identity scheme with a human-facing field beside it, on the shipped precedent of `relationLabel` on edges and `shortName` on nodes. **That would dissolve the fork rather than pick a side, which is why it is worth testing against the corpus rather than adopting now.** **The rollback line, stated so the provisional status is real: this reverses if a case requires a port id to be *derivable* rather than *looked up*.** Opacity's cost is exactly that --- an opaque port id cannot be reconstructed from what it binds, only retrieved from the port list --- so a corpus case where an external reference must be rebuilt without that list in hand is what falsifies it. **PQ-14 remains orthogonal and declared-vs-derived remains PQ-9's** |
| **PQ-5** | **Do ports chain, and is a port itself addressable as a part?** A rope inside a box, tied to something in the room, needs the box to expose a port forwarding to the rope's port. **This is the recursion question flagged when AB-34 was opened** | **Yes to both** --- which is the fractal working correctly. Consequence: traversal depth becomes unbounded and needs a cycle guard. `computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has(...)` is the precedent, and exists for exactly this reason. **Wording corrected 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), substance unchanged:** a port is addressable **as an endpoint**, not *as a part* --- the answer is right and the phrase is the one [the negative statement](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) exists to prevent. **Chaining decided as a requirement 2026-08-10 --- and grounded more broadly than the case that prompted it.** [C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c22-the-ships-wheel-and-the-lighthouse) --- the bridge inside the boat inside the bay --- was raised as the case that establishes the need, and it does. But **the requirement does not depend on [the perceptual-link candidate](#candidate-perceptual-links-as-port-chains-through-the-containment-hierarchy-2026-08-10) being adopted**, and recording it that narrowly would leave it re-openable by dropping that scheme. **It follows from the two locked clauses at any nesting depth greater than one:** locked-frame clause 1 gives every whole its own graph, and locked-frame clause 2 requires each boundary crossing to be a binding the interior owns --- so a relation between a node two levels in and a node outside is **necessarily** two bindings meeting in an intermediate graph. That is a chain, for **any** relation kind, and there is no third option that is not direct addressing of an interior node from outside. **C22's contribution is that it is the first case where the fiction forces depth greater than one** rather than a thought experiment supplying it, and that the hops carry *different* facts per level (*you can see it* at the bridge, *where the boat lies relative to it* at the bay) --- [port-as-scale-boundary](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06) rather than a relay, so the chain is not collapsible even where its hops look alike. **The addressable-as-an-endpoint half settles with it**, since the chain's intermediate hop *is* an interior edge terminating on a port. **Two obligations this promotes from hypothetical to owed.** The **cycle guard** on unbounded traversal depth (`computeCarryClosure`'s `closureSet.has(...)`, above) becomes an implementation obligation of the port scheme rather than the consequence of a leaning. And **PQ-14's fan-out cost becomes mandatory rather than avoidable** --- while chaining was a leaning, the *d*-ports-per-*d*-boundaries cost could in principle be dodged by declining to chain, and it cannot now. That raises PQ-14's urgency without touching its answer |
| **PQ-6** | **Egress discovery: how does a host find who references its ports?** Ingress is fine --- a room's edge names the port, which names the host, so plan-time footprint is discoverable in that direction. **The reverse is not indexed anywhere.** See **AB-36**. **Widened 2026-08-06 to absorb the case PQ-7 used to carry:** *interior* mutation invalidating an *exterior* reference. Fission repartitions the rope and releases port 1 while `ROOM#A`'s edge still names it --- and the rope has no idea anyone is pointing at it. **The rule that fixes it is the existing discipline one level over --- a host may not release an externally-referenced port** --- so this is not a new contract, only a new thing that must be discoverable for the old contract to be enforceable. **Largely answered 2026-08-06, and by the design rather than by an index: [ports are single-use and name the graph they exit to](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06).** A whole's own graph therefore enumerates its referrers --- **the port list is the reverse index, stored where it is needed** --- so egress discovery is local, the footprint is computable at plan time (which `computeStepSequenceFootprint` running once demands), and the release rule becomes locally enforceable. **What remains open** is whether a port's exterior half is discoverable when the *exterior* graph is the one being mutated without opening the interior, which is PQ-1's record-ownership question | **No longer the blocking item.** Deflated by the single-use design; residue folds into PQ-1 |
| **PQ-7** | **~~Does assert-and-throw survive cross-host references?~~ Restated 2026-08-06 --- the original claim was wrong, and the correction moves the cost off this proposal.** As first written: ports break the same-host locality that makes `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` enforceable, so this was "a real loss of a shipped guarantee." **Withdrawn under a worked scenario.** A rope tied to a cliff-top boulder puts the boulder, the rope node and the edge `boulder -TiedTo-> OBJECT#ROPE:1` **all in `ROOM#CliffTop`'s graph** --- so `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing` scans one graph and finds it. Requiring every connection to `OBJECT#ROPE`, port-qualified or not, to be dissolved before removal **is** the existing contract, unchanged. **The real question, and it is not about ports:** *when* does a port reference live on a graph the whole's node is absent from? Under **multi-host presence + ports**, never --- the node is in every room it is present in, so anything that can see a port also holds the node, and locality is preserved. Under **single-host at an Area + ports**, always --- room B references the port while the node sits at the Area. **So the exposure belongs to [H1 clause 4](#h1-is-a-conjunction-and-its-clauses-are-tested-separately), not to P3, and ports are merely what made it visible** | **Restated, not open.** Kept rather than deleted per this plan's rule on recording supersessions; ~~the surviving cost is logged against H1 clause 4~~ **--- and H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07, so that is a dead home. Flagged by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) 2026-08-10: the exposure was conditional on single-host-at-an-Area and may be *moot* rather than homeless, but the underlying question is first-order for locked-frame clause 2 --- does interior-owned encapsulation cost the shipped `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` guarantee? --- and should be answered or retired, not left parked on a withdrawn clause.** **Answered and retired 2026-08-10 --- the fork this row keeps live was decided twice and neither decision reached it.** *Single-host at an Area* was **H1 clause 4**, entailed by **H1 clause 1b**; H1 and [P1](#proposal-p1-single-host-objects--hosting-at-the-containing-node) were **withdrawn 2026-08-07** with no clause of H1 clause 1 surviving [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine). Independently, **AB-8 was re-framed 2026-08-09 toward (a)-with-ports** --- presence is *the set of graphs holding a qualified reference*, one interior graph, not duplicated --- which **is** multi-host presence, arrived at from AB-4 rather than from this row. So the branch that carried the exposure has been dead for three days and the branch that dissolves it has been the working model for one. **Signed off 2026-08-10: objects are multi-host; the whole is not hosted at an Area.** What is *not* decided by this, and must not be read in: AB-8 stays **Open** between (a)-with-ports and (b), and framing (c) remains a modeller's option under H3 --- what is settled is only that *removing* multi-host is off the table. **The first-order question the back-sweep raised is answered rather than merely mooted, and locked-frame clause 2 survives it.** Under multi-host + ports the whole's node sits in every graph that references it, so `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing` scans one graph and finds the edge --- this row's own boulder scenario. **And [chaining](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) does not reintroduce the exposure**, which is the one thing that could have: a chain is not a long-range reference but **a sequence of intra-graph hops**, each with both endpoints in the graph that holds it, so per-hop locality is exactly what the chain preserves. Interior-owned encapsulation therefore costs the shipped guarantee **nothing architecturally**. **The whole residue is PQ-10** --- the matchers cannot *see* a port-qualified endpoint, so the guarantee is sound in the model and broken in the code until they are fixed. A live row with a bounded fix, which is the right home for it |
| **PQ-11** | **RETIRED 2026-08-06, same day it was opened --- the premise was withdrawn.** The ingress list moved from `positionGraph` to `positionCache`, so there is no denormalization in truth, no drift, and no repair story owed. **Kept rather than deleted** per this plan's supersession rule, and because one observation in it survives as a note on **AB-35**: a derived list is repaired by **recompute rather than merge**, which is materially cheaper than `repairObjectPlacementDrift`'s job and is a property worth having on record for whatever the cache's invalidation story turns out to be. Original text follows | **Retired --- premise withdrawn** |
| **PQ-11 (original)** | **The ingress list can drift --- what is the conflict rule, and what repairs it?** Opened 2026-08-06 as the obligation created by accepting the ingress port list as a denormalization inside `positionGraph` rather than in cache. **The conflict rule is the easy half and follows from authority: the interior egress list wins**, mirroring the shipped "`positionGraph` wins" rule for `Meta` placement. **The repair story is the open half, and nothing does this today.** Sub-questions: what detects divergence (a sweep like `repairObjectPlacementDrift`, a read-time reconciliation, or a write-time assertion), what the failure looks like from a player's seat (a port listed on a node that the object no longer exits to renders as... nothing? a coarsened attachment? --- note **the coarsening default makes the *reverse* direction benign** but says nothing about a *phantom* ingress entry), and whether repair is delete-only like the objects lane's precedent or must reconstruct. **Note the asymmetry worth exploiting:** the ingress list is fully derivable from the egress lists of objects present, so repair is a **recompute**, not a merge --- which is a materially easier repair than `repairObjectPlacementDrift`'s. | **Conflict rule settled** (interior wins); recompute-not-merge is the shape; detection and cadence open. Ties to **AB-35** |
| **PQ-12** | **Must relocation follow adjacency, and is that a rule or the caller's business?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in). Hauling a rope in moves the far end `ROOM#C -> ROOM#B`, not `ROOM#C -> ROOM#A`, because the rope is continuous and its end travels the path. **Nothing in the port scheme says so.** If it is a **rule** --- a part may relocate only to a host adjacent along the relation licensing its connection to its siblings --- then **AB-26's adjacency licensing appears on the *write* side**, where this plan has only ever discussed it as read-side structure, and that is a genuine widening of that row. If it is **the caller's business**, nothing prevents teleporting a mid-span, and the rope's spatial coherence becomes a convention rather than an invariant. **Cost is not the discriminator:** hauling [Ariadne's thread](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) twenty rooms is N steps, but the footprint is known up front from the egress list, so `MultiKeyUpdate` folds them into one `transactWrite`. **What is open besides the rule itself:** narration cadence for an N-step haul, which belongs to the relational-narration initiative rather than here | Leaning **rule**, because the invariant is cheap to state and the alternative makes spatial coherence unenforceable --- but this widens AB-26 and should be decided there rather than assumed here. **Re-framed 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10), ~~and it strengthens the leaning~~ --- *corrected later the same day; see the reversal at the end of this cell*:** under locked-frame clause 1 no part relocates to a host --- a **port's egress is rewritten**, and an egress is a boundary fact the interior owns, so the rule is **locally enforceable inside the whole**. The *rule versus the caller's business* fork assumed the enforcement point was outside. **Leaning reversed 2026-08-10 --- now *against* a hard adjacency rule, and the back-sweep note above is withdrawn as an argument for one.** **The disagreement is not with the coherence reasoning.** A rope's end travelling the path is right, and some invariant at some level should say so. **The objection is that the fork was malformed, and both halves of it share the defect:** *rule* versus *the caller's business* conflates **whether there is an invariant** with **which layer enforces it**, and those are independent. Spatial coherence can be guaranteed by the **instruction planner** --- which composes the steps and knows the route --- without any operation primitive refusing a non-adjacent egress rewrite. **We do not yet know which layer it belongs to, and that is the actual open question**; the row currently reads as though it were answered. **The concrete hazard, and it is about how this file is read rather than about the design.** Written as a hard rule, a cold-read collaborator will push the restriction **down toward the operation primitives**, which is further than anything here has proven workable --- and **the back-sweep's own note is the first instance of that drift**: *locally enforceable inside the whole* establishes that enforcement **could** live at the primitive, not that it **should**. A capability read as a mandate is exactly the failure being guarded against, produced here by the sweep that was supposed to catch such things. **The asymmetry that settles which way to lean while the layer is undecided.** A primitive that permits non-adjacent relocation can always have a rule imposed above it; a primitive that forbids it cannot be relaxed from above, and it forecloses paths that legitimately need to violate adjacency --- **authoring, world-building tools, and error repair**, none of which travel a route. Refusing at the primitive makes those unimplementable; permitting at the primitive costs only that coherence is not yet enforced anywhere, which is today's situation. **So: no hard rule at the primitive layer; the invariant stays real and unhomed until the layer question is worked.** **What this does to AB-26:** the widening onto its write side is **withdrawn rather than deferred** --- there is no write-side adjacency licensing to place there until the enforcement layer is chosen, and the earlier note asked AB-26 to decide a question that belongs to the planner/primitive split |
| **PQ-13** | **Does a plain object carry a presence port?** Opened 2026-08-06 from [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in)'s membership rule --- *an object is present in a room iff at least one of its ports egresses there*. Applied uniformly, a lantern on a table needs one port, egressing to the room, presence-linked to its own root. **The tension is real in both directions.** Uniformity makes a simple object the **degenerate case rather than a special case** --- one port, zero hops --- which is the same shape as the argument that rescued [position 2](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) (*a loop that sometimes iterates once is one mechanism, not two*) and is what the [fractal](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#clean-fractal-dirty-implementation) demands if it is to be honest. ~~**Against it:** this is overhead on the commonest object in the game, on the hottest read path.~~ **The hot-path half of that objection was withdrawn 2026-08-06** when the ingress list moved to `positionCache`: ports now live only in the object's own graph, so a room read never sees them and the lantern's port costs the hot path nothing. **What survives of the objection** is that a per-object graph for something with no interior is close to pure ceremony --- a storage and write-path cost, not a read one. **A third option not yet priced:** the port is *implied* for undecomposed objects and materialized only on first decomposition --- cheap, but it makes presence mean two different things depending on a state nobody declared, which is the kind of split this plan has repeatedly regretted | **Take the uniformity** --- but deliberately, on the fractal argument, not by aesthetics, and with the hot-path cost measured rather than assumed **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the third option stopped being cheap on the day it was priced.** *The port is implied for undecomposed objects and materialized only on first decomposition* was charged one cost --- *presence means two different things depending on a state nobody declared* --- and **AB-9's committed carve-out gives that state a trigger**: a perception command actively seeking detail may mint the level it seeks, so the undeclared state is now changed **by looking at things**, on a read-shaped path, for the commonest object in the game. **The uniformity option gains from the same source**, since AB-5's mint *interposes a level between A and B* and wants the lantern already addressable as a whole-in-waiting rather than converted into one. **Not a clause finding** --- locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 are silent on a partless object, a thing with no interior having no boundary to mediate. What moved is the **price**, and this row's recommendation is better supported now than when it was written. **Narrowed 2026-08-20, carried here 2026-08-23 from the ludicGraph-ports plan on its close.** That plan had flagged this row's *forced yes* for a re-run, on the ground that it was argued when there was **one** containment relation and `In`/`PartOf` had since split it in two --- so *plain* now has to say **empty of which**. **The re-run narrowed the flag rather than clearing it, and the narrowing is the useful part: the flag covers only the adjective *plain*** --- how one would enumerate the category this row denies exists. **It says nothing about mechanism, and in particular it does not reopen whether a hosted thing has a presence port**, which this row answers **yes** and which is the base case locked clause 1 rests on. Reading the flag wider than that caused a false blocker once, in the plan that raised it. **What is genuinely unanswered is a different question and has its own row:** whether a hosted thing gets a *materialized* graph for the port to live in --- [AB-62](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) --- which turns on locked-frame clause 3's deliberate silence about minting, not on this row. **Still owed here:** the one-line statement of *empty of which*, now that containment is two kinds. |
| **PQ-14** | **Does single-use survive multi-level chains --- i.e. may one port carry more than one interior edge?** Opened 2026-08-10 from [C22](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c22-the-ships-wheel-and-the-lighthouse). **[Single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) is not disqualified --- it is under pressure**, and the pressure's character is what makes this a row rather than a note: the [fan-out cost](#the-fan-out-cost-stated-precisely-because-it-is-the-schemes-real-risk) is **quadratic in world size and exponential in depth**, and it appears **under quite ordinary fiction** --- a boat in a bay with a few landmarks --- rather than at an exotic extreme. A scalability risk reachable by normal play is a different class of objection from one reachable only by contrivance. **Two things must be worked before this is decided, and neither is settled here.** **(i) What single-use actually buys is narrower than the prohibition.** Both of its payoffs --- [the port list as a reverse index](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) and a footprint computable at plan time --- depend on a port having **one egress**, not on it having one *interior* edge. Fan-in on the interior side may leave both intact, which would make the relaxation far cheaper than the section's framing implies. **What it does threaten is [the port as the unit of lifecycle](#a-port-is-a-scale-boundary-not-a-relay-2026-08-06)**: if several rooms share a port, releasing it releases all of them. **So the candidate condition is *interior edges may share a port only when they share a lifecycle*** --- true for boat-level visibility (the whole boat sees the lighthouse or does not), false in general. Work that condition, not the blanket question. **(ii) A modelling answer may make it moot.** If a `Shown` edge is only ever created at the level where the perception is actually determined --- the boat's position decides visibility, so the chain originates at `AREA#Boat`'s root, not at each room's --- then the count is per (container, landmark), not per (room, landmark), and `R x L` collapses to `L` **without touching single-use at all**. The cost is one traversal hop for a reasoning step asked about the bridge specifically. **Decide (ii) before (i)**, since a modelling convention that dissolves the pressure is cheaper than re-opening a locked-with-a-lower-bar property. **What would settle it:** P4-iteration-1's instrumentation --- real landmark counts per level, real nesting depth in play, and whether the concentrated mid-level boundaries are reached at all. **This is the first time the lower bar has actually been met** --- single-use is re-openable *on a case*, and C22 is one. **What single-use *is*, characterized 2026-08-10, because it changes what re-opening costs.** It is **not a derived requirement**: no corpus case demands it, and the plan nowhere argues it is better than fan-out. It entered as a ***clarification of a misreading*** --- *"an earlier reading treated ports as reusable boundary interfaces; that reading is wrong"* --- which is a statement about **what was meant**, not about what would work. The richer version was corrected, never evaluated. **Its two justifications arrived afterward** and are written as *consequences* of the clarification, not grounds for it. **So it is a *minimal primitive whose justifications were discovered post-hoc*** --- distinct from a **placeholder** (`Custom -> defer`, known-inadequate and awaiting design) and from a **derived constraint** ([C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s DAG, [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s where-along). **The operative consequence: the absence of fan-out is not a decision against fan-out, it is the absence of a decision about it.** Re-opening means doing the reasoning for the first time rather than overturning any --- materially cheaper than, say, re-opening H1's clause 1b, where there was an argument to defeat. **And the payoffs cannot be cited as grounds**, being post-hoc; note that both plausibly *survive* the relaxation (they rest on one egress), and **a justification that survives removal of the thing it justifies is a hint the thing was not doing the work.** **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche C)](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): the seventh member of [the clause-2 direction cluster](#finding-6-the-clause-2-direction-cluster-reaches-seven-and-one-member-sequences-work-on-the-answer), and the one whose **sequencing** depends on the answer.** (ii) is an outward read --- create the `Shown` edge at `AREA#Boat`'s root and let a step asked about the bridge pay *one traversal hop*, which is the interior reaching its container's root. **Under the strict reading of locked-frame clause 2 the cheap answer is illegal, (ii) is unavailable, and *decide (ii) before (i)* inverts**, leaving the relaxation of single-use as the only route --- the expensive one this order exists to avoid. The evidence still leans to the gloss, so (ii) probably survives; what has changed is that *probably* now sequences work. | **Open --- single-use under pressure, not withdrawn.** Sequenced: modelling answer (ii) first, lifecycle-sharing condition (i) second, both against P4-i1 measurements rather than argument |
| **PQ-10** | **Port-qualified endpoints pass the id predicates and fail the equality checks --- a silent-wrong-answer hazard, verified 2026-08-06.** [`isEphemeraTaggedId`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/baseClasses.ts) splits on `#` and requires exactly two sections, so **`OBJECT#ROPE:1` passes `isEphemeraObjectId`**: one `#`, correct tag. `isEphemeraPositionRelationalEdgeData` therefore accepts the edge as well-formed and it parses into `relationalEdges` --- and then `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing`'s `edge.from === id` compares `'OBJECT#ROPE:1'` against `'OBJECT#ROPE'` and **the assert passes vacuously.** `edgeReferencesObjectId` has the same equality and the same hole. **The edge is simultaneously valid to the parser and invisible to the check**, which is worse than a loud rejection. Not architectural --- the matchers must learn to resolve a port-qualified endpoint to its base object, in the same change that introduces ports, never after | Fix the matchers; the open part is **which** sites (at minimum `assertNoRelationalEdgesReferencing`, `edgeReferencesObjectId`, `bothObjectsOnGraph`), and whether resolution belongs in the parse or at each comparison |
