| **PQ-8** | **Where do sibling links' licensing labels live?** AB-26's part-connections are labeled by the *licensing spatial relation* --- "connects to the shaft section by way of the 'into the abyss' exit" --- which is **exterior world truth**. So a sibling link sits inside the rope while its label points outward. Referencing an Exit by id is arguably fine (an Exit is an edge, not a graph node, so it is not a port case), but it reopens **AB-28 / AB-29**: repair the floor-hole and the dependent link is now buried a level deeper inside an object | Ports do not help here and may make discovery worse. **This is the loose end in the claim that AB-26 gets a home below the rooms.** **Raised 2026-08-10 by [the back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) to a question about the lock itself:** this row's defence (*an Exit is an edge, not a graph node*) answers locked-frame clause 2's **gloss**, which names only inward addressing; against the clause's **headline** (*boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns*) an outward reference from an interior node is unmediated. **The clause does not state its direction, and this row is the case that needs it stated** **A counterpart row arrived 2026-08-20: [AB-57](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only), the same coupling read from the other end.** This row is an **interior** edge whose label names **exterior** truth; AB-57 asks whether the **exterior** should carry a representation of an **interior** crossing --- and it was opened without citing this row, which is [the process finding](#the-process-finding-a-row-that-corrects-another-row-records-the-correction-on-itself) recurring on a row three months of sweeps have not caught. **Two consequences for *this* row.** **(i) *Where the label lives* and AB-57's *manner* residue are close to one question.** AB-26's label is a manner carrier (an aperture, a direction, a reversibility), so a home for one is probably a home for the other --- and AB-26's own correction already widened licensing past Exit to **any spatial relation of any provenance**, which is the widening AB-57's aperture needs. **(ii) Do not merge them.** This row is a **part-to-part** edge within one whole; AB-57 asks whether the **host** carries anything at all. Answering them together is sensible; treating them as one row is the instance-taken-for-the-category error AB-26 itself was a correction of |
| **PQ-9** | **Numbered or named, declared or derived --- and what separator?** A named port (`ROPE:end-a`) is authored surface area; a numbered one is minted at attachment. Separately: a *declared* port is an explicit interface, a *derived* one re-poses AB-6's stored-versus-derived question one layer down. **Related to PQ-4 but distinct** --- PQ-4 asks about reuse, this asks whether the ordinal means anything. **The separator was filed as cosmetic and is not, per PQ-10.** A `:` is **silently swallowed** by every id predicate in the system; a second `#` makes `isEphemeraTaggedId` **throw** `Illegal nested EphemeraId`, loudly, at every site not yet converted | On the separator: **prefer the one that fails loudly.** Choosing `#` converts a silent-wrong-answer class into a crash class, which is the same instinct as BD-33/35's no-silent-strip rule. On declared-vs-derived: **[single-use](#ports-are-single-use-and-that-is-not-a-detail-clarified-2026-08-06) leans *derived*** --- a port minted per connection falls out of the connection rather than being authored. It does not foreclose authored ports (a lantern with a hook), but the default now follows from the design. ~~Nothing recorded on numbered-vs-named~~ **First concrete argument on numbered-vs-named, 2026-08-09, and it favours numbered.** Named shorthand keeps re-appearing in design conversation because it conveys semantics instantly --- `thread:skein` reads better than `thread:1` --- and **that convenience is exactly the hazard.** A port named after the interior node behind it re-creates the misreading [the negative statement](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) exists to foreclose, in which the port identifier is taken to *be* the part. That misreading has already propagated twice in a day once, and a naming scheme that makes it the natural reading will do so continuously. **Numbered keeps the boundary and the interior visibly separate:** `thread:1` and `thread:2` are boundary facts, and *which* of skein or span each reaches is an interior edge in the thread's own graph. **A lean, not a decision** --- authored ports may still want names, and the separator question (PQ-10) is untouched by either **[Back-swept 2026-08-10 (tranche B)](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10): a third option arrived on this row's axis the same day.** [PQ-4](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s compact pseudo-random id is neither numbered nor named, and **it satisfies this row's own argument more strongly than the option this row leans to**: if the hazard is an identifier that invites being read as the interior node, an opaque id makes that reading **unavailable** where an ordinal only makes it wrong. **Both rows independently record the same residual cost** --- authored ports (the lantern with a hook) want names --- without either noticing the other. **Provenance stated rather than left implicit: PQ-4's candidate was written by tranche A of this sweep**, so this is an obligation the sweep created, not one the lock exposed. **Separator premise verified against shipped code 2026-08-13** (prompted by the user, after an unverified premise in CC1c failed the same way): [`isEphemeraTaggedId`](../../../../../packages/mtw-interfaces/ts/baseClasses.ts) splits on `#`, **throws `Illegal nested EphemeraId` when there are more than two sections**, returns `false` under two, and otherwise compares section 0 to the tag. So `OBJECT#ROPE#ab6129d` throws and `OBJECT#ROPE:ab6129d` returns **`true`**. **"Every id predicate in the system" is literal rather than a generalization** --- `isEphemeraObjectId`, `isEphemeraRoomId`, `isEphemeraCharacterId` and the rest are all this one factory applied to a tag. **One thing sharper than the row's wording: `:` is not tolerated or stripped, it is *affirmed*** --- and since the predicate is a type guard (`value is EphemeraWrappedId<G>`), **TypeScript narrows a port address to `EphemeraObjectId` and hands every downstream consumer the compiler's endorsement of a wrong type.** **Two limits on the finding, both of which a reader could easily over-read past.** **(i) There is no compile-time protection either way.** `EphemeraWrappedId<T>` is `` `${T}#${string}` `` and `${string}` matches `ROPE#ab6129d`, so **the nested form satisfies `EphemeraObjectId` statically** --- assigning a port address to an `EphemeraObjectId` compiles clean. The entire separator difference is *runtime* behaviour inside the predicate; nothing here is caught by types. **(ii) The loudness is a throw *inside a predicate*, which has a cost this row does not price.** `isEphemeraObjectId(x)` is the natural way to ask *is this an object or something else*, and under `#` that branch **crashes** on a port address rather than returning `false` --- so a port address cannot be handed to the tagged-id family even for legitimate discrimination. **Choosing `#` therefore obliges a port-aware discriminator to land *in* the same change**, PQ-10's pattern applied to itself. **The finding survives both limits and the lean stands**, but it is a runtime fail-fast argument with an attached obligation, not a typing argument. **Graduated to a decision 2026-08-17, via `ludicGraphPorts` LP1.** Scoped exactly as the 2026-08-15 rescoping already states, not widened by the graduation: the terminal is now structured, so the separator governs only the **serde/Dynamo string form**, deferred behind a gate (the first time a port address must stand free of an edge). Within that scope, `#` is decided --- fail loudly rather than silently affirm. Outside that scope there is nothing to decide yet, because the domain layer does no parsing at all. **Numbered-vs-named superseded 2026-08-13: [PQ-4](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) took the compact opaque id provisionally**, which is the third option tranche B flagged as satisfying *this row's own argument* more strongly than the numbered lean recorded above. **The lean is retired rather than reversed** --- it was right about the hazard and reached for the weaker instrument. **Declared-vs-derived is untouched and stays this row's**, as does the authored-port residual that PQ-4 explicitly left open. |

### The motivation, stated plainly (2026-08-06)

**Ports are an attempt to find out whether the design can do without [H1 clause 1](#h1-is-a-conjunction-and-its-clauses-are-tested-separately)** --- because multi-hosting of wholes looks, on reflection, **actually important**. Recorded because this plan's working-hypothesis mechanism depends on knowing *why* a candidate was built, and a scheme designed to make a rival framing viable should not be assessed as though it were neutral.

**This does not withdraw H1**, and nothing below is a decision. What it does is put a fourth, different kind of pressure on H1 clause 1b, which is worth tallying in one place:

1. **The same-mechanism requirement** (AB-32) --- an argument from requirements, producing [the three positions](#three-positions-on-reference-and-what-h1-clause-1b-actually-costs) and the narrowed dichotomy.
2. **Ports re-pricing position 1** --- the C7 where-along objection, which was the main charge against multi-hosting, is answered by ports without single-hosting.
3. **PQ-7's restatement** --- the cross-host enforceability cost turns out to belong to **H1 clause 4** (entailed by 1b), and it vanishes under multi-host + ports.
4. **A stated judgment that multi-hosting matters** --- weaker than the others as evidence, and load-bearing as a statement of intent.

**What withdrawal would cost, so the decision is made with the number in view.** The `H1-dependent` set currently stands at **six rows: AB-6, AB-8, AB-9, AB-11, AB-15, AB-17** --- and AB-9's tag covers only the C6 escalation, not its question. That is a small set for a hypothesis this prominent, which is exactly the measurement [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) was built to produce: **H1 has been deciding less than its position in this document suggests.** Note also that H1 clause 3 (spanning things are parts) is *not* uniquely H1's and survives a withdrawal intact, as does 1a --- so what is actually at stake is 1b and its two entailments, not the hypothesis wholesale.

**Do not let ports decide this by momentum.** The scheme's independence from any hosting model is its stated virtue; if it also happens to make one model cheaper, that is evidence to weigh, not a conclusion to arrive at by having built it.

### The failure mode all nine are measured against

**An address with no declaration is [the phantom edge](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) one scale down.** Ports exist precisely to give the address a declared home on both sides. Any answer above that leaves a reference implicit, unindexed, or unenforced re-introduces the defect the scheme was built to remove --- which is what makes PQ-6 and PQ-7 the blocking pair rather than two of nine.

---

## The whole/part dissolution and its unswept consequences (2026-08-09)

**Not a new decision --- an entailment of one already locked, recorded because a sweep found it had not propagated.** [H3 clause 2](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) establishes that whole and part are **roles relative to a level, not kinds**, on [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s four-level chain; the [graduated concepts entry](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports) carries it durably with the consequence attached --- *any rule that gives parts and wholes different behaviour is not a rule at all, since it assigns two behaviours to one object*; and [AB-0](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s lock cites the same finding as the name's payload, *nothing distinctive at any level to type*. **Three independent statements of it, and it still did not reach four rows.**

### The entailment, stated once so rows can point at it rather than re-derive it

**"Whole" is not a term of art and denotes no category.** It is a contextual position --- *this is the whole comprising these parts* --- and the concepts entry's own definition (*a thing with a `positionGraph` of its own and a root node in it*) is, term for term, **a host that has a root node**. Three consequences, each of which a row below was still treating as open:

1. **"Whole" adds nothing to "host."** Locked clause 1 is therefore not a claim about a construct at all; it is the claim that **hosting is recursive**. [P2](#the-conceptual-error-it-starts-from-hosting-creates-a-phantom-edge) already wrote the equation in passing --- *"the rope hosts its parts exactly as the box hosts its contents --- same mechanism"* --- and treated it as a load-bearing reading rather than an aside, which is what stopped its P2 clause 3 recreating the phantom-edge defect one scale down.
2. **A fork of the form "owned by the room, or by the whole?" is not a fork.** A room is a whole of its contents; a whole is a host of its parts; there is one slot, asked from two directions.
3. **A question whose answer turns on whether something *is* a whole has no purchase**, because everything is one relative to whatever it contains --- including things that contain nothing, which is exactly **PQ-13**.

**What this does not settle, and the distinction matters more than the entailment does.** The claims are **earned below the room and aspirational above it**, per the lock's [own recorded risk](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#consequences-accepted-by-locking-recorded-so-they-are-owned-rather-than-rediscovered) and the warning that travels with the concepts entry. `EphemeraMembershipHostId = Room | Character` is a **closed enumeration**, and the room boundary is an authored asset-stack merge seam rather than a play-time graph mutation. **The residual privilege lives in that union, not in the vocabulary** --- which is **AB-12**'s deliberately separable second bet, and it stays open. **Dissolving *whole* does not dissolve *Room*, and this section must not be cited as though it had.**

### The four rows still written against it

| Row | The framing that presumes the dissolved distinction | What the entailment does to it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **PQ-13** --- does a plain object carry a presence port | Recorded as *"a lean, not a decision,"* weighing uniformity against ceremony --- while its own text already says the level at which an object switches mechanisms *"**is** the whole/part line C11 dissolved"* | **Forced yes by entailment, not decided on the balance of argument.** There is no category *plain object* to switch **from** --- only a thing nobody has decomposed yet. The row identified its own decider and then went on weighing. **This is the base case locked clause 1 rests on**, so it unblocks what it was flagged as blocking |
| **AB-4** --- what is the level-crossing relation | The record option is *"a whole need not be a graph node, needs no host"* --- and the row calls this the **highest-leverage choice in the initiative** | **That horn is dead**, and was dead on 2026-08-06: locked clause 1 gives every whole a graph and a root node. The row's stated deciding sub-question (*can a whole itself be a part of a bigger whole?*) is trivially yes under the contextual reading **and** was already answered yes by AB-32. **The fork has one live horn; the row is not blocked on AB-32** |
| **AB-32** --- what must composition express | *"The actual decider: must a relation ever attach to a whole rather than to one of its parts? Relations need nodes, so a yes forces wholes to be nodes"* | **The decider cannot discriminate.** Wholes are already nodes by locked clause 1, whichever way the requirement goes. **The row half-noticed** --- it appends that the decider *"assumed endpoints are node references"* --- and did not follow through. Its two answered requirements (wholes nest; one reference mechanism) are untouched |
| **AB-42** --- are negatives nodes or a collection | Option (a) is *"a negative collection owned by the room **or** the whole"* --- consequence 2 above, verbatim | **Not two candidates on that axis.** The room *is* the whole. What survives is AB-42's real question, node-versus-collection, which P2's phantom-edge argument already answers one level up and which this section does not touch |

**Its source case states the fork and flags it as a test, which is the closest anything came to catching this.** [C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about): *"The coarse envelope could belong to the **room** or to a **whole** inside it … Under the fractal's no-privileged-level claim these should be one mechanism, and whether they are is a real test of it: H3's ports are the boundary mechanism for a **whole**, and a room carrying negative records has no port to hang them on."* **The last clause is the presumption doing the damage** --- it infers *a room has no ports* from *a room is not a whole*, which is the dissolved distinction supplying a premise. The case was right that this tests no-privileged-level and wrong that the test was unrun.

**Counter-evidence from inside the same cluster, worth recording because it shows the usage was already split.** [AB-45](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) treats *"what a tavern needs"* as a whole carrying an intensional description. **A tavern is room-scale.** So one row in the negatives cluster was using whole=host in practice on the same day another posed them as alternatives.

### Vocabulary residue: three rows that reach the right answer under a name that will re-teach the error

**Separated from the table above deliberately** --- nothing here is a wrong conclusion, and treating naming residue as equivalent to a stale decider would inflate what the sweep found:

- **AB-37, *"Whole-level reasoning summaries."*** Implies a summary tier. The row's own finding --- *"sufficiency is question-relative, so there is no single summary"* --- says there is none; summaries are at whatever level is read.
- **AB-25** --- *"any scheme that hosts a whole 'above' its parts."* H1-era height framing; a whole hosts its parts **in itself**. The row is already deflated and this is residue on a narrowed question.
- **AB-31** --- *"mint a second whole."* The identity question it owns (one new whole, or two and retire the original?) is real and untouched; the surrounding multi-step-write description is H1-era, which the row already says.

**Sixteen further rows use *whole* contextually and correctly** (AB-6, AB-11, AB-13, AB-15, AB-16, AB-17, AB-19, AB-26, AB-34, AB-36, AB-39, AB-41, AB-45, AB-46, PL-1, PQ-2/6/7). **Recorded as a count rather than a list of nothing**, because it is the measurement that makes the four above a specific failure rather than a general vocabulary problem.

### The process finding: the distinction grew back after it was locked

**The four rows split cleanly by date, and the split is the finding.** AB-4, AB-32 and AB-25 were written **before** C11 (2026-08-07) and carry deciders that predate it --- ordinary staleness, and exactly what this plan's supersession convention exists to absorb. **AB-42 and C17 were written on 2026-08-09, two days *after* the lock**, and reintroduced room-versus-whole as a live fork. **That is not stale text. That is the distinction growing back in new work.**

**Why it is worth a note rather than a fix.** This plan already names the failure mode --- *a caveat nothing acts on becomes an assumption* ([AB-0](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)), and the lock's own risk entry warns that *"the room/area seam is now less visible, not less real."* **This is the mirror image of both**: not a caveat that decayed into an assumption, but a **locked claim that failed to reach a cluster opened after it**, in a plan whose locked claims are otherwise its most-cited text. The mechanism that failed is the one thing locking was supposed to guarantee.

**The likely cause, stated so it can be checked rather than assumed:** the negatives cluster (AB-42--AB-45, C17, C18) came in through **P5 and improvisation licence**, not through the positional lane, and every statement of the whole/part dissolution lives in positional-lane text --- H3's clause table, the locked frame, the concepts entry. **A lock is only reached by whoever reads the section it lives in.** Worth watching whether the next cluster opened from a non-positional direction repeats it; a second instance makes this structural rather than incidental, and would argue for the dissolution being restated at the head of the Open decisions table rather than only in the frame that produced it.

---

## The parts-and-ports back-sweep (2026-08-10)

**The retroactive half of [the dependent-set discipline](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09), executed.** [Locked-frame clauses 1 and 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) --- *a whole has its own graph with a root node*, and *boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns* --- could not enumerate their dependents at lock time, so this is the re-read that stands in for the set that was never captured.

**It is a re-read, not a reconstruction, and nothing below claims to be that set.** A row that this sweep clears is a row nobody found a problem in today; it is not a row certified independent of the clauses.

**Scope: every open row of [Open decisions](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) and of [the PQ table](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused).** `P4-N`, `PL-N` and the corpus are out unless a finding reaches them. ~~**Tranche A is recorded below; tranches B and C are pending.**~~ ~~**Tranches A and B are recorded below; tranche C is pending.**~~ **All three tranches are recorded below and the sweep is complete: 64 rows read, 36 implicated, 28 clear.**

### The triage, and the one thing it does not mean

**The lock's text entered the file in commit `a3e20a95b`.** A row byte-identical between that commit and now has **not been edited since the clauses existed** --- a fact about the file rather than a guess about attention, which is what makes it a better ordering signal than the dates rows carry in their own prose.

| Tranche | Rows | |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A --- not edited since the lock landed** | 27 (16 AB, 11 PQ) | Swept below |
| **B --- edited since, but not against these clauses** | ~~25~~ **24** | Swept below. **The headline was one high against its own enumeration**; the three tranches sum to 64, which is the whole scope, so nothing was missed |
| **C --- opened after the lock** | 13 | Swept below. ~~**Lowest yield, not zero:** AB-33's precedent is a row *malformed at birth*, which no staleness signal catches~~ **Highest yield of the three, 9 of 13** --- the *not zero* hedge was right and the *lowest* headline was backwards; see [the process finding](#the-process-finding-byte-identity-ordered-the-read-backwards) |

**One confound measured rather than assumed:** the 2026-08-10 clause-reference correction pass (the convention at the head of this file) edited many rows for hygiene alone. Normalizing construct prefixes away before comparing moves exactly **one** row (PQ-7) across the tranche boundary, so the confound is real and small.

**And the limit worth stating, because the tranche name overstates itself.** `a3e20a95b` was a batch commit carrying P3-era content alongside the lock, so **a tranche-A row may already be fully port-aware** --- AB-26, AB-31, AB-34, AB-36 and most of the PQ table are. *Not edited since the lock landed* is not *written before ports existed*. The tranche orders the reading; it predicts nothing about what is found.

### Tranche A: eleven rows clear, sixteen implicated

**Clear: AB-10, AB-14, AB-20, AB-22, AB-23, AB-24, PQ-2, PQ-3, PQ-6, PQ-10, PQ-11.** Recorded as a count with names rather than silently, on the same reasoning the whole/part sweep gave: it is what makes the rest specific.

**Two findings are structural and account for most of the rest; the remainder are per-row.**

#### Finding 1: "parts on different hosts" is a pre-lock premise, and one row is built entirely on it

**Under locked-frame clause 1 a whole's parts are nodes in *its* graph.** They do not sit on different hosts; the *whole* has ports into different hosts. **AB-26 and AB-27 are both framed on the opposite premise** --- AB-26's title asks *how do parts on different hosts connect*, AB-27 divides the world into parts-sharing-a-host and parts-on-different-hosts.

**AB-26 survives the correction; AB-27 largely does not.** AB-26 already found its answer (*part-connections become sibling links inside the whole's own graph*) and keeps a real open question in [PQ-8](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) --- only its framing is stale. **AB-27's central claim is the framing.** Its "genuinely new edge kind is required exactly and only where the current model already refuses to go" locates a joint at the host boundary, and locked-frame clause 1 moves every part off that boundary: `applyStepSequenceCore`'s throw-on-host-mismatch never fires for parts of one whole, because they share the whole's graph. Its closing question --- *when a co-located assembly is pulled apart across hosts, does a same-host relational edge become an adjacency-mediated connection* --- describes an event that does not occur; [C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in) already established there is no whole-migration under ports, only a port whose egress changes. **What survives is one narrower question:** whether the sibling links inside a whole reuse the shipped intra-host relational vocabulary or need their own kind.

**PQ-12 carries the same residue at operation scale.** *Must relocation follow adjacency* is posed as a constraint on **a part relocating to a host**; the operation C9 actually describes is **a port's egress being rewritten**. That is not only vocabulary: an egress is a boundary fact the interior owns, so the constraint is **locally enforceable inside the whole** --- materially stronger than this row's *rule versus the caller's business* fork, which assumed the enforcement point was outside.

#### Finding 2: port identity is a boundary fact, and three rows treat it as an interior one

**[What a port number is not](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) forecloses reading a port as a name for the node behind it.** Three tranche-A rows still do, in three different registers:

| Row | The residue |
| --- | --- |
| **PQ-4** | Its second candidate --- *key ports by what they bind rather than by ordinal* --- makes port identity a function of the interior node, which is the foreclosed reading as an allocation scheme. **And the argument against it already exists**: [PQ-9](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) reached exactly this conclusion on 2026-08-09 about *naming* (`thread:skein` re-creates the misreading) and it was never carried the two rows across to PQ-4, where it bears on a live candidate rather than a lean |
| **PQ-5** | Answers *yes* to **"is a port itself addressable as a part"** --- correct in substance (an exterior edge may terminate on a port, and chaining is the fractal working), stated in the one phrase the negative statement exists to prevent. A port is addressable **as an endpoint**; it is not a part |
| **AB-21** | *"Worth resolving with this row rather than separately, since the obligation is identical and only the scale differs."* **The obligation is not identical.** The north wall needs a *nominal* identity so two descriptions reach one thing (`stableKey`'s problem). A port needs an *allocation* discipline so one slot is not silently reused. They rhyme; their fixes do not, and merging them is what makes PQ-4's key-by-binding candidate look natural |

#### Per-row findings

| Row | What locked-frame clause 1 or 2 does to it |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-13** | **Two problems, opposite in kind.** *Already answered:* the row asks whether multiple parents are permitted; [H3 clause 6](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) states membership **is** a DAG as a hard requirement forced by [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer). *Written against a withdrawn model:* its one taken decision --- *[part-mediated reference](#part-mediated-reference-how-h1-answers-its-own-replacement-trigger)'s parent index should be plural from the start* --- names H1 machinery. **AB-13 was not among the six `H1-dependent` rows [rolled back](#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07)**, so the decision was never re-homed. **The insight survives the loss of its carrier and should move rather than go:** widening a singular field late is the expensive order, and under locked-frame clause 2 the field it now applies to is the **port list**, which is plural by construction --- so the decision is *discharged* by the design rather than merely relocated |
| **AB-19** | **Half answered, and the half is the structural one.** The row asks what a recognized abstraction must carry so an LLM step over it is *bounded*. Locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 answer the **extent**: the packet is one level of interior graph, and its boundary is the port set --- which is [H3 clause 4](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice)'s grounding invariance read from the reasoning side. **What stays open is the content** (what annotation, in what vocabulary), which is the row's live half and is untouched |
| **AB-31** | Its concrete write sketch --- *mint a second whole, repartition the member set, rewrite the parent pointers of the moved parts* --- is scoped **"under H1"** in its own words and was never restated after the withdrawal, and AB-31 was not in the rollback set either. Under locked-frame clause 1 fission is *split one graph into two, each with a root, and re-bind ports*; there are no parent pointers. **The row's corrected port findings sit on top of an uncorrected mechanism sketch**, which is the more misleading of the two states |
| **AB-34** | **Status cell stale on three counts, and it is the row the whole PQ table hangs from.** (i) *"Provisionally answered by P3"* --- P3's two shape claims are **locked**, and only its calibration is provisional. (ii) *"PQ-6/PQ-7 blocking"* --- **PQ-6** deflated on 2026-08-06 (its own cell reads *no longer the blocking item*) and **PQ-7** was restated to not-open the same day. (iii) *"nine sub-questions"* --- there are fourteen |
| **AB-35** | ***The one finding here that is new rather than stale, and it is the sharpest.*** `positionCache` is specified as *a denormalized read structure holding expanded graphs across multiple host nodes*. **An expansion that crosses port boundaries is precisely the thing locked-frame clause 2 buys encapsulation to prevent, arriving as a performance measure rather than as a decision** --- and the lock's own recorded risk is that *"external code cannot see what scale a thing is at ... the failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained."* **A flattening cache is how that happens silently.** None of this row's open sub-questions asks it. **The question to add: does expansion stop at a port, and if it does not, what preserves the caller's knowledge that it crossed one?** **Closed in candidate form 2026-08-12 by [P6](#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) --- and the sweep's framing of it was right in substance but wrong about which half gives.** Expansion **does not** stop at a port; what changes is the *deliverable*. A reference-location hit returns **a handle, not a subgraph**, so the flattening stays inside an index whose only output is a pointer into unflattened truth, and no caller is ever handed a crossed-port structure to conclude from. **This is the sweep's single strongest vindication so far:** the finding was recorded as a question with no answer in view, and the answer that arrived came from re-justifying the construct rather than from patching the objection |
| **PQ-1** | **Its two halves have different standing and the row states one verdict for both.** *Which side is authoritative* is **locked** --- locked-frame clause 2 says the binding is the one **the interior owns**, and the row's *interior egress list authoritative* is that clause, not an independent settlement. *Where the ingress denormalization lives* is genuinely provisional and rests on **AB-35**. The [exclusion list](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-is-not-locked-and-must-not-ride-in-on-this) calls *PQ-1's record split* **the least locked part of ports** without drawing the line |
| **PQ-7** | **Points at a dead construct.** Its residue is *"logged against H1 clause 4"*, and H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07. Worse, its reasoning is conditional on that hypothesis: the exposure exists *"under single-host at an Area + ports"* and **never under multi-host presence + ports**, which is the assumption ports were designed under. **So the question may be moot rather than homeless --- but nobody has said which**, and the underlying question is first-order for locked-frame clause 2: *does interior-owned encapsulation cost the shipped `RelationalEdgeStillReferencedError` guarantee?* It should be answered or retired explicitly, not left restated-and-parked on a withdrawn clause |

### The finding that is about the lock rather than a row: locked-frame clause 2 does not state its direction

**Locked-frame clause 2 reads:** *"Boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns --- not by direct addressing of interior nodes from outside."* **The headline is direction-neutral (*boundary crossings*); the gloss names only the inward case.** Nothing says which is the clause.

**[PQ-8](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) sits exactly in the gap, and it is not a hypothetical.** A sibling link **inside** the rope carries a label naming an Exit **outside** it. The row reasons that this is *"arguably fine (an Exit is an edge, not a graph node, so it is not a port case)"* --- which is a defence against the **gloss**. Against the **headline** it is an unmediated boundary crossing and locked-frame clause 2 forbids it.

~~**Four rows depend on which reading holds**~~ ~~**Five, corrected in tranche B**~~ **Seven, corrected again in tranche C, which also adds the first outward-reading members and the first consumer that sequences work on the answer --- see [Finding 6](#finding-6-the-clause-2-direction-cluster-reaches-seven-and-one-member-sequences-work-on-the-answer)** --- PQ-8, AB-26's licensing labels, AB-28's floor-hole repair, AB-36's second surviving consumer, and **AB-29**, which is the cluster's origin rather than a fifth peer (AB-36 was split out of AB-29's gap (1)); it was missed here only because AB-29 sorts to tranche B --- and they form one cluster: **reverse discovery from an exterior relation to an interior dependent.** Under the strict reading, that discovery is illegal and needs a mediating construct nobody has proposed. Under the loose reading it is merely *harder*, which is what AB-26 and PQ-8 already record.

**[AB-57](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) is a candidate eighth member, deliberately *not* counted, 2026-08-20.** The count above stays at seven. **The cluster is defined narrowly** --- *reverse discovery from an exterior relation to an interior dependent* --- and AB-57 is not that: it asks whether an **exterior** structure should mirror an **interior** crossing, which is the same boundary and the opposite traffic. **Recorded here rather than folded in, for two reasons.** **(i) It bears on the direction question directly.** Under the **gloss** (inward addressing only) a host-level crossing record is untouched; under the **headline** it is a boundary crossing that the *exterior* would own, which inverts *the binding the interior owns* rather than violating it --- **a third case the clause's two readings do not obviously cover**, and the first one where stating the direction may not be sufficient to settle it. **(ii) Bumping the count would have hidden that.** Seven members share a shape and one answer disposes of them; an eighth with a different shape is evidence the clause needs its **direction** stated *and* its **ownership** stated, which is more than tranche A asked for. Whether that is one request or two is itself open. **The lean is unchanged** --- the gloss is probably the clause --- but this member does not follow from that lean.

**This does not meet [the re-open bar](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause) and is not offered as meeting it.** No corpus case is unrepresentable and locked-frame clause 2's encapsulation payoff is untouched. **It is a request to state the direction**, which is cheaper now than after something is built against the reading nobody chose. **Note which way the evidence leans:** the payoff [argued for clause 2](#the-strongest-argument-for-ports-is-not-the-traversal-one) is that *interior repartitioning stops being externally breaking*, which is entirely about inward addressing --- so the gloss is probably the clause and the headline is probably over-broad. That is a reading, not a decision.

### The process finding: a row that corrects another row records the correction on itself

**Three instances in one tranche, all the same shape.** PQ-9 settled an argument in 2026-08-09 that bears on **PQ-4**'s live candidate, and PQ-4 does not know. AB-26 observed that locked-frame clause 2 buries AB-28's dependent link a level deeper, and **AB-28** does not know. AB-36 recorded that the port list demotes two of its four consumers, and **AB-34** still names the demoted pair as blocking.

**This is the [dependent-set discipline](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09)'s gap, one tier down.** That section fixed the failure for **locks** --- enumerate dependents at the lock, where the knowledge exists. **Ordinary rows correct each other constantly and have no equivalent**, so a correction is recorded where it was *discovered* and the corrected row goes on being read as written. Every instance above was cheap to fix at the moment of discovery and expensive to find afterwards, which is the same economics that argued for enumeration at lock time.

**Not proposed as a new discipline here, because the cost profile differs.** A lock is a rare, deliberate event that can afford a ceremony; row-to-row correction is continuous, and requiring a back-reference on every one would tax the ordinary case to catch the occasional one. **Recorded as a measured rate --- three in twenty-seven rows --- so a second tranche can confirm or deflate it before anything is built on it.**

### Tranche B: thirteen rows clear, eleven implicated

**Clear: AB-0, AB-1, AB-2, AB-3, AB-4, AB-7, AB-15, AB-16, AB-18, AB-30, AB-32, AB-33, AB-37.** Several are clear *because* they were written against the clauses --- AB-2, AB-3 and AB-33 were closed by [locked-frame clause 3's dependent set](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09), and AB-16 cites [what a port number is not](#what-a-port-number-is-not-added-2026-08-06) directly.

**Coverage correction, recorded rather than fixed silently: this tranche is 24 rows, not the 25 the triage table claims.** The headline count disagrees with its own enumeration (`AB-0…9, 11, 12, 15, 16, 17, 18, 25, 29, 30, 32, 33, 37; PQ-9, 13` is 22 + 2). The three tranches sum to 64 = 49 AB + 14 distinct PQ + PL-1, which is the whole scope, so **nothing was missed --- one headline was one high.** Corrected in the table above.

**And the prediction this tranche was ordered on was wrong in an instructive way.** The triage reasoned B would yield less than A because its rows had been under the author's eye since the lock. Per row it did, slightly (11 of 24 against 16 of 27) --- but **the composition is almost entirely different, and byte-identity does not order it at all.** Only two findings below are clause staleness. The rest are **propagation failures**: a decision landed in another row, *after* the lock, and never crossed. A row being actively edited is not evidence it is current; it is evidence it is the kind of row other rows get corrected against.

#### Finding 3: mint transfers members across a boundary the shipped code can only sever, carry, or defer

**AB-5 resolves minting as *interpose a level between a host A and a subset B of its members*, compiling to an ordinary `transferMembership` (`entityIds` = B, `fromHostIds` = {A}, `toHostId` = the new whole) plus an `establishRelation` carrying `PartOf`. Two writes are named. A third is required and no row names it.** Under locked-frame clause 2, the moment B's members become nodes in W's graph, every pre-existing relational edge from a member of B to a **non**-member is a boundary crossing, and must become **a port W owns**. AB-5's *"everything involved already exists ... the only genuinely new entity is the whole's own identity"* is true of the nodes and false of the edges.

**This is not a gap that surfaces later --- the shipped code already decides it, the wrong way, through a closed union.** [`boundaryEdgeOutcomes`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts) selects exactly the edges with one endpoint in the transfer set, and `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer` returns `'dissolve' | 'carry' | 'defer'`. **There is no fourth outcome, and two of the three are actively wrong here:**

- **`dissolve`** --- recognizing a snare trap whose rope is `Against` a tree **unties the rope**, as a committed write, because the tree is not in B.
- **`carry`** --- a lantern `On` the snare's stick is **absorbed into the transfer set**, so a non-member silently becomes part of the whole. `carry` exists to keep a load with its bearer during an ordinary move; under mint it is a membership decision made by an edge kind.

**Two rows inherit this and one of them states the contradiction outright.** **AB-11** gives the transfer/establish split precisely and concludes *the membership half genuinely does not grow the step vocabulary* --- true of the members, silent about their edges. **AB-17** takes the stronger form: establish and dissolve are ***exact inverses***, *"A and B swapped in role."* **As described they are not.** Dissolve is explicitly port-aware --- AB-5 derives its plan-time footprint from *"a whole's port list, which enumerates every host referencing it"* --- and establish mints no ports at all. **An operation and its inverse cannot disagree about whether ports exist**, and that asymmetry is load-bearing for AB-17, whose ratchet worry is declared dissolved *by* the inverse claim.

**What is owed is small and specific, not a re-argument.** One clause in AB-5's op shape (the mint's third write binds each boundary edge to a port on W) and a fourth outcome in the classifier. **It also re-weights AB-5's parked verifications:** `establishRelation` carrying `PartOf` is a capability that must be *added*, with no rival answer, which is why parking it was correct on AB-40(a)'s precedent. Port-minting is a shipped construct that must **change**, which is a different class --- and `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer`'s union is exhaustive by type, so the change is caught by the compiler rather than discovered in play. **That is the one piece of good news here:** the design gap is real, but the code will not let it be silent.

#### Finding 4: the H1 rollback is still under-covering, three days on

**AB-25's Status cell reads *"still H1's only live falsification trigger"*, and its body says the same twice.** [H1 was withdrawn 2026-08-07](#rollback-mechanics-executed-2026-08-07). There is nothing left to falsify, and the row's actual content --- is Room/Area containment a tree, now spanning two node kinds of different provenance --- never needed H1 and survives its withdrawal unchanged. **The row is not wrong; its standing is.** A row carrying the initiative's last live falsification test reads as urgent, and this one has been reading that way for three days while owning an ordinary open question.

**This is the fourth instance of one failure, and the count is the finding rather than the row.** The rollback re-homed six rows tagged `H1-dependent`. Tranche A found **AB-13** and **AB-31** carrying live H1 machinery outside that set and **PQ-7** parked on a withdrawn clause; AB-25 is the fourth. **The rollback's scope was the tag, and the tag recorded what each row's author noticed at writing time** --- so it caught rows that *declared* a dependency and missed rows that merely *had* one. **AB-25 is the sharpest case precisely because it was H1's designated test.** A falsifier is a dependency of the strongest kind and the least likely to carry the tag, since the tag was tracking rows that would need *re-deriving*, and a test needs *retiring*.

**Mechanically closable, and worth doing once rather than a row at a time.** Fifteen rows across the two tables still mention `H1`; most legitimately, as history or as a superseded note. **The check is not "does it mention H1" but "does it assert something in the present tense about H1"**, which is a bounded read of fifteen cells. Not done here --- the four known instances are recorded, and tranche C will cover the rows opened after the withdrawal, where the same reference should not appear at all.

#### Per-row findings

| Row | What the clauses, or a decision that never crossed, do to it |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-6** | **Half its remaining open scope is discharged by locked-frame clause 1.** The row's Status is *"Open only on the drift-repair story for the stored half and the settle-state vocabulary for the derived half."* **There is no drift to repair.** Under locked-frame clause 1 plus **AB-4**'s `PartOf` resolution the member set **is** the `PartOf` edges of the whole's own graph --- one representation, not a stored set standing beside a graph that could disagree with it. Trim-to-valid (hook 5) is likewise structural: a destroyed part takes its edge with it. **What survives is not drift but *decay policy*** --- does a whole with one member remain a whole --- which is a smaller and different question, and belongs to AB-17's reabsorption criterion rather than here |
| **AB-8** | **Framing (b) has been absorbed and the row still lists three live framings.** *(b) the abstraction is itself a manipulation host above Room* splits under the lock: **its host half is now true of every framing** --- locked-frame clause 3 makes every host a node in its own graph, so *is the whole a host* stopped discriminating --- and **its ladder half (*above Room*) is AB-12(b)**, which is on the lock's own exclusion list and is being decided on AB-30's evidence, not on AB-8's. **This sharpens what [PQ-7's sign-off](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) recorded 2026-08-10**, which bounded AB-8 as *"still open between (a)-with-ports and (b)"*: (b) is not an AB-8 fork either. With (c) already recorded as a modeller's option rather than a rival, **AB-8 has one framing left**, and its Status should say so or say why not |
| **AB-9** | ***Correct in conclusion, wrong in its stated ground, and the ground is the part being carried forward.*** The row's *"substantially deflated 2026-08-06 by P2 clause 3"* rests on *"the rope is one node and its ends are endpoint qualifiers."* **AB-4 recorded on 2026-08-09 that P2 retracted exactly that phrasing** --- [the very next section](#where-p2-clause-3-risks-recreating-the-very-defect-it-fixes) reads *"`rope: rope-end` is an **address into the rope's own graph**"* --- and locked-frame clause 1 settles it: **the ends are nodes.** **The deflation survives on a different ground:** they are nodes in the *rope's* graph, so the **room's** membership layer still holds one node and still has nothing to select between. **But one clause of it is now false.** C5's permanent multi-level steady state is *not* *"an artifact of that same constraint rather than a fact about anchored things"* --- under locked-frame clause 1 it is exactly a fact about anchored things; what encapsulation removes is its visibility at room scale. **The practical consequence is a status change, not a re-argument:** the Status cell owes *"re-weighing under P2"*, and the re-weighing is owed under the locked frame instead, comes out the same, and is therefore **dischargeable now** |
| **AB-12** | **The room-nesting park prices apprehension and not addressing --- the weakest finding here, and recorded as a question rather than a defect.** A nook minted as a Feature `PartOf` the room is, by this row's own argument, *"structurally identical"* to a Room; under locked-frame clause 3 it has a graph and a root, and under locked-frame clause 2 **anything placed in it is behind a port from the room's graph.** The park's stated unmodelled cost is perception gating (*who can hear this*), and its three keep-the-exit-cheap constraints are about nesting rather than crossing. **So the question the park does not ask: when the ballroom is described, does the lemonade table's contents list come back?** Either nook contents live in the nook's graph and the room-level read must cross a port to see them, or they live in the room's graph and *"the schema may store it"* is weaker than it sounds. **Both are answerable; neither is answered**, and the row's own **no-silent-omission** obligation is the reason it matters --- a nook that quietly swallows its contents from room-scale description is the failure that rule exists to catch |
| **AB-29** | **A fifth member of [the clause-2 direction cluster](#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction), and arguably its origin.** Gap (1) is stated in the cluster's exact terms --- *"remove a **relation**, find the part-connections referencing it ... a reverse lookup from a relation to its dependents, and nothing indexes it"* --- with the part-connections **inside** wholes and the licensing relation **outside**. AB-36 was split out of this very gap, so the cluster's other members inherit from AB-29 rather than sitting beside it. Tranche A missed it only because AB-29 is a tranche-B row. **Also carries [Finding 1](#finding-1-parts-on-different-hosts-is-a-pre-lock-premise-and-one-row-is-built-entirely-on-it)'s residue at footprint scale:** gap (2) locks *"Room B (the rope's far end)"*, which sites a part in a room. Under the lock the footprint is the rope's own graph plus each **egress host**, read off the port list --- the same correction, and here it makes the footprint *more* computable rather than less |
| **PQ-9** | **Its numbered-vs-named axis gained a third option yesterday and does not know.** [PQ-4](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s compact-pseudo-random-id candidate (2026-08-10) is neither numbered nor named, and **it satisfies PQ-9's own argument more strongly than the option PQ-9 leans to**: if the hazard is that an identifier invites being read as the interior node, an opaque id makes the reading *unavailable* where an ordinal merely makes it wrong. The two rows also record the **same** residual cost --- authored ports (the lantern with a hook) want names --- from opposite directions without either noticing. **Flagged with its provenance: this is not a clause finding.** PQ-4's candidate was written by tranche A of this sweep, so this is an obligation this pass created, and recording it here rather than leaving it is the point of [tranche A's own process finding](#the-process-finding-a-row-that-corrects-another-row-records-the-correction-on-itself) |
| **PQ-13** | **Its third option was priced *cheap* and stopped being cheap on 2026-08-10.** *"The port is implied for undecomposed objects and materialized only on first decomposition"* was charged one cost: *"presence means two different things depending on a state nobody declared."* **AB-9's committed carve-out gives that state a trigger** --- a perception command actively seeking detail may mint the level it seeks --- so the undeclared state is now **changed by looking at things**, on the hottest read-shaped path in the game, for the commonest object in it. **The uniformity option gains from the same source:** AB-5's mint is *interpose a level between A and B*, which wants the lantern already addressable as a whole-in-waiting rather than converted into one. **Not a clause finding either** --- locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 are silent on a partless object, since a thing with no interior has no boundary to mediate. What moved is the price, and the row's recommendation (*take the uniformity, with the hot-path cost measured*) is now better supported than when it was written |

### The process finding: tranche A's measured rate is confirmed, not deflated

**Tranche A recorded three instances in twenty-seven rows of *a row that corrects another row records the correction on itself*, and explicitly deferred acting on it: *"recorded as a measured rate ... so a second tranche can confirm or deflate it before anything is built on it."*** **Tranche B has four in twenty-four** --- AB-4's retraction never reaching **AB-9**, locked-frame clause 1 plus AB-4 never reaching **AB-6**, AB-12(b)'s split never reaching **AB-8**, and AB-5/AB-9's same-day decisions never reaching **PQ-13**. **11% and 17%, which is one rate, not two.**

**What the second measurement adds is the shape, and it is worse than the rate suggests.** Tranche A's three instances were spread over days. **Three of tranche B's four point at decisions taken on 2026-08-09 and 2026-08-10** --- the two most productive days in this plan's history. **The failure is not decay; it is concurrency.** A day that closes six rows creates six chances for a seventh row to go stale, and the rows most likely to be missed are the ones *not* being edited that day --- which is precisely the population byte-identity triage would have sent to tranche A, except that these rows were edited recently for other reasons and were therefore sorted here.

**That is the ordering signal's real limit, and it is worth stating plainly next to the triage that opened this sweep.** *Not edited since the lock* finds rows written against a superseded model. **It cannot find a row that was edited yesterday and corrected by a different row this morning**, and on the evidence of two tranches that second population is the larger one.

**Still not proposed as a new discipline**, on tranche A's own cost reasoning --- but the measurement is now firm enough to name what a discipline would have to catch: **not every row-to-row correction, only corrections taken on a day when several rows close at once.** That is a much smaller surface than the general case, and it is the one a batch of same-day decisions could be swept against at the end of the day, while the knowledge is still present --- which is [the dependent-set discipline](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locks-need-a-dependent-set-too-2026-08-09)'s argument, applied to a decision batch instead of a lock.

### Tranche C: four rows clear, nine implicated

**Clear: AB-38, AB-43, AB-45, AB-46.** AB-38 is a naming question about triggers, AB-43 a keying question with no boundary in it, and AB-45 and AB-46 both attach their content to a whole or to a record it carries --- which is what locked-frame clauses 1 and 2 would ask for.

**The prediction this tranche was ordered on is not merely wrong, it is inverted.** The triage called C *lowest yield, not zero*. Measured across the three: **A 16 of 27, B 11 of 24, C 9 of 13.** The tranche predicted lowest is the highest by a wide margin, which is the sweep's own evidence about its ordering signal --- recorded in [the process finding](#the-process-finding-byte-identity-ordered-the-read-backwards) below rather than here.

**And the check tranche C was asked to run passes cleanly.** [Finding 4](#finding-4-the-h1-rollback-is-still-under-covering-three-days-on) left one edge open: rows opened *after* the withdrawal should not reference `H1` at all. **Exactly one of the thirteen does** --- PQ-14, comparing what re-opening single-use costs against *"re-opening H1's clause 1b, where there **was** an argument to defeat"* --- and it is historical and comparative rather than a present-tense claim, so it passes the bounded check that finding proposed. **The rollback's under-coverage is therefore bounded to rows opened before 2026-08-07**: four known instances, none after.

#### Finding 5: AB-48's two containment kinds are `carry` in both directions, and one of those directions absorbs the container

**[AB-48](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) resolved on 2026-08-09 that `In` and `PartOf` join `HostRelationalEdgeKind`, and booked the implementation obligation with its answer already filled in: *"each new kind must declare its transfer outcome (both are unconditional `carry`)."*** **Unconditional is the one thing a transfer outcome cannot be**, and the shipped code says so in its own comment.

**`carry` is direction-asymmetric by construction, which is why every shipped kind that has a carry branch also has a `movedRole` branch.** In [`interactionUnderTransfer.ts`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/ludicGraph/expandValidate/interactionUnderTransfer.ts), `computeCarryClosure` resolves `otherId = movedRole === 'subject' ? edge.to : edge.from` and a `carry` verdict **adds `otherId` to the transfer set**. A containment edge runs contained-thing to container, so:

- **Move the container.** Target moved, `otherId` is the contained thing, and it comes along. Correct, and it is the case the resolution was thinking of.
- **Move the contained thing.** Subject moved, `otherId` is **the container**, and *the crate is absorbed into the transfer set and travels with the treasure taken out of it*. Under `PartOf`, taking the fork from the `PlaceSetting` drags the `PlaceSetting` with it.

**The breaking case is not hypothetical --- it is [AB-40](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)(a), and it is the one operation the new kinds exist to support.** *Taking and returning the fork* is the corpus case AB-40 was opened from; a containment vocabulary that gets the take direction backwards has failed at exactly the move that motivated it.

**The file already states the rule that was skipped:** *"`carry` only exists where there is an actual object to absorb (a load relation, `On`)."* A containment relation moved by its subject has nothing to absorb --- it has a relation to **end** or to **defer**, which is what `On`'s own subject branch does (`dissolve`, the lantern lifted off the stick).

**And the correct answers plausibly differ between the two kinds, which is the substantive cost of collapsing them.** `In` reads as `dissolve`: taking treasure out of a crate ends the containment, with nothing owed. `PartOf` reads as `defer`: a part leaving a whole is [AB-17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s reabsorption question and [AB-6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s member-set upkeep, not a silent amputation --- and AB-40(b) has already decided that the whole must *notice*. **So *both are unconditional `carry`* flattens two distinct answers into one wrong one**, and it does so on the two kinds AB-48 argued at length are not the same kind.

**Same good news as [Finding 3](#finding-3-mint-transfers-members-across-a-boundary-the-shipped-code-can-only-sever-carry-or-defer), and the same limit.** `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer` is an exhaustive switch, so the compiler will insist a branch is written for each new kind. **It cannot insist the branch is right**, and this row supplies the branch in advance, which is how a wrong answer gets past a check designed to catch a missing one.

#### Finding 6: the clause-2 direction cluster reaches seven, and one member sequences work on the answer

**[Locked-frame clause 2's unstated direction](#the-finding-that-is-about-the-lock-rather-than-a-row-locked-frame-clause-2-does-not-state-its-direction) was raised in tranche A as a request and corrected to five members in tranche B. PL-1 and PQ-14 are the sixth and seventh, and they change its standing.** Tranche A's argument was that stating the direction is *"cheaper now than after something is built against the reading nobody chose."* **Both conditions have now arrived.**

**PL-1 is the interior reading its ancestors.** *Of all the positive and negative facts held by containing abstractions higher in the fractal, which are relevant to improvisation at a local level* is an outward read across a boundary, in the opposite direction from every other cluster member. Under locked-frame clause 2's **gloss** (*not by direct addressing of interior nodes from outside*) it is untouched. Under its **headline** (*boundary crossings are mediated by an explicit binding the interior owns*) an unmediated read of a container's contents is a boundary crossing and is forbidden --- and if it is forbidden, the whole candidate list changes shape, because *copy-at-import* stops being one option among seven and becomes the only legal family.

**And PL-1 is not parked.** [Iteration 1 is an elected Prototype](#pl-1-iteration-1-brute-force-relevance-classified-as-a-prototype-2026-08-09) whose naive form is LLM judgment at mint time over a packet of ancestor facts --- which is the built thing tranche A hoped to get ahead of.

**PQ-14 is the sharper case, because the dependency is on its *sequencing* rather than its content.** The row instructs *decide (ii) before (i)*, on the reasoning that a modelling convention which dissolves the pressure is cheaper than re-opening a locked property. **But (ii) *is* an outward read:** create the `Shown` edge only at `AREA#Boat`'s root, and let a reasoning step asked about the bridge specifically pay *"one traversal hop"* --- the bridge's interior reaching its container's root for a fact. **Under the strict reading the cheap answer is illegal, (ii) is unavailable, and the sequencing inverts** --- (i), the lifecycle-sharing relaxation of single-use, becomes the only route, and it is the expensive one the order was designed to avoid.

**The evidence still leans the way tranche A said it did**, so both rows probably survive: the payoff [argued for locked-frame clause 2](#the-strongest-argument-for-ports-is-not-the-traversal-one) is entirely about inward addressing. **What has changed is that *probably* is now load-bearing** --- it sequences PQ-14's work and it sits under a Prototype with code. **This still does not meet [the re-open bar](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause) and is still not offered as meeting it.** It is the same request, with the cost of not answering it now visible.

#### Per-row findings

| Row | What the clauses, or a decision that never crossed, do to it |
| --- | --- |
| **AB-39** | ***Written in the pre-lock vocabulary the day after the lock, which is the malformed-at-birth shape the triage predicted for this tranche.*** The row reasons that *is the door powered* is *"reachability through intact edges"* which something must run *"over some version of **the** graph."* **Under locked-frame clause 1 there is no *the* graph** --- the cut cable and the door may sit at different scales, and a causal path crossing a whole's boundary is a path through a **port**. That bears directly on the row's own open sub-question *what is the evaluation unit (a system? a path? a query?)*: ports bound it, and the port list is the reverse index a causal walk would consult. **This row has no port, H3 or PQ dependency at all**, alone among the rows that reason over paths. **It also splits C12's finding 3 in two.** *Whether an unreachable address returns **unknown** rather than the whole's answer* now has two unreachables: **not connected**, which is what C12 argued and where coarsening is unsound; and **not addressable**, where returning the whole's answer is not coarsening at all but locked-frame clause 2's encapsulation working as designed. **They must not get the same answer**, and as written the row cannot tell them apart |
| **AB-40** | **(a) is locked-frame clause 2's forbidden sentence, written out in full and parked as an implementation item.** *"Whether the existing transfer step can address a node inside another object's interior graph as a source or destination at all"* is *direct addressing of interior nodes from outside*, verbatim. **The complication is that locked-frame clause 2's scope over *mutation* is unstated:** [H3 clause 5](#h3-ports-as-the-boundary-mechanism-decomposition-as-a-modellers-choice) binds **relation kinds** crossing a boundary, and a transfer is not a relation --- so either (a) is already answered *no*, or it is the case that forces the clause's scope. **Either way it is not what the row moved it to [Parked extension points](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark) as.** *Scoped, known-needed, unbuilt* books a capability whose behaviour is agreed and whose code is missing; here the behaviour is what is missing, and parking it hides a design fork inside a schedule. **It is also [Finding 5](#finding-5-ab-48s-two-containment-kinds-are-carry-in-both-directions-and-one-of-those-directions-absorbs-the-container)'s breaking case**, so this row is now load-bearing in two directions at once |
| **AB-41** | **Inherits [Finding 3](#finding-3-mint-transfers-members-across-a-boundary-the-shipped-code-can-only-sever-carry-or-defer), and it is the first slice that will be built.** An ephemeral whole is *"an H3-shaped whole (root, parts, **ports**)"* minted around objects that already exist, so under locked-frame clause 2 its boundary needs port bindings exactly as a durable one's does --- and the row names `boundaryEdgeOutcomes` (`dissolve`/`carry`/`defer`) as the mechanism that handles absorption, which is the closed union Finding 3 shows has no port outcome. **Softer than AB-5 in one respect and sharper in another.** *Softer:* nothing is written to the graph, so the ports need not persist. *Sharper:* the ephemeral whole's carry closure **is** its behaviour, so a wrong classification is a wrong committed move rather than a wrong stored shape --- and this row is [Phase 5 candidate (b)](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order), so it is where the classifier's gap is first exercised in code. **The 2026-08-10 Feature guard sits on the same mechanism** and inherits whatever the fourth outcome turns out to be, which is an argument for settling Finding 3 before that slice rather than during it |
| **AB-42** | **Its central candidate says *"nodes in the ludic graph"*, and locked-frame clause 1 makes *which graph* a second question the row does not ask.** It matters, because it **partly prices cost (i)**, which the row records as unpriced: a negative established inside a whole is a node in **that whole's** graph and is invisible to traversal, summary and packet-building at the containing level, so *"every traversal, summary and LLM packet pays for them"* holds only at the negative's own scale. **It does not rescue the row's own example** --- a kitchen's five hundred *there is no X* nodes are in the kitchen's graph, and the kitchen is the level doing the reasoning --- so this is a partial pricing rather than a dissolution, and it narrows the accumulation objection to *negatives established at the scale you are reasoning at*. **Recorded also because this row does the locked-frame clause 2 half explicitly and well** (a negative needs an interior end for a port to bind to); it is the only tranche C row that consults the clauses at all, and it still missed the clause-1 half |
| **AB-44** | **Locked-frame clause 2 offers a discriminator for this row's exact question, and the row's own warning is why it cannot simply be taken.** Inert-versus-load-bearing is posed as a property of the negative. Under locked-frame clause 2, whether anything **outside** the whole can depend on a negative is decided by whether a **port binds it** --- so load-bearing is a *boundary-relative* fact, and a negative may be load-bearing inside its whole and inert beyond it. **That reshapes the question from *are negatives one kind* to *at what scale is this negative load-bearing*, and it reshapes the lifecycle half with it:** an unbound negative is expiry-eligible at the containing level without being expiry-eligible inside, which is a better-behaved answer to [PS-10](#open-questions-p4-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) than a global one. **The row's own warning bites the offer, correctly.** AB-19 rejected deciding consequence *"deterministically rather than by an LLM step"*, and port-binding is structure. **So this is a candidate for the representation question the row actually asks, not for the judgment it forbids** --- the same line the row already draws, needing to be drawn a second time in a place it did not anticipate |
| **AB-47** | **Vocabulary residue, and it is the one place in the file where `root` names two different things in one argument.** The unification case reads *"a licence narrowed at the fractal's **root** ... and since **no level is privileged** a root licence is just a licence."* [Locked-frame clause 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06), added the same day this row settled, makes **root node** mean *the one node of a graph that the graph designates* --- every graph has one, at every scale. AB-47's root is the **outermost whole**, which under locked-frame clause 3's reading is not a root but one root among many. **And the argument borrows a negative statement from the other sense:** locked-frame clause 1's *the root node is not a privileged kind of node* is a claim about node kinds **within** a graph; *no level is privileged* is a claim about scales. They are different claims reaching the same word. **The settled answer does not depend on either**, so this is residue rather than a defect --- but it is residue in the argument recorded **for the side that lost**, which is precisely the text someone re-reads when they re-open the question |

### The process finding: byte-identity ordered the read backwards

**The triage predicted A > B > C and staked the sweep's order on it. The measurement is A 16 of 27, B 11 of 24, C 9 of 13 --- 59%, 46%, 69%.** The tranche called *lowest yield* is the highest, and the two the signal was built to separate are the two that came out closest. **As an ordering signal, byte-identity did not merely underperform; it pointed the wrong way.**

**The mechanism is the one [tranche B named](#the-process-finding-tranche-as-measured-rate-is-confirmed-not-deflated), arriving with more force.** C's rows were born inside the frame --- but **eight of the thirteen were opened on 2026-08-09 and 2026-08-10**, the same two-day burst that produced three of tranche B's four correction failures, and the frame they were born inside was **still being written**: locked-frame clause 3 landed on 2026-08-09 and made *root* and *usable as an endpoint* normative after several of these rows had already used those words. **Being born inside the frame protects a row only if the frame is the vocabulary its author is already thinking in**, and a clause less than a day old is not that yet. AB-47 is the clean demonstration --- it settled on 2026-08-09 and uses `root` in the sense locked-frame clause 3 retired that same day.

**One thing the triage got right, and it is the reason it hedged.** *Lowest yield, **not zero** --- AB-33's precedent is a row malformed at birth, which no staleness signal catches.* **AB-39 is a second instance and a better one**: opened one day after the lock, reasoning over *the* graph as though locked-frame clause 1 had not been written. The hedge was correct and the headline it hedged was not.

**What actually predicted a finding, in all three tranches, was proximity to a day on which several decisions closed at once.** That is not derivable from the file's commit history --- it is a fact about the *rate* of decision, available at the time and lost afterwards --- which is why it belongs in the discipline tranche B's process finding was circling (*sweep a batch of same-day decisions at the end of the day, while the knowledge is present*) and not in a triage computed from diffs. **The triage's own disclaimer stands and should be read as the whole of its value:** *"it orders the read; it does not bound it."* **Coverage was total, so the wrong order cost nothing but the prediction.**

---

## Proposal P4: settling, dataflow, and narrative heat

**Recorded 2026-08-07 from conversation, and it is the largest piece of design in this initiative that until now existed only there.** ~~**Not promoted to a working hypothesis**, for the reason [candidate H2](#candidate-h2-not-adopted-ab-12s-privileged-level-reframe) gives and [P2](#proposal-p2-separate-the-storage-shard-from-the-containment-relation) and [P3](#provisional-p3-ports-as-host-boundary-bindings) already follow: H1 is still in flight, and two hypotheses make the dependent sets overlap.~~ **That reason no longer holds --- H1 died the same day this was written, and P3 was promoted into H3 for exactly the same reason.**

**Status: accepted 2026-08-07, as a Prototype, not a Proposal or a Working Hypothesis --- deliberately a third category.** See [What P4 being load-bearing here means](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) for why the name matters and not only the label. The heading above is left as **Proposal P4** rather than renamed, per this plan's supersession rule and to keep every existing anchor into this section valid --- read "Proposal" in the heading as historical, not current.

**Provenance, and it predates this initiative by a long way.** The scheme comes from the **Ludic Network** conception worked out when `Variable` and `Compute` elements were abandoned --- *a connected graph-of-graphs of story elements*. The conflict it was built to resolve is the one [C12](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c12-the-moonbase-power-system-and-the-cut-cable) has now reached from the practical side. **Recorded as prior conception rather than as a new idea**, because a design arriving twice by different routes is evidence, and because this plan has twice mistaken a remembered claim for a verified one. **Checked 2026-08-07 for scalability precedent and found silent, not favorable:** `Variable`/`Compute` were abandoned for a *flexibility* reason --- a deterministic JS cascade couldn't address unexpected player input, and the JS itself was a black box no LLM step could sensibly read --- not a scaling one; by the author's own account it scaled fine. **This history neither supports nor undermines P4's scalability**, and should not be read as either.

### What P4 being load-bearing here means (2026-08-07)

**The pattern to watch for is named in H1's own write-up, and it is happening again.** [Trigger 2's conclusion](#h1-single-host-objects-hosting-at-the-containing-node) warned that "conditional adoption goes de facto permanent once the dependent set is large enough that rollback means redoing fifteen answers --- at which point [a hypothesis] survives on accumulated cost rather than merit." Three rows lean on P4: **AB-39** (opened first, "if it falls this question returns intact"), and, added the same day as this section, **AB-40(b)** and **AB-37**, both narrowed by [C13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c13-taking-and-returning-the-fork) toward P4's topology-change/settling answer for whole-summary upkeep.

**Accepted, and accepted as staged --- iteration 1 only, not the full scheme.** [Discussed and agreed 2026-08-07:] P4 is a natural fit for iterative implementation, because the concrete grounding later iterations need (real fan-out, real diamond frequency, real settle time) is discoverable only by running an early one, not by further design-time reasoning. Four iterations sketched, in order: **(1)** a naive everything-at-once cascade, single invocation, committed in **sequential batches rather than one atomic transaction** --- this is what keeps it distinct from the path [the problem table](#the-problem-both-obvious-answers-are-unbounded-and-one-of-them-is-impossible) already rules out as structurally impossible, since each batch still declares its own footprint even though the scheduling across batches is naive. Untenable at scale; workable for a prototype the size of CoyoteGame; buys the partial-completion resilience that makes committing in batches safe at all, which nothing later can skip. **(2)** split into separately scheduled invocations, naive ordering --- this, not iteration 1, is what actually unparks the unshipped `thinking` `claimed`-status worker noted in [Parked extension points](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#parked-extension-points-this-initiative-would-unpark). **(3)** heat-maps, used to influence scheduling. **(4)** decay and re-energize --- the largest *conceptual* jump (lossless eventual consistency becomes a lossy, attention-driven cascade) but, by that point, not a large *implementation* one.

**Only iteration 1 is designed now; only iteration 1 is what the three dependent rows may cite.** AB-40(b) and AB-37 need nothing beyond "a self-maintaining, eventually-consistent cascade exists, with a stated partial-completion guarantee" --- iteration 1's whole content, no heat or decay required. AB-39 is only partly cleared: iteration 1 gives it a concrete "against which snapshot" answer (the last completed batch), but its PS-1/PS-3 sub-questions are about the reachability mechanism, not the cascade's staging, and stay open regardless of how many iterations P4 eventually has. **Tag dependent rows `P4-i1-dependent`, not `P4-dependent`** --- a row citing the full scheme when it only needs iteration 1's guarantee is the same over-tagging error [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) already warns against, one proposal over.

**Why "Prototype" and not "Proposal," and it is not a labeling nicety.** H1's rollback, had it come, cost **design time only** --- reasoning re-done, rows reverted to Open, no code touched, because H1 was never built. **P4-iteration-1 is going to have code behind it before iterations 2--4 are designed.** If it fails, the rollback is not "revert some Status cells" --- it is deciding what happens to shipped code that was known, going in, to be non-scalable. That is a heavier and more dangerous failure mode than H1's, not a different-flavored version of the same one: sunk *engineering* cost pulls harder toward keeping something that isn't working than sunk *reasoning* cost ever did, which is exactly why this needed its own category rather than riding on the Working-hypothesis vocabulary built for a design-only bet.
