**One respect in which this is a *better* prototype bet than P4's, recorded because the two will be compared.** **Iteration 1's output has the same shape as any scaled version's** --- a filtered set of relevant facts --- so iteration 2 is a **drop-in replacement**, where P4's iteration 1→2 changes the scheduling architecture and is a genuine rewrite. **And iteration 2 is already named rather than sketched:** *annotate at mint time, not at reasoning time* is the first row of [the Coyote prior-art table](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#coyote-as-prior-art-bounded-llm-reasoning-that-is-not-bounded-structurally), and precomputing relevance per (ancestor-fact, entity-kind) pair is exactly that pattern applied here. P4's iteration 3 is considerably vaguer.

**Two cautions, recorded now rather than discovered during the build.**

1. **Depth is *not* a problem, contrary to first impression.** CoyoteGame today is two-level, and [depth is the corpus's named blind spot](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#phase-0-corpus) --- but **a prototype that builds abstraction layers creates its own depth**: genre → room → contraption → part is four levels, [C11](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c11-the-rube-goldberg-machine)'s shape. It tests depth by construction.
2. **Volume *is* a problem, and it is the same one P4 has.** Brute force fails on the **number** of restrictions, and Coyote is a small authored world that will not accumulate many. **So iteration 1 validates the *mechanism* and not the *scaling threshold*.** Same limitation P4 iteration 1 already accepts (*workable for a prototype the size of CoyoteGame*), same mitigation: **instrument to extrapolate, not to pass.**

**What cannot be measured, stated because the P4 framing invites skipping past it.** P4 measures countable things --- fan-out, settle time, diamond frequency. **Relevance has no ground truth.** *Was that judgment right* is undecidable for the same reason [there is no wrong fiction](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-a-fault-even-is-given-the-medium-2026-08-09). **The prototype can therefore measure cost and not quality.** The available quality proxy is **contradiction frequency**, which is [already on P4 iteration 1's instrumentation list](#what-p4-being-load-bearing-here-means-2026-08-07) --- **the two prototypes share the measurement rather than building separate machinery**, which is also a reason to build them in the same window.

**Rollback triggers, set now while they cost nothing, and stated on cost and contradiction rate --- never on accuracy.**

1. **If the *relevant fraction* does not fall materially below the total** --- if nearly every ancestor restriction is judged to bear --- **then relevance as a framing failed, not this iteration.** Filtering that filters nothing cannot be rescued by precomputing it, because there is nothing to precompute away. **This is the direct analogue of P4's *fan-out does not compress* trigger**, and it fires against PL-1 itself rather than against candidate 6.
2. **If relevance judgments are unstable** --- the same (ancestor-fact, candidate) pair judged differently across calls --- **iteration 2 is impossible**, since there is nothing stable to cache. That kills the named optimisation path specifically rather than the framing, and it is cheap to measure from day one.

**What this does to P5's status, and it is the point of doing it.** The plan previously recorded that *P5's shape claims stand alone but its utility is gated on relevance --- the initiative's founding unsolved problem*. **That is now weaker in a specific and favourable way: PL-1 is no longer open-ended, it is buildable**, with a candidate elected, an iteration 2 named, and triggers set. **P5 is blocked on a build rather than on an unsolved problem** --- a different and much better position, and the one that makes its shape clauses worth locking ahead of the utility question.

### The cost that is not optional, and it is AB-19's

**If licence is only ever authored, P5 recreates the thing AB-19 rejected.** That row killed the authored-pattern-vocabulary shortcut partly on the objection that it *"limits players to what someone anticipated"* --- and a licence system whose limits all trace to an author is that objection with a new name. **So mechanisms by which licence widens in play are load-bearing for P5's acceptability, not a later refinement.** One is already available and should not be mistaken for the whole answer: **licence governs what the *system* may invent, not what the world may become.** A player who puts a napkin on a closed place setting is not improvising --- the whole gains a member by ordinary mutation, and the licence was never in the path.

## Proposal P6: ludicCache as the attention-scoped reference structure

> **The *semantics of attention* --- what a surfacing event records, what keeps a referent live, and what lets it go --- moved to [`AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md`](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md) and its [corpus](AGENT.attentionHistory.corpus.planning.md) on 2026-08-12.** This section keeps P6's clauses, its place in the design, and the invariant. **PC-1 and PC-2 are handed off** (see the table below); **PC-3 and PC-4 stay here.** Do not settle a question about *when a referent stops being referrable* in this document.

**Opened 2026-08-12, from conversation. It is the first positive content AB-35 has had**, and the reason that row sat at *not started* for six days while everything around it moved is worth stating: [P2 clause 2](#the-proposal-in-three-clauses) justified the cache **entirely by latency** --- *a denormalized read structure holding expanded graphs across multiple host nodes, so that host-nesting does not cost a read per level*. **A cache justified only by speed cannot be argued against**, so there was nothing in the row to falsify and no shape to design toward. P6 replaces the justification. Everything else in this section is downstream of that swap, including the parts that still look like performance decisions.

### The claim

> **`ludicCache` is not a faster view of `ludicGraph`. It is the structure serving reference-location, held at whatever depth the current scene has actually surfaced.**

### Why this justification is better than speed, and it is not a rhetorical improvement

**Attention is information that exists nowhere in `ludicGraph`.** A box with a switch inside has exactly one structure whether or not anyone has opened it. Once the cache tracks what has been *surfaced*, it holds something the graph cannot express --- so the argument for a second structure stops being *traversal is slow* and becomes **there is a second thing to be true about.** That is the first argument in this design for why the cache should exist at all, rather than merely why traversal should be avoided, and it is what makes the row designable.

**It also gives the cache a shape [the mutation axis](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-five) can afford.** Promotion and demotion of a subtree into and out of a host's cache node is a bounded local edit. *Recompute an expanded graph* is not.

### The five clauses

1. ~~**Scope: reference-location only.** The cache serves the second of [apprehension's two jobs](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#apprehension-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-12).~~ **Restated 2026-08-13, widened in name and narrowed in principle: the cache serves *theory of mind* --- what this scene has established as common ground.** Reference-location is that job's simplest instance, asked at word scale, so the old scope is **contained rather than overturned**; what changes is that the boundary is now a **rule** instead of an enumeration. **Description stays a lossy read-side coarsening rule over the graph and caches nothing**, and **[consequence-reasoning](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13) is excluded on correctness grounds** rather than budget ones: both ask *what is true*, and the cache does not hold that. **The test a new consumer must pass:** *does this question ask what is in the shared scene's head, or what is true of the world?* Only the first may read the cache. See [Clause 1 is the clause under pressure](#clause-1-is-the-clause-under-pressure-relations-are-not-apprehensions-alone-2026-08-13); **PC-6** carries what remains open.
2. **Depth is attention-scoped, not exhaustive.** A host's cache node holds its members at the depth the current scene has surfaced. A box nobody has opened contributes one handle; its contents live in their own node. Once the box is looked into, **its contents are promoted into the room's node**, and the box's own node degrades to a pointer at the host now carrying that depth.
3. **A hit returns a handle, not a subgraph.** Reference-location resolves a word to an **address**; acting on it means traversing `ludicGraph` from that address. **This is what answers the back-sweep's port-boundary objection** --- see below. **Briefly reopened and restored unchanged 2026-08-13:** the pressure that looked like clause 3's turned out to be clause 1's --- see [Clause 1 is the clause under pressure](#clause-1-is-the-clause-under-pressure-relations-are-not-apprehensions-alone-2026-08-13). *Reference-location's deliverable is still an address*; what is in question is whether reference-location is the cache's only tenant.
4. **Fast path, never the sole path.** A miss falls through to the graph walk, which may resolve, improvise ([P5](#proposal-p5-improvisational-licence-as-a-first-class-graph-item)), or refuse on narrative grounds (*"you would have to open the box first"*). **A cache miss is never an answer of "no."**
5. **Derived, never authoritative.** The cache is materialized from (`ludicGraph`, **the attention ledger**) and is droppable at any time. ***Revelation record* was the original wording; replaced 2026-08-12** when the second input became [a planned artifact rather than a byproduct of narration](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-attention-ledger-as-an-instruction-set-2026-08-12) --- emitted as compiled instructions on the presentation kernel's narrate branch, not inferred from the message stream. The clause is unchanged in force; what changed is that its second input now has an owner and a contract.

**And as of 2026-08-12 that contract has a proposed *form*, which is worth knowing here because it constrains what "materialized from" can mean.** The ledger folds into the cache under [algebraic laws](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-algebraic-contract-2026-08-12) --- **commutative, associative, with compaction as a homomorphism** --- so materialization is order-independent and a partially-ordered or out-of-order ledger produces the same cache. **What the seam fixes is the laws, not the entry type**, which is why a `ludicCache` iteration can be built while the ledger's primitives are still moving. **Nothing about clause 5 changes**; the clause simply now knows what shape its second input arrives in.

### What clause 3 does to the back-sweep's objection, which was AB-35's sharpest open question

[The back-sweep](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) found that a cache holding *expanded graphs across multiple host nodes* **crosses port boundaries --- exactly what [locked-frame clause 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) buys encapsulation to prevent, arriving as a performance measure rather than as a decision** --- against [the lock's own recorded risk](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-the-lock-delivered-for-scale-invariance-and-where-it-stops-assessed-2026-08-06) that *"the failure mode is a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained."* The question left open: **does expansion stop at a port, and if not, what preserves the caller's knowledge that it crossed one?**

**Under P6 the answer is: expansion does not stop at a port, and nothing needs to preserve that knowledge, because the caller never receives a crossed-port structure.** Reference-location is precisely the job that must see through ports --- a player can say "fork" --- but clause 3 hands back an address, not a shape. **Nothing structural can be silently concluded from the cache, because the cache never returns structure.** The flattening the lock feared is confined to an index whose only output is a pointer into the unflattened truth.

### Clause 1 is the clause under pressure: relations are not apprehension's alone (2026-08-13)

**From conversation, and recorded with its wrong turn attached because the wrong turn is the reusable part.** A proposal arrived for `ludicCache` as a **consolidated cross-host graph** --- nodes carrying `shortName` and a vector embedding, edges spanning from room to bookshelf to books, multi-hosted objects contributing only the parts present in *this* cache's host. It was read against P6 and produced two successive misreadings before it produced a finding.

**Misreading 1, and it is a real defect in the proposal.** The proposal's final clause dispensed with ports where both sides of a boundary sit in the same cache, collapsing `ROOM`'s `boulder -TiedTo-> ROPE:4` and `ROPE`'s `rope-end -TiedTo-> (egress)` into a single `rope-end tied to BEDPOST` edge under an innermost-wins tie-break. **That is [the back-sweep's feared flattening](#the-parts-and-ports-back-sweep-2026-08-10) exactly** --- not merely crossing a boundary, but discarding the only evidence a boundary was crossed, and discarding one side's relation-text silently. **The distinction that survives the exchange, and it is narrower than the back-sweep's headline suggests:** consolidating structure across hosts is *not* what [locked-frame clause 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) forbids. **Silently discarding which parts of it crossed a boundary is.** A consolidation that keeps each hop's relation-text and each host entered is disclosure, not erasure, and the lock's recorded risk --- *"a future reader concluding the fractal is uniform because nothing complained"* --- is a risk about silence.

**Misreading 2 was this document's reader, not the proposal, and it is the one worth recording.** The worked example was a **relational edge** (`TiedTo`, across a port), and it was assessed by asking which of [apprehension's two jobs](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#apprehension-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-12) it served --- description or reference-location --- because clause 1 scopes the cache to one of them and the enclosing section was P6. **That is a category error with a name already in this file:** [*when a row will not resolve, check whether it is being argued on the wrong axis*](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#how-to-use-it). Clause 1's scope is a claim about **the cache**; it was silently upgraded into a claim about **what relations are for**.

**Relations are read by four of the five axes, and [the register](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register) says so in its own rows:**

| Axis | What it reads relations for |
| --- | --- |
| **Mutation** | `classifyInteractionUnderTransfer` returns dissolve/carry/defer **from the edge kind**. The register's [second general finding](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register) exists because edge placement *"silently overrides"* that classifier |
| **Reasoning** | **The register's founding entry is the causal kind**, and its content is that relations do not coarsen: *"the system is powered" -> "the door is powered"* is **false** after a cut; answers are **evaluated, not looked up** (AB-39). *If I tug the other end of this rope, what happens* is this axis, and it is decided by the far end being `TiedTo` a bedpost. **Refined 2026-08-13:** that example is the **consequence** half, which reads relations **from `ludicGraph`** and never from the cache --- see [Reasoning is two jobs, not one](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13). It remains a relation-consumer either way, which is all this table claims |
| **Positioning** | The shipped spatial kinds (`On`, `Under`, `Against`, `Custom`) |
| **Apprehension** | Description coarsening, and reference-location disambiguation. **One consumer of relations, not the frame they are defined in** |

**What this does to the crossings chain: it survives, and its justification improves.** The chain --- each element naming the relation-text at that level and the host entered by that hop, terminating either in a node resolved within the cache or in a **port address into a host the cache does not represent** --- was first argued for on locked-frame clause 2 boundary-disclosure grounds. **The stronger argument is the register's**, and it arrives from the reasoning side:

1. **[Soundness of a mechanism is a property of the relation kind, not of the mechanism.](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register)** A collapse rule is a mechanism applied uniformly across kinds. Coarsening is sound for containment and **unsound for causal**, so any single collapse rule is wrong for at least one kind in the register.
2. **The [cross-kind composition question](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register) is open** --- does *character `In` nook* plus *nook `PartOf` room* entail *character `In` room*? --- and it is flagged there as bearing on *"the system's most load-bearing derived fact."* **A cache that collapses chains answers that question silently, per traversal, for every kind-pair at once.** The unmerged chain is the only shape that declines to answer it.

**So the chain is not reference-location's deliverable.** Clause 3 stands: a reference-location hit still returns an address. **The chain is what the cache holds about boundary-crossing relations for whichever axes read the cache** --- and *which axes those are* is precisely what clause 1 asserts without having asked. The terminal address is a fast path either way; acting on it still means grounding against `ludicGraph`, so [clause 4 and clause 5](#the-five-clauses) are untouched and the cache remains an index rather than a second copy.

### Who may read chain depth, and why it is not a free fact (2026-08-13)

**A chain makes cache depth visible** --- how many hops resolved before falling through to an unexpanded port address is [clause 2](#the-five-clauses)'s promotion state read as data. **That is legitimate information for some consumers and a trap for others**, and the split is not intuitive, so it is tabled rather than left to judgement.

| Consumer | May read chain depth? | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Attention ledger / promotion machinery** | **Yes** | Depth *is* what this machinery tracks and mutates. Reading it is the job |
| **Reasoning --- theory of mind** | **Yes, and it needs the hops themselves, not just their count** | *What did the player mean* is answered by what the scene surfaced, and an unexpanded terminal port address is a **known-unknown this job must be able to see** --- [*no silent omission*](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#correction-same-day-reasoning-wants-no-silent-omission-not-losslessness) applied to common ground rather than to world truth |
| **Reasoning --- consequence** | **No, and not on a depth ground at all** | ~~*What happens if I tug* requires the chain~~ **--- it does, and it requires it from [`ludicGraph`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13).** This row first read *Yes* on that example, conflating *needs relational chain data* with *needs it from the cache*. **This job does not read the cache**, so chain depth is not a permission it can be granted or refused |
| **Referent-matching** | **No** | Matching cost must be a function of the referent, not of how deeply the world happens to be modelled. A matcher that branches on chain length makes a well-decomposed abstraction **more expensive to refer into** than a flat one --- inverting the bet that decomposition is free to the reference layer, and making abstractions costly exactly where P6 exists to make them cheap |
| **Mutation** | **No** | Same finding as [PQ-1's mutation row](#which-axes-want-the-ingress-list-and-why-pq-1-does-not-reverse-2026-08-12): footprint reads egress plus membership and never cache state. **A write path that reads cache depth depends on cache warmth**, which is the objection that section records as permanently dead and which must not return through this door |

**Not resolved: how the *no* rows are held.** Clause 3's bare handle was **enforceable by shape rather than by discipline** --- write-through was impossible, not merely forbidden. A chain readable by every consumer is the weaker form, and reintroduces exactly what that phrase was written to avoid. Recorded as **PC-5**.

### Attention as a projection of the transcript --- and the honest cost of it

**The first form of this proposal claimed attention could be projected from the message stream with nothing new made durable.** That claim does not survive contact: recovering *which addresses were revealed* from rendered prose is an open-ended-phrasing problem, which is an LLM job and absurd on a low-latency path. **Attaching a structured revelation record to the message at emit time is the workable form, and it is new durable state** --- calling it a projection does not change that.

**It is new durable state of a particular kind, and that is what keeps it from being a second truth:**

| Property | Why it defuses the second-truth failure |
| --- | --- |
| **Append-only and immutable** | Two truths can contradict only if **both** can be mutated. Drift needs a writer on each side; there is one |
| **Written at emit time by code that already knows the answer** | The look handler knows what it enumerated. No re-derivation, no inference, no second pass |
| **Answers a different question** | `ludicGraph` is truth about world **structure**; the revelation record is truth about **what was said**. *"The box contains a switch"* and *"the room was told about the switch"* cannot disagree |

**Which lets AB-35's invariant be restated more precisely, and more strongly, than the version [P2 clause 2 proposed](#the-invariant-that-keeps-p2-clause-2-honest-with-local-precedent):**

> **There is exactly one *mutable* truth about structure. Attention is immutable, append-only, and answers a different question. `ludicCache` is derived from both and authoritative over neither.**

**That is enforceable by shape rather than by discipline** --- clause 3's handle-return makes write-through impossible rather than forbidden --- and unlike *"the write path never touches the cache; invalidate-and-rederive only"*, it survives the arrival of attention state instead of being contradicted by it.

### It settles the scope question the stored-attention form leaves open

**Per-character or per-room?** Deriving from what the room was *told* forces per-room, and forces it correctly. What licenses saying "switch" is not that a mind perceived it but that **the room's narrative surfaced it**: if A opens the box and the room sees *"A opens the box, revealing a switch,"* B may refer to the switch. That is not a leak, it is [reincorporation](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#fiction-is-a-fault-tolerant-medium--and-what-that-does-not-excuse-2026-08-09) working --- narrative salience is a property of the shared scene, not of individual heads.

### Demotion is the window definition, not a mechanism

**Demotion is the hard half** --- if nothing demotes, the cache accretes back to all-the-data-all-the-way-down and the entire benefit is lost; but aggressive demotion is **worse than never promoting**, because telling a player about a switch and then answering *"you don't see any switch"* is a narrative-integrity violation of the kind [the fault-tolerant-medium section](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#fiction-is-a-fault-tolerant-medium--and-what-that-does-not-excuse-2026-08-09) treats as non-negotiable.

**Under a scene-scoped revelation window, demotion stops being a mechanism at all.** Attention *is* "revelations since the current scene boundary." The scene resets, the window empties, cache nodes collapse to shallow. **No eviction policy to tune, nothing running on a timer, and no way to demote a referent mid-scene while a player still has it in their head.** The bound is natural rather than enforced: a room's referent surface is bounded by how much has been said in it, and a room where a great deal happened *should* carry a deep node.

### Which axes want the ingress list, and why PQ-1 does not reverse (2026-08-12)

**P6's first form claimed the ingress list is traversal data and would have to leave the cache, costing a second PQ-1 reversal. That was wrong, and the test that caught it is the one this plan should reach for first: ask which axis reads the thing.**

**The list is narrower than its description suggests.** It holds *which of this object's ports are live here* --- but [the 2026-08-06 reversal](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06) already established that a port with an exterior edge is **already named by the room's own graph** (`boulder -TiedTo-> ROPE:4`). Its only unique contribution is therefore **ports no edge names**: presence-only ports.

| Axis | Wants it at the `ludicGraph` node? | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Positioning** | No | Filter only. The egress list records the arrangement; the axis is satisfied and goes quiet |
| **Mutation** | **No** | Footprint computation reads the **egress** list --- the half that "must be complete for plan-time footprint computation" --- plus the membership node. Moving X out of a room reads X's egress, and you already hold X. Enumerating what is in the room reads membership. **Cascade-by-reference is AB-36's index, not this list** |
| **Reasoning** | No | Under [*no silent omission*](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#correction-same-day-reasoning-wants-no-silent-omission-not-losslessness) rather than losslessness, reasoning needs the fact to **exist and be retrievable**. Membership reports the thread; opening it yields where-along. Detail costing a hop was the deal, and reasoning has the budget. **Re-read 2026-08-13: this row was answering for [consequence-reasoning](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13) and answering correctly** --- the *theory-of-mind* half had no name yet, and it is the half with a claim on the cache. The verdict is unchanged and its ground improves: consequence reads consensus structure as a **correctness** requirement, not as a budget preference |
| **Apprehension --- description** | No | The room's edges render the coarse view. A presence-only port coarsens to *there is a thread here*, which membership already carries |
| **Apprehension --- reference-location** | **Yes, and it is the only one** | *"Untie the rope"* with two rope-ends live in this room is a **disambiguation** requiring port-level handles that no room edge names |
| **Authoring** | No | Provenance; orthogonal |

**Traversal is conspicuously absent, and that is the whole correction.** Traversing from a room into an object does not consult the list --- the edge names `ROPE:4` and the port is addressed directly. **The list exists only to enumerate what no edge names, and enumerating referrable things nothing else names is reference-location by definition.**

**So PQ-1 does not reverse a second time. P6 confirms the 2026-08-06 placement and supplies the reason it was right** --- which that decision was made without, resting on a weak-versus-strong *storage* argument (1:1 saves one read of an object already known; N:1 saves scanning the world) rather than on what job the data serves. **Two independent arguments, six days apart, agreeing** --- worth more than either alone.

**The ingress list is therefore the first worked instance of what `ludicCache` is for**, rather than a tenant it inherited: a room-level index of referrable handles the room's own edges do not already carry. Three checks, all clean:

1. **Attention-scoping fits.** A presence-only port for a thread nobody has noticed should not be referrable until the scene surfaces it --- [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s corridor, and clause 2 working rather than an exception to it.
2. **[C9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c9-coiling-the-rope-back-in)'s invariant is untouched.** *Present iff at least one port egresses here* relates the **egress** list to membership, and is already recorded as "not a derivation any reader performs." Attention-scoping the ingress side adds no obligation.
3. **The original rejection reason cannot return.** *The room's local read is too hot to depend on cache warmth* was already found not to survive inspection; the mutation row above is why it stays dead --- **mutation never reads this list**, so no write path depends on cache warmth.

### What it does not resolve

- **The unbounded window --- the one genuinely open piece, and the place this will bite first.** A busy hub that never empties of characters has **no natural scene boundary**, so the window never resets and clause 2's attention-scoping degrades toward exhaustive depth. Recorded as **PC-1**; deliberately not attacked in this pass.
- **Whether the cache is persisted at all.** If a scene's revelation window is small, rebuilding a node may be cheap enough that materialization is an optimization to add when measurement demands it. **Good for a construct at provisional tier** --- it keeps a storage commitment out of a design that has not yet been tested against the corpus (**PC-3**).
- **What the revelation record contains**, beyond "more than rendered text" (**PC-2**).
- **Whether the crossings chain needs a per-consumer projection** --- so referent-matching cannot read hop-depth while theory-of-mind and the ledger can --- or whether a documented norm is enough (**PC-5**).
- ~~**Which axes read the cache at all.**~~ **Largely resolved 2026-08-13** by [splitting reasoning](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13): theory-of-mind in, consequence out on correctness grounds, clause 1 restated over the rule. **Residue:** authoring unpolled, and the data/time separability rule unenforced (**PC-6**).
- **The ingress list's home is *not* an open item, and the first form of P6 got this backwards.** It was stated here that the list is traversal data and so probably leaves the cache, triggering a second PQ-1 reversal. **Corrected 2026-08-12, same day, by running the list against the axes** --- see [Which axes want the ingress list](#which-axes-want-the-ingress-list-and-why-pq-1-does-not-reverse-2026-08-12). **Traversal never reads it:** when a room's edge names `ROPE:4` it addresses that port directly, so the list's only unique contribution is **ports no edge names** --- presence-only ports --- and enumerating referrable things nothing else names is reference-location by definition. **PQ-1 does not reverse; P6 confirms its placement and supplies the reason it was right.**

### Open questions (P6 sub-questions; ids stable, never reused)

**Parallel to P3's PQ series, P4's PS series and P5's PL series.**

| ID | Question |
| --- | --- |
| **PC-1** | **HANDED OFF 2026-08-12 to [`AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md`](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md) as AH-6, and widened there.** Kept here rather than deleted because P6's demotion story depends on its answer. **Why it moved:** it is a *representation* row opened with no requirement half in hand --- the [AB-4 conflation](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) one construct over --- and the sub-plan supplies the requirement half first, via a nine-case corpus ([A9](AGENT.attentionHistory.corpus.planning.md#a9-the-busy-hub) is this row's own case). **One thing already learned there and worth carrying back: a decaying record set may shrink this row rather than answer it**, leaving room-empties alive as an optimization rather than as the mechanism. Original wording follows. **What is a scene boundary when the room never empties?** The demotion story rests entirely on the window having an end. A hub with continuous traffic has none, and an unbounded window silently restores the exhaustive-depth cache P6 exists to avoid. Candidates not yet assessed: per-character windows layered over the shared one, a cap that degrades oldest-first, or a heat-based reset borrowing [P4's narrative heat](#narrative-heat-the-optimization-that-is-probably-not-only-an-optimization) |
| **PC-2** | **HANDED OFF 2026-08-12 to [`AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md`](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md)**, split there into **AH-1** (where on the retention axis the record sits) and **AH-2** (what confers salience). **The framing below assumed a flat backward read over the `messages` table, and that assumption was discarded:** append-only immutability is a property of that table, not of attention. Most messages have **no bearing on referents**, and attention operations **aggregate** --- pushing and pulling a lever is not meaningfully different from pushing it twice --- so the natural write is an **idempotent upsert**, not an append. The sub-plan records full-transcript and record-set as **endpoints of a spectrum** rather than as a fork, and treats the unmapped middle as where the answer probably lives. Original wording follows. **What does a revelation record hold?** More than rendered prose, less than a subgraph. Presumably the set of addresses a narrated event surfaced, plus the host they were surfaced *within* --- but whether that is emitted by every event or only by perception commands is undecided, and it interacts with [AB-9's perception carve-out](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) |
| **PC-3** | **Is the cache persisted, or rebuilt per request?** `internalCache.Positions` is a per-request memo and cannot answer this. Deferred deliberately: the answer is a measurement, and the design does not depend on it. **Cost model changed 2026-08-12 without the row changing:** under [the attention ledger](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-attention-ledger-as-an-instruction-set-2026-08-12), *rebuilt* means **replaying a ledger of compiled instructions**, not rescanning the `messages` table --- a materially cheaper and more predictable operation, which makes the rebuild branch more plausible than it looked when this row was written. **Still a measurement, and still deferred**; it now also depends on **AH-10b** (the ledger's carrier type --- re-pointed 2026-08-12 when AH-10 split, since replay cost is a property of what entries *are*, not of the laws they obey). **Given an instrument 2026-08-13, and this is the change that matters most to the row:** [the iteration ladder](#the-ludiccache-iteration-ladder-exhaustive-first-persisted-second-2026-08-13) makes **iteration 1 rebuilt-per-request by construction**, so this row stops being a question answered in advance and becomes **one that iteration 1 answers by running** --- reads and wall time per rebuild, tagged by object count and nesting depth, in a regime already measured as **round-trip dominated rather than byte dominated**, so the cost is roughly one round trip per nested shard. **The row is therefore no longer deferred for want of evidence; it is scheduled to receive some.** **And it must not be pre-empted:** the expectation that persistence will pay is well-motivated but unmeasured, and letting iteration 2 follow on a timetable would answer this row by impatience rather than by data. **What the measurement buys beyond a yes/no is *where the knee is*, which is what tells iteration 2 whether it should be optimizing round trips or something else** |
| **PC-4** | **Does description-coarsening ever want structure reference-location does not, or is it purely a read rule?** Clause 1 asserts the latter. If a case shows description needing its own cached structure, P6 splits into two constructs and the name has to go to one of them |
| **PC-5** | **Opened 2026-08-13 with [the crossings chain](#who-may-read-chain-depth-and-why-it-is-not-a-free-fact-2026-08-13).** Does the chain need a **per-consumer projection** --- [theory-of-mind](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13) and the ledger seeing hops, referent-matching seeing only the terminal address --- or does a documented norm hold? *(Consequence-reasoning and mutation drop out of this row: neither reads the cache at all, so there is no projection to give them.)* The bare handle was enforceable **by shape**; a chain readable by every consumer is enforceable only **by discipline**, a weaker guarantee than clause 3 used to make. Candidates not yet assessed: a narrow accessor returning terminal-address-plus-a-boundary-crossed-boolean for the *no* rows; a runtime assertion that trips when matching code inspects hop count; or accepting the gap as PL-1-style Prototype-tier risk. **Note the two *no* rows fail differently** --- referent-matching degrades to *expensive*, mutation degrades to *dependent on cache warmth*, which is a correctness concern rather than a cost one, so a single mechanism may not serve both |
| **PC-6** | **Opened and substantially answered the same day, 2026-08-13** --- kept as a row because the residue is real and because the *shape* of the answer should not have to be re-derived. **Opened as:** *which axes read `ludicCache`?* Clause 1 said *reference-location only*, reached by asking which of **apprehension's** two jobs the cache serves --- a question that structurally cannot return any other axis, so the other four were never polled. **Answered by splitting the asker rather than by polling:** [reasoning is two jobs](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#reasoning-is-two-jobs-not-one-2026-08-13), and they land on opposite sides. **Theory-of-mind reads the cache** (and reference-location is its word-scale instance, so clause 1's old scope is contained in the new one); **consequence does not, on correctness grounds** --- it asks *what is true*, which an attention-scoped structure does not hold at any budget. Clause 1 is restated over that rule. **What remains open, and it is smaller than the row was:** (i) whether **authoring** has a claim --- untouched by this pass, and [the axis with zero corpus coverage](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-the-tally-immediately-produced), so the frame's own weakest evidence base is the one still unpolled here; (ii) whether the theory-of-mind/consequence line **holds under implementation**, given the joint `(identity, plan)` proposer interleaves them by step --- the rule is *separable in data, not in time*, and nothing enforces it yet; (iii) **PC-4** is unaffected and still asks its own question about description |

### The `ludicCache` iteration ladder: exhaustive first, persisted second (2026-08-13)

**From conversation, proposed by the user, and it is the first thing in this initiative to license code.** The question that produced it: CoyoteGame is about to allow objects placed **in, on, under** other objects, and the moment it does, apprehension and referent-matching stop being design topics and become the hot path. Establishing general relations --- much less abstractions, much less mutating objects and features --- **before** the `ludicCache` contract exists would build a large consumer surface against a seam that then has to move.

**The ladder, and only iteration 1 is licensed today:**

| Iteration | What it is | What it exists to settle |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | **Exhaustive and derived per request.** Every referrable node in the room, including the nested contents of every object present; rebuilt on each read and stored nowhere | How the apprehension and referent layers **interact with the cache's structure** |
| **2** | **Persisted, same contents** | How `ludicGraph` mutations **cascade** into the cache |

**The split is clean because iteration 1 has no invalidation problem by construction** --- derived-per-request is fresh by definition --- so iteration 2 introduces exactly one new variable.

#### Why an exhaustive cache is safe to build ahead of the corpus

**It violates exactly one of [the five clauses](#the-five-clauses), and in the permitted direction.** Clause 2's attention-scoping is the only casualty; clauses 1, 3, 4 and 5 hold unchanged. **The failure this design calls non-negotiable is the demotion one** --- telling a room about a switch and then answering *"you don't see any switch"* --- and **an exhaustive cache is structurally incapable of it**, because it never demotes.

**It is equally incapable of [AH-12's fast-wrong hazard](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#attention-near-the-accept-gate-can-produce-fast-wrong).** A cache with **no salience** has nothing to feed `T_MARGIN` and no basis on which to pre-filter a candidate pool, so it complies trivially with the attention plan's *keep attention out of `decideEmbeddingMatch`* rule. **Iteration 1 is therefore not blocked on [A10](AGENT.attentionHistory.corpus.planning.md#a10-the-book-on-the-table-and-the-books-on-the-shelf)**, otherwise the corpus case with standing over what any iteration may do at the accept gate.

**And [the measured constants](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-measured-constants) say exhaustive is affordable at this scale:** ~400--650 B per node, a real ceiling near 600--1000 nodes, marginal residency in microseconds against an abstention costing ~1000x. CoyoteGame is five rooms of staged Acme objects --- **two orders of magnitude below the pressure that makes attention-scoping necessary**, which is precisely why now is when the seam can be got right without also solving the policy.

#### Clause 3 is the discipline that makes the ladder work

**It is also the clause a crude implementation will casually break, and the only violation here that is real refactor debt.** When every nested object's contents are already resident, handing back a **subgraph** instead of a **handle** is the path of least resistance --- and it is exactly the port-boundary crossing [locked-frame clause 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) buys encapsulation to prevent, arriving as convenience rather than as a decision. **Consumers coded against *a hit returns a handle, a miss falls through* are indifferent to whether the cache is exhaustive or attention-scoped**, which is the whole reason the internal policy may churn behind it. Consumers handed structure are not.

> **Hold clause 3 rigidly from the first commit. Everything else in the ladder is a bet that can be rolled back; clause 3 is what bounds the rollback set.**

#### Iteration 1 is not scaffolding

**[Clause 5](#the-five-clauses) makes the cache droppable at any time, so iteration 2 needs a cold-rebuild path regardless --- and iteration 1 *is* that path**, promoted rather than discarded. It is also iteration 2's **differential oracle**: [the convergence property test](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-band-resolves-cold) already queued for the ledger channel --- cold rederivation and warm incremental evolution must reach the same steady state --- is normally hard to write because only one implementation exists. **Here both do, and the test is nearly free.**

#### Cascade is softer than it looks, and must not be transactional

**Work the two drift directions against clause 4.** A **stale-positive** entry (the object left, the cache still holds it) resolves to a handle that grounds against `ludicGraph` and yields a **correct refusal** --- [A2](AGENT.attentionHistory.corpus.planning.md#a2-the-rope-that-was-carried-away)'s subject, and though A2 is not worked it flags the cheap direction as plausibly also the **better** one, since *"the rope is no longer here"* beats *"you don't see any rope."* A **stale-negative** (the object arrived, the cache does not know) is a **miss**, and misses fall through.

> **Both drift directions degrade to slow, never to wrong. So cascade may be lazy and eventual rather than transactional.**

**That is what keeps iteration 2 off the mutation critical path** --- out of `computeStepSequenceFootprint` and clear of the `MultiKeyUpdate`-cannot-be-re-entered constraint that has killed or re-priced more proposals here than apprehension has. **The instinct will be to make cascade transactional; that is the version that gets expensive, and clause 4 is why it is unnecessary.**

**The shape it should take is already shipped in this lane.** `affordanceCache` bumps a catalog version on write and rebuilds only on a **stale read** --- not on invalidation receive ([`internalCache/AGENT.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/internalCache/AGENT.md)). That matters because [the write-cost finding](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#write-cost-and-the-second-argument-for-two-layers) makes per-mutation cascade expensive at any record size: DynamoDB charges an update against **total item size**, so touching a fat room record costs ~100 WCU however little changed. **Cheap marker on write, expensive rebuild on next stale read** is the pattern that survives it --- and adopting it is joining a shipped pattern rather than inventing a layer, the same argument the ledger got from the two-kernel structure.

#### What iteration 1 must measure, because both answers are cheap to take and expensive to reconstruct

1. **PC-3's own measurement** --- reads and wall time per rebuild, tagged by object count and nesting depth. Exhaustive expansion costs roughly **one round trip per nested shard** in a regime already measured as **round-trip dominated rather than byte dominated**, so this is the curve that says whether persistence is needed, and *where the knee is*.
2. **The counterfactual miss** --- log what *would* have missed under attention-scoping, without acting on it. That is [branch (b)](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#hit-and-miss-emit-the-same-signal)'s signal obtained with no ledger built, and it is what tells the ledger channel whether it has earned its build. It also makes [A9](AGENT.attentionHistory.corpus.planning.md#a9-the-busy-hub) an early measurement rather than a late surprise.

#### Three hazards, recorded so they are designed for rather than discovered

1. **With no misses, clause 4's fall-through path is never exercised.** It will be dead code until the day the cache stops being exhaustive --- the worst possible moment for its first run. **Build it and test it deliberately**, with tests that force a miss the ordinary path cannot produce.
2. **An exhaustive cache neutralizes AH-3 rather than answering it.** Everything is promoted, so [A3](AGENT.attentionHistory.corpus.planning.md#a3-the-box-inside-the-box) cannot be worked against the prototype. Fine under *the corpus judges the iteration, never the reverse* --- **but nobody may later read the prototype's behaviour as the depth answer.**
3. **PC-3 must be answered by measurement, not by schedule.** The expectation that persistence will pay is a hypothesis with a stated mechanism, and it is cheap to check --- **but "I am confident" is not the measurement**, and letting iteration 2 follow on a timetable would answer PC-3 by impatience. *(If iteration 2 is instead run early as deliberate **de-risking** of the cascade problem, that is legitimate and different --- it just carries a different rollback trigger and should say so.)* **The legitimate hedge is to keep iteration 1's rebuild function pure and free of per-request assumptions**, so that if the measurement says persist, iteration 2 is a delta rather than a rewrite.

#### Why CoyoteGame is the right consumer for it

**Recorded because it is the reason the timing is now rather than after Phase 5.** CoyoteGame's intended trajectory --- unbounded Acme object types, object-manipulation physics built into staging, and reasoning that derives intent from **a constructed tree of abstractions** rather than a bag of leaf nodes --- is a demand curve this plan has otherwise had to imagine. **It supplies the two things both corpora are short of:** real authored-and-improvised object population above the trivial, and real referent traffic against it. A Coyote setting a trip-wire in one room attached to a spring-loaded trap in another is [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread)'s multi-host extent **and** [the relation-kind register](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register)'s unbuilt causal kind in one player action. **The near-term slices therefore exercise the parts of the register still recorded as prediction rather than finding**, which is the coverage this initiative most needs and cannot manufacture.

### Status

**Iteration 1 elected a Prototype 2026-08-13; the proposal itself remains provisional.** Under [the graduation tiers](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11) P6 has **not** been promoted: it has not been run against the corpus, PC-1 is open, and its central claim is still a reframe rather than a tested rule. What is licensed is **one bounded bet** --- [the exhaustive iteration 1](#the-ludiccache-iteration-ladder-exhaustive-first-persisted-second-2026-08-13) --- on the terms [PL-1 iteration 1](#pl-1-iteration-1-brute-force-relevance-classified-as-a-prototype-2026-08-09) took. **A proposal is not a lock and a Prototype is not a graduation.**

**Rollback trigger, named before the code exists:** if holding clause 3 (handle, never subgraph) proves impossible against a real consumer --- that is, if the referent path genuinely needs structure the cache would have to return --- **iteration 1 rolls back and P6 clause 3 is re-opened**, because that is the clause the whole ladder's churnability rests on. Cost, latency and cache-shape surprises are *not* rollback triggers; they are the measurements the iteration exists to take.

**Examined and not fired, 2026-08-13.** A consolidated-graph proposal looked at first like the trigger's case --- *the referent path needs structure the cache would have to return* --- and clause 3 was briefly reopened on that reading. **It was the wrong clause.** Reference-location's deliverable is still an address; what the proposal actually pressured is **clause 1's tenant list**, since the relations it wanted preserved are read by mutation and reasoning as much as by apprehension. See [Clause 1 is the clause under pressure](#clause-1-is-the-clause-under-pressure-relations-are-not-apprehensions-alone-2026-08-13). **Clause 3 stands unmodified and the trigger stands unfired** --- which matters, because it is the clause the ladder's churnability rests on and a trigger fired by mistake would have cost that for nothing.

**Two things did come out of it and are open:** the **crossings chain** as the shape for boundary-crossing relations the cache holds (justified on [the register's](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-relation-kind-register) kind-dependent coarsening soundness, not on clause 2 alone), and **who may read chain depth** --- **PC-5** and **PC-6**.

**The name `ludicCache` remains locked and its design remains provisional** --- P6 changes the second, not the first.

### The case that would test it

**[C16](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c16-the-bare-table-and-the-room-that-is-just-a-mess) is the discriminating one**: a room that is just a mess has no candidate pattern to coarsen toward, yet referents must still bind. Under clause 2 a mess is nearly empty until someone rummages, and each rummage promotes a slice --- **either exactly right or obviously wrong**, which is what makes it a test rather than an illustration.

**[C17](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c17-the-professional-kitchen-the-chefs-knife-and-the-stand-mixer-nobody-asked-about) is the one clause 4 exists for.** Walk into the professional kitchen, type `get chef's knife`, nobody has looked at anything. Attention-scoping alone says unfindable; [the authoring axis](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#the-five) says this is the case that must improvise one. **They reconcile only if a miss falls through**, which is why "never the sole path" is a clause and not a caveat.

**[C8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c8-the-flashlight-that-vanished-when-someone-looked-at-it) is worth a second reading under P6**, because its defect inverts: *looking closely makes a thing unreferrable* is precisely what clause 2 cannot do, since looking closely **promotes**. If that holds it is a second, independent fix for C8 --- and a fix arriving from a mechanism built for something else is evidence for the frame rather than a coincidence.

---

## Proposal P8: the edge as an entity --- independent existence, identity, and multi-graph occurrence

**Numbered P8 because P7 is reserved and unwritten** --- the `ludicCache` reducer, recorded in [that corpus](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md) as *"the author's to write"* and already cited as a gate. **The gap is deliberate; do not fill it with this.**

### Status

**Provisional as design, written 2026-08-28 from conversation, and it is a proposal rather than a decision --- with an *iteration 1* classified as a **Prototype** on 2026-08-30, which is a licence to build and not a promotion.** It arose in the [edge-abstractions](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md) lane while grading [EP-1 against EP-2](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#how-ep-1-and-ep-2-discriminate--what-ea-c5-must-show), and **it is placed here rather than there because its subject is what an edge *is*, at any scale, where that lane's subject is what one edge *means* at another scale.** Its consequences land in four lanes that plan does not own: [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s cover, the `ludicCache` reducer, [AB-52](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s reverse index, and [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)'s graduated verdict on edge identity.

**[The leg-granularity fork](#the-fork-inside-p8-whole-path-or-per-leg) splits by concern rather than resolving to a side:** identity and reference adhere to the **chain**; hosting, adjacency and mutation-site adhere to the **leg**. Four of the five discriminator rows are filled; **row 5 (`P7`) stays open**, as does the **mixed-pair case of [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule)**. **The discriminator table is what produced that answer rather than illustrating it** --- it was built empty, on the [`ludicCache` tally](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#cache-difference-tally)'s precedent, because a pre-filled table makes a proposal argue for a conclusion instead of reaching one.

~~**No corpus case has been run against any of this.**~~ **One has, 2026-08-29: [LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption), the named falsification target booked here per [rule 3](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#phase-0-corpus), re-graded rather than read because it was written to test the reducer's interface and not a storage model.** **Its substantive result is a reattribution on discriminator 3** --- what saves LC8's second job is **clauses 1 and 3(b)**, not chain identity. **On discriminator 2 it corroborates rather than discriminates**, and only against *bare* per-leg, which is not the adopted position. **What it does not do is falsify anything, and that is the honest limit of it:** clause 3(b)'s partial occurrence **dissolves** the case's hazard rather than being tested by it, so **P8 still holds no corpus case that could have gone the other way.** That is a **Locked**-tier bar, not a Prototype one --- recorded as an exclusion below. **Clause 1 remains argument-only: LC8 does not exercise independent storage at all.**

**A second was read the same day: [EA-C6](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#read-against-p8s-chain-scoping-2026-08-29-and-the-case-can-carry-less-of-this-than-the-booking-assumed), booked by clause 2, and clause 2 records *confirmed*.** **It does not change the exclusion above**, and the reason is instructive rather than incidental: its relation is **single-leg**, so chain-scope and record-scope are **indistinguishable on it** and it could not have gone the other way either. **What it adds is corroboration of the principle from a mover independent of the one the resolution used** --- fields move under a fictional beat as leg counts move under re-tuning --- **and nothing that costs P8 anything.** **Clause 1 remains argument-only after both reads.**

**Three claims from that item were made and withdrawn the same day, under two rounds of user challenge, and the item is thin because thin is what it was.** *(i) Wrong-id exposure scales with leg count* --- **false**: re-tuning splits copy an id the caller already holds, and the one real decision point was already counted separately. *(ii) Continuity is bought by clause 1 rather than clause 2* --- **false and flattering**: clause 1 gives an edge a record, not a way to change one. *(iii) P8 cannot express the case's beat, so it owes a decision about where `relationLabel` lives* --- **false at the root**, and it was written *while correcting (ii)*: delete-plus-add is the condition under which **identity wins**, since the `add` carries the id, so no update op is owed; its second horn also imported a **multi-leg** hazard into a case the same item had just established is **single-leg**. **It was briefly booked as a tenth graduation item and is withdrawn**, along with the collateral damage below. **The generator is shape-matching to the previous item's payoff** --- item 2 resolved into a reattribution, so item 3 grew one, then grew a replacement when that one fell. Logged in the [performance tally](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.performance.md).

**The path to a tier is written and bounded: see [what graduating this requires](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)** --- **ten items**, the discovery steps scheduled first, and the exclusions stated with their reasons. **Each item states whether it grew the list and from where**, which is checkable per item rather than asserted once for all of them: **item 9** came from the destination tier's entry conditions (item 2's run), **item 10** from item 4's decision going against its recorded lean. **Item 3 added none**, though it briefly claimed a tenth on an unfounded ground.

**Classified as a *Prototype*, 2026-08-30, and this paragraph is [item 9](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires) --- written before the code exists, not reconstructed after it.** On [P6's](#the-ludiccache-iteration-ladder-exhaustive-first-persisted-second-2026-08-13) and [PL-1's](#pl-1-iteration-1-brute-force-relevance-classified-as-a-prototype-2026-08-09) precedent, and for their reason: **the evidence P8 is missing is not obtainable by argument.** [The exclusion list](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires) says so in as many words --- P8 holds **no corpus case that could have gone the other way**, which is a bar for *Locked* and is the ordinary condition for *Prototype*.

- **Iteration 1 is the field, and only the field.** `chainId?: string` carried on every leg, compared ahead of structure in `edgesMatch` per [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule). **No Edge record, no reverse index, no presence for edges, and no mint or strip operation** --- [item 10](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s two operations are **specified and booked, not built**.
- **Tag dependent rows `P8-i1-dependent`, never `P8-dependent`**, per the over-tagging warning [the tagging test](#the-tagging-test-and-why-it-is-the-point) gives and `P4-i1-dependent` follows. **What a tagged row may cite is iteration 1's guarantee alone:** *a chain-scoped id is carried on every leg and consulted before structure*. It may **not** cite the entity half --- independent existence, multi-graph occurrence, reverse discovery --- which iteration 1 does not build and no row may assume.
- **The set is one row, and its size is the measurement rather than an embarrassment.** **[EA-10](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-10) is `P8-i1-dependent`** --- its five structure-as-identity sites are waiting on exactly this rule, and it is the row that named the need. **[AB-65](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) is deliberately *not* tagged**, and the distinction is the tagging test working rather than an oversight: what AB-65 takes from P8 is the **concern split** and the four-case rule, both proposal-side and both surviving a rollback of the field intact. **A row that would still stand if the code came out does not belong in the rollback set.**

**The rollback trigger, named now, and it is a clause.** **P8 rolls back if a write path must decide *same chain or not* and no vocabulary can supply the answer** --- that is, the deciding fact is **absent at every caller**, not merely unthreaded through the ones that exist. **Iteration 1 is withdrawn and [AB-65](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) returns to open if it fires**, because chain-scoped identity would then be a thing the substrate cannot mint honestly, and *edges are entities* would have failed at the only point that matters.

**The trigger is sharpened past the version item 9 offered, and [EA-C6](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#read-against-p8s-chain-scoping-2026-08-29-and-the-case-can-carry-less-of-this-than-the-booking-assumed) is why.** The offered form --- *a caller that must decide with no access to the legs that would answer it* --- **was instantiated on 2026-08-29 and did not fire**: a relabel's `add` half may arrive with no predecessor to inherit an id from, and the endpoint-pair fallback cannot recover one, **yet the relabel operation itself knows which relation it is relabelling.** The information was **unpassed, not absent**, and the answer was a build requirement (the mutation vocabulary carries the id). **A trigger that fires on that case would have fired on a survivable one**, which is the same defect as naming a cost, one level in.

**Not triggers, stated so no measurement reads as a rollback signal:** record-size growth from an id on every leg; the number of legs a re-tuning split must copy an id to; the awkwardness of threading `chainId` through the mutation vocabulary --- **that one is settled as a build requirement and must not be re-litigated as evidence against the tier**; and the count of populations that carry no id at all, which iteration 1 expects to be nearly all of them per [AB-65](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s *inline unidentified* majority.

**Where the build is booked: [Channel E](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order) in the parent's Recommended order**, opened 2026-08-30 with this classification. **Items 7 and 10 land there as `CE1` and `CE2`/`CE3`**, and the channel --- not this list --- is where follow-on implementation accrues once the prototype is live.

**The open row this proposal answers is [AB-65](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only), *is an edge a value or an entity?*** --- written **2026-08-29**, after this proposal rather than before it, on the user's instruction to place the proposal first. **The row is titled to name the claim rather than this proposal**, so a reader who rejects P8 still has the question; it records that *edges are leaves* is argued and *edges are values* never was; and it carries the two things this proposal has settled inside itself (the leg/chain concern split, and [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule) complete in all four cases) **so that the row is not re-argued from scratch.** **What it does not take from here is the evidence bar:** it records that P8's destination is Prototype and that no corpus case could have gone the other way. **The back-formed order is recorded on the row rather than smoothed over**, since a question written to fit an existing answer is worth flagging as such.

### Leg and chain, and why the corpus does not reliably distinguish them

**Added 2026-08-28. Read this before the clauses, because the corpus's word *edge* covers both of these and every section below depends on which one is meant.**

- A **leg** is one `from`/`to` pair terminating on nodes or ports, lying wholly inside one graph. **This is what the substrate stores**: `EphemeraLudicRelationalEdgeBase` has exactly one `from` and one `to`.
- A **chain** is **how a relationship is addressed across boundary crossings** --- the route by which one end reaches the other when the two do not sit in the same graph --- **and the independent referent that route answers to**: what `edgeId` names, plus whatever record is persisted for reference, **multi-present at each of its legs.** **Function first, because the identity conditions below do not by themselves say what a chain is *for*, and the nearest wrong answer is *the relationship itself*.** A chain is **not** a fictional relation and is **not** made of them: a rope is *n* connections, one per piece-to-piece contact, and cutting it is an act on **one connection**. **What a chain is made of is crossings.** **The substrate cannot represent it at all** --- there is no field for it. **A chain is not *identified* by an aggregation over legs** --- neither a sequence of them nor a set of them --- since either gloss makes the durable thing's identity rest on occurrences that re-tuning changes. **The claim is about identity, not composition.** A chain's **extension** at any moment *is* its legs --- legs compose it. What legs do **not** do is **constitute its identity**: what makes this chain *this* chain cannot be a quantity that re-tuning changes. **Three things force that direction.** **(1) Locality of reference.** A chain **always has legs somewhere**, but a **reference** to a chain may be hosted where **none of its legs are**. So a reader holding the reference cannot resolve identity by inspecting local legs, because there are none there to inspect --- **identity has to travel as a name, independently of the occurrences.** **(2) Partiality.** The legs anyone can enumerate are never guaranteed to be all of them, so a leg-constituted identity would rest on a set no reader can close over. **(3) Re-tunability**, the original argument: legs change when the layering is re-tuned and the fiction does not. **All three bear on identity; none forbids composition.** See [multi-abstraction wrapping](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28).

**The distinction is not merely descriptive, and this is the fact that decides where identity goes.** A leg exists *because a chain crossed a boundary*, and that boundary may be **containment** (visible in the fiction) or **abstraction** (not). **So leg count is not invariant under re-tuning:** introduce an abstraction between two nodes and one chain becomes three legs with nothing in the fiction having changed; collapse it and they become one again. **A chain's count is invariant, because it answers to the fiction rather than to the current tuning.**

**Read *answers to the fiction* as *invariant under re-tuning*, and not as *addressable by the fiction* --- the visibility asymmetry above is a claim about chains, not only about counting legs.** A chain built of **abstraction** crossings is **invisible to the fiction by design**: nothing a player or an authored action does names it, targets it, or can observe it come apart. **So breaking a chain is an *authoring* event, not a fictional one.** Its shape: a connection runs from the candlestick to the `[In-the-PlaceSetting][In-the-Utensils]` fork, and an author withdraws `[In-the-PlaceSetting] -> [In-the-Utensils]` as a route because the fiction should not be thought about that way. **Nothing in the world moved and the connection did not change** --- what changed is how it can be **reached**. **This is where independent identity earns its keep, and the argument does not need fiction to see it:** were identity an aggregation over the route, withdrawing a crossing would **destroy the referent**, so re-organizing addressing would silently change which relationships exist. Identity held apart from the route makes the same event **owe a re-addressing** instead --- the route is gone, the referent is not, and the reverse index names who was pointing at it. **Re-organization is attested constantly; it is what re-tuning *is*.**

**Which is the argument, and it is a correctness argument rather than an aesthetic one:** anything that must be **semantically stable** --- identity, citation, *the same relationship as before* --- **cannot adhere to a quantity that changes when the layering is re-tuned.** **This is stated as checkable, not as established**: it should be run against the re-tunability rows, and it is the load-bearing new reasoning in this section.

**How badly the corpus conflates them, measured rather than asserted (2026-08-28).** *Leg* is **not a missing word** --- it appears 17 times across the edge-abstractions files at `dd24e208d`, and [EP-1b](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#open-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) already asks its question in leg vocabulary (*"which graph hosts the end-legs?"*). But it appears **only in structural passages, is nowhere defined, and never carries a semantic claim**, against roughly 700 uses of *edge*. **The risk is concentrated, not diffuse:** the sites where the two readings actually diverge are [EA-10](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-10), EP-1b, [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s dissolution, and the five structure-as-identity call sites in EA-10's survey. **A corpus-wide vocabulary sweep is owed and is not done here**; its useful form is *audit and annotate* --- classify each *edge* as leg, chain, or genuinely-either, and treat every genuinely-either as a suspect rather than a pass.

### Multi-abstraction wrapping: a node in two abstractions, and what chain means after it (2026-08-28)

**This amends the leg/chain section above rather than extending it.** The setup: `ROOM#DinnerParty` contains two abstractions, `OBJECT#PlaceSetting` and `OBJECT#HeirloomSilverware`; a fork is a part of **both**; a candlestick at room level is related to the fork. **Which boundary does that relationship pass through?**

**The topology is already attested and did not need writing.** It is [LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption) with the names changed --- `OBJECT#D` inside both box `OBJECT#B` and contraption `OBJECT#C`, which is the case cited above as the standing witness that node membership is not unique. **One difference is worth keeping:** LC8's two hosts are **mixed**, containment and abstraction, so it is the weaker witness for *legs are artifacts of tuning*; the fork's two hosts are both abstractions.

**The question has no answer, because it presumes an answer is required.** It asks which boundary the relationship passes through as though *some* boundary must carry it and the rest must not. **Abstractions are partial.** PlaceSetting may state the candlestick-to-fork relation and HeirloomSilverware may say nothing about it, and that is not loss --- **it is what an abstraction is.** Neither one promises to be exhaustive about the fork.

**The rule this yields, and it is the durable part of the section: an abstraction may omit; it may not contradict.** Partiality about what is *stated*; no partiality about what is *true where stated*. Omission needs no mechanism. Contradiction does, and that is the only thing here that costs anything.

**With completeness dropped, the three candidates re-price.**

1. **The constructor states the relation in one wrapper and not the other.** **Viable, and ordinary.** It is a partial statement, which is the normal condition of an abstraction. It is also what the shipped substrate produces without anyone deciding to: one `from`, one `to`, no path.
2. **Two records, same endpoints, in two abstractions.** **Splits.** Sharing an `edgeId` makes them one relation stated twice --- fine, and the useful case. Carrying **different** ids makes the fiction hold two candlestick-to-fork relations **because of a tuning decision**, so the count of facts about the fork varies with how many abstractions happen to contain it. **That branch stays falsified** by re-tunability; it is the only one that does.
3. **Every crossing recorded.** **Viable but never obligatory.** Nobody must enumerate crossings they cannot discover, because nothing requires the record to be complete.

**What the real hazard is, and the corpus already names it.** A partial abstraction is safe. A partial abstraction **read as complete** is a **cached negative** --- [LC9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc9-the-place-setting-laid-across-two-tables)'s *"this place setting has no cup"*, which [the algebraic contract](AGENT.attentionHistory.planning.md#the-algebraic-contract-2026-08-12) classes as an entry that is *"a claim about the world"* and fails both to commute and to degrade to slow. **This is a place where a cold read invents a completeness claim**, and that --- not routing --- is what wants a warning.

**What a chain is.** A chain is an **independent referent**: the thing `edgeId` names, plus whatever independent record is persisted for convenience and reference. **It is multi-present at each of its legs.** Its **identity** is *not* an aggregation over legs --- **neither a sequence of them nor a set of them** --- and both glosses are wrong for the same reason, that they make identity rest on occurrences that re-tuning changes. **The claim is about identity, not composition.** A chain's **extension** at any moment *is* its legs --- legs compose it. What legs do **not** do is **constitute its identity**: what makes this chain *this* chain cannot be a quantity that re-tuning changes. **Three things force that direction.** **(1) Locality of reference.** A chain **always has legs somewhere**, but a **reference** to a chain may be hosted where **none of its legs are**. So a reader holding the reference cannot resolve identity by inspecting local legs, because there are none there to inspect --- **identity has to travel as a name, independently of the occurrences.** **(2) Partiality.** The legs anyone can enumerate are never guaranteed to be all of them, so a leg-constituted identity would rest on a set no reader can close over. **(3) Re-tunability**, the original argument: legs change when the layering is re-tuned and the fiction does not. **All three bear on identity; none forbids composition.**

**Two consequences follow directly, and both are gains rather than costs.** A chain **always has legs somewhere**, but a **reference** to it may be hosted where **none of its legs are** --- the referent does not depend on the referring graph holding an occurrence. And a chain may be **a terminal of an edge**, which the first consequence makes more expressive rather than less: the edge can be hosted somewhere neither of its terminal chains has a leg.

**The worked shape:** `CoarseEdge -[JustifiedBy]-> DetailEdge`. A coarse statement and the detailed statement that warrants it, related **as an edge between two chains**, hosted wherever the justification is worth recording --- **not** constrained to a graph where either chain has a leg. **This is the coarse/detailed sibling relation from the consistency discussion above, given a representable form**: what reconciliation must otherwise infer, this states.

**And [EA-1](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register) already found the reason it must be stated rather than derived.** Its granularity-explicitness check found the row *"naming **one** granularity relation (coarse edge to its expansion) and treating it as **functional**"* --- and broke it on multiplicity: an Arrangement justifies **zero or more** coarse edges, so **"composition does not recover which members support which claim."** **A relation composition cannot recover is one that has to be written down**, and an edge whose terminals are chains is the thing that can write it. **Cited as convergence, not as EA-1's own conclusion** --- EA-1 identified the gap; it did not propose this filling.

**Hosting-apart is foundational rather than merely convenient, and a second argument reaches it independently of the identity one.** EA-1's multiplicity finding requires a justification relation that is **stated rather than composed**. **Every candidate form of one needs referents hosted apart from their legs**, and that is the load-bearing step: under partiality **there is no guarantee that a coarse chain and a detail chain share any graph at all** --- the coarse statement sits at the outer level, the detail inside the abstraction, and nothing obliges an overlap. **So a rule requiring a justification record to sit where its referents' legs sit does not make the record expensive; it makes it unrepresentable for some configurations.** That is an impossibility, not a cost.

**Two independent arguments reach hosting-apart** --- locality of reference, above, and expansion-recording, here --- **so the requirement survives either one failing.** That is worth more than a restatement of the first would be.

**The hazard recurs one level up.** If justification is stated rather than composed, and the record is partial, then the **absence** of a `JustifiedBy` record does **not** mean the coarse edge is unjustified. **Same cached negative**, now about justification rather than about relations.

**Two items. (a) is a live choice; (b) is answered.** **(a) Chain-terminal edge versus reified justification node.** An edge whose terminals are chains is one form; a **node** referencing one coarse edge and N detail edges is another, and the corpus has already chosen that second shape for the structurally identical problem --- [EA-7](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-7) resolved to *reification with reference, not reification with containment*, an Arrangement being a node whose member edges stay siblings. **EA-1's finding forces a stated relation; it does not pick between these two forms**, and anything adopting the chain-terminal form owes an argument against EA-7's precedent rather than a preference. **Note both forms need hosting-apart equally**, so the paragraph above is unaffected by which wins. **(b) Nothing fixes the host of a chain-terminal edge, and nothing needs to.** Node-terminal edges take their host from where their terminals sit; chain-terminal edges need not, and **no rule replaces that one**. **A reader who needs everything about a chain consults the chain's reverse index and reads the graphs it names --- exactly what a reader needing everything about a node already does**, so the record may be hosted wherever the fiction makes it worth stating. **The question this replaces asked for a *unique legal host*, which nothing in this system has** --- not nodes, not edges. **It is worth naming as the same failure this section is about:** a demand for a determinate location is a completeness demand in a locality costume.
