**What (b) leaves behind, and it is a requirement rather than an open question.** If chain-referents are found through a reverse index, **that index must cover hosts that reference a chain, and not only hosts holding one of its legs** --- the two have just been told apart, and a `JustifiedBy` record sitting where no leg sits is invisible to a leg-only index. **Clause 5's index is currently the leg form**: keyed on chain identity, with its fan-out bounded to *the legs of one chain*. **Widening it to legs plus referencing hosts is the concrete change this section asks for**, and it is a widening of an index that already exists rather than a new mechanism.

**Three things about that widening, so it is priced honestly.** **(i) It does not resurrect the truth-side index this proposal shed, and it is priced at parity with the node case.** The entry is written where the referencing record is created --- the mutation site --- and that is the same write nodes already pay: `EphemeraPositionAdjacency` is a **maintained** truth-side record with a dual write and an atomicity discipline, not something the substrate supplies for free. **It is not the *port list is the reverse index* mechanism**, which is PQ-6's egress discovery and which [AB-55](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) sorts apart from the adjacency record by the question each answers. **So there is no cost asymmetry between finding a node across its hosts and finding a chain across its references.** **(ii) It should not take [P6](#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) clause 4's *an error costs slow, never wrong* licence**, even though the lookup is read-side. A missing entry does not contradict anything and so stays inside the *may omit* floor, but the failure it produces is a reader concluding **unjustified** from an absence --- the cached negative this section already ranks as the top hazard. **That argues for a construction-time obligation: registering the reference is part of the write that creates the record, not a reconciliation job.** **(iii) It inherits [AB-52](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s open pressure**, which is the honest cost of taking the node index as the model: that row's complaint is that `EphemeraPositionAdjacency` answers *immediate host* and degrades to an unbounded multi-hop walk once Object, Feature and Area are hosts. A chain index built on the same shape gets the same problem, and gets it without a separate decision.

**A finding this section records but does not act on: [EA-7](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-7)'s closure of *containment between edges* has a new argument against it.** That row closed candidate (b) **on cost, and on cost alone** --- a second hosting story, a second port story, a second conflict rule. **Chains being multi-hosted the way nodes are makes the first of those three not a second story but the first one applied to a new kind of referent**, and separately supplies the requirement the row recorded as unmet (*containment over edges needs an edge nameable as a terminal, which EA-8 does not supply*). **Two of three grounds have moved from one piece of work.** **Three cautions before anyone acts on it.** The **port story is untouched** --- *there is no port on a port* is about a locus finer than the referent, and multi-hosting supplies none. **EA-7's containment question is about Arrangement *membership*, not about justification recording**, and EA-1's finding (*composition does not recover which members support which claim*) means a stated justification relation survives whichever way EA-7 goes --- containment would change how membership works, not replace `JustifiedBy`. And **EA-7's verdict is [EP-1](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#proposal-ep-1-the-arrangement--an-edge-side-abstraction-as-a-component-with-outbound-membership)'s clause 2**, per that row's own 2026-08-28 amendment, so re-opening it edits a proposal clause rather than a lone row. **This proposal does not re-open it; it records that the ground has shifted and leaves the act to whoever owns the row.**

**Chain terminals answer the ambiguity that opened this whole line of work.** [EA-10](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-10)'s requirement --- *the terminal of an edge must be able to be another edge* --- was ambiguous between leg-to-leg and leg-to-chain once the two were told apart. **It means leg to *chain*.** A terminal pointing at a leg would point at a tuning artifact; a terminal pointing at a chain points at something the fiction can keep.

**Consistency: atomicity for mutation, reconciliation across the abstraction lattice, and *not* atomic consistency.** A mutation is atomic **at its site**. It does **not** propagate atomically to every abstraction that states the relation. Divergence across the lattice is resolved by **reconciliation** --- the same way a coarse reading and a detailed reading of one world are related as siblings rather than held identical. **This is what the *may not contradict* floor buys, and it buys it without atomicity across the lattice**, which is the expensive reading and is now rejected.

**So chain-scoped identity is a *consistency* affordance, not a *representation* constraint --- and that fits the shipped field better than the alternative did.** Two records naming one truth are not illegal to leave unlinked; linking them is what lets a later reader understand them as two views of one thing. **[The thread that jams in the keyhole](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#ea-c6-the-thread-that-jams-in-the-keyhole) is the case that says so**, and it was answered in exactly these terms: *"whatever mechanism reasons about the machine has a stake in matching the new edge to the old, and without stored identity that matching has to be reinvented dynamically."* **Expensive to reinvent, not illegal to lack.** [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)'s *"an optional label an edge may carry, and nothing more"* reads naturally under this and had to be explained away under the legality reading.

**The honest cost, recorded so the reading is not adopted on comfort.** A hint that nothing enforces **can be wrong** --- one chain given two ids, or two chains given one. Under a legality reading that is a validation error; under this one it is data that quietly degrades reasoning. **Whether that is tolerable turns on whether anything makes a *decision* on the identity**, and the keyhole reasoner does exactly that. ~~**Unchecked.**~~ **Checked 2026-08-29 against [EA-C6](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#read-against-p8s-chain-scoping-2026-08-29-and-the-case-can-carry-less-of-this-than-the-booking-assumed): tolerable for *absence*, not obviously tolerable for *wrongness*, and the two are not symmetric.** A missing id makes the reasoner reinvent the match --- expensive, and it fails openly. **A wrong id makes it answer confidently that a connection it never saw is the one it knew.** **Chain-scoping does not widen the exposure**, and a first pass claiming it did --- *asserted at every leg, so wrongness scales with leg count* --- **was withdrawn the same day**: a re-tuning split hands the new legs an id the splitting caller already holds, so those are copies of a known value rather than decisions. **The hazard is [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)-era, not P8-added**, and it is [item 8](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s already-booked business rather than a new reason for it.

**What this does not do, stated because it looks as though it should.** It does **not** discharge [the *is the demand witnessed* question](#what-it-does-not-resolve). That asks whether **edge multi-membership** --- one relation as a **member** of two abstractions --- is attested. The candlestick-to-fork relation may be *stated* in two abstractions, which is weaker; it is not *part of* the place setting in the fiction. **P8's motivating demand remains unwitnessed**, and this case must not be counted as the corpus search coming back positive.

**How this section was reached, kept in one paragraph because the error class is the reusable part.** The first pass argued that crossing a boundary is **not elective**, and concluded that legs must therefore be **derived rather than recorded** and that candidates 1 and 3 were unavailable. **That argument equivocated** between *traversal* (to walk to the fork you must enter some graph holding it --- true, and it needs one route) and *representation* (the record must show every crossing --- a completeness demand, imported without notice). **The user caught it as a completeness smuggle**, which is the same class of error as reading a partial abstraction as complete --- **committed while writing the section that warns against it.** Logged in the [performance tally](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.performance.md).

**A naming hazard for whoever writes this up as a case.** [LC9](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc9-the-place-setting-laid-across-two-tables) is already *the place setting laid across two tables* --- a different shape, one Arrangement in two caches, contingent on [EA-6](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-6). **Do not reuse place-setting vocabulary for the diamond.**

### The unstated assumption this contests, and the argued rule it leaves alone

**Two claims about edges have travelled together and only one has ever been argued.**

- ***Edges are leaves: targetable, never containers.*** **Argued, cited, load-bearing** --- [EA-7](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-7)'s grading calls it *"a load-bearing rule rather than an incidental preference,"* because it is what keeps candidate (b)'s second-hosting-story cost off the table.
- ***Edges are values*** --- inline, matched structurally, no identity consulted by any comparison. **Never argued anywhere.** It is a storage and identity claim, and it is presupposed by the locked frame, by `edgesMatch`, by AB-52, by PR-9 and by both EP proposals.
    - **Corrected 2026-08-29: this claim used to read *inline in exactly one graph's `edges` array*, and that phrase describes a *leg*.** A **leg** lies wholly inside one graph; a **chain** may cross boundaries and have legs in several graphs' arrays, **and it may do so today with nothing naming it** --- which is [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s dissolution stated from the other side. **So the *one graph* half was never part of the unargued claim; only *inline, structural, no identity consulted* was.** Keeping the two apart matters here more than anywhere, because this section is where the claim is contested.

**P8 contests the second and leaves the first intact.** An edge with independent existence contains no other edge and is still a leaf. **State this before anything else, because the first misreading available is that P8 revives [EA-7](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-7)(b)** --- a containment graph *between* edges --- **which it does not**: (b)'s cost was a second hosting story, and P8 gives edges storage without giving them a graph, ports, or members.

### What already exists to build on (assessed against code, 2026-08-28)

- **`EphemeraLudicGraphData`** is `{ hostId, rootId, nodes, edges?, ports }`, and **`edges?: EphemeraLudicRelationalEdgeData[]`** --- **each array entry is a *leg*, inline as a value in that one graph's array.** **The per-graph array is the code fact; *the relationship lives in one graph* is not one** --- a chain crossing a boundary already has entries in several arrays, linked by nothing.
- **Nodes are not.** `EphemeraLudicGraphNode` carries a `universalKey`, so a node is a **reference** and genuinely appears in two graphs at once. **[LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption)'s containment diamond is legal for exactly this reason**, and the presence lane relies on it as the standing counter-example to membership being unique.
- **So co-membership is free on the node side and does not exist on the edge side**, and the asymmetry is not arbitrary: it is [EA-5](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-5)'s asymmetry one level down --- *a node can move and remain itself because it has position; an edge cannot, because it **is** its endpoints.*
- **`edgeId` exists and is inert.** `edgeId?: string` sits on the shared base ([EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register), shipped in `dd24e208d`), and the type's own comment records it as *"an optional label an edge may carry, and nothing more."* **No constructor mints one; no comparison consults one.** Verified in code: `addRelationalEdge` dedups by `edgesMatch` and `removeRelationalEdge` filters by it, both purely structural.
- **Nothing enforces uniqueness of `edgeId`**, within a graph or across graphs --- the validator checks only that it is a non-empty string.
- **`crossings` is proposed, not shipped.** [CC1b](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order)'s join produces an edge carrying `crossings`; **no such field exists in `ephemeraMeta.ts`.** Do not cite it as substrate.

### The proposal

**Clause numbers are stable and cited.**

1. **An edge may have independent existence** --- stored as its own record rather than inline in a graph's `edges` array. **It gains storage and identity; it does not gain a graph, ports, or members**, so *edges are leaves* holds unchanged.
2. **Identity for such an edge is minted and unique, not structural.** This is the clause that touches a **graduated** row: [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register) holds that identity is *optional*, and P8 makes it **required** for any edge with independent existence. **There is a reading on which this continues EA-8 rather than reversing it** --- EA-8 graduated on *structural keying is a uniqueness constraint on the fiction rather than a neutral reference mechanism*, and a minted key is what **relieves** that constraint, letting two structurally identical edges be two edges. **Recorded as a reading to be checked against [EA-C6](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#ea-c6-the-thread-that-jams-in-the-keyhole), not as an established one**, and the change goes **through EA-8's row** rather than riding in here. **--- Sharpened 2026-08-28 by [the resolution](#the-fork-inside-p8-whole-path-or-per-leg): the minted identity is *chain-scoped*.** It is not one id per stored record; it is one id per relationship, carried by every leg of it. **And the reading above is no longer a reading: EA-8's row states it directly** --- *"structural keying is a uniqueness constraint on the fiction, not a neutral reference mechanism, so identity is required exactly where that constraint must be violated."* **So clause 2 continues EA-8 rather than reversing it, on the row's own words rather than on an inference from its artifact**, and the EA-C6 check above is owed for the *chain-scoping*, not for the continuation. **EA-8 needs no amendment from this proposal** --- see [what it already booked and what P8 discharges](#what-ea-8-already-booked-and-what-p8-discharges). **--- Checked 2026-08-29: [confirmed](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#read-against-p8s-chain-scoping-2026-08-29-and-the-case-can-carry-less-of-this-than-the-booking-assumed), and the strength must be stated with it.** EA-C6's relation is **single-leg** --- thread and keyhole are both nodes, nothing crosses a boundary --- so a chain of one leg is indistinguishable from EA-8's flat id and **the case cannot discriminate chain-scope from record-scope.** This booking asked for more than the case can supply. **What it does return is corroboration of the principle from a second mover**: the resolution scopes the id above the leg because *leg counts move under re-tuning*, and EA-C6 says the same because *defining fields move under a beat*. **And the continuity it demands is bought exactly as this clause says it is** --- the relabel's `add` carries the id, so identity crosses a delete-plus-add that structure cannot. **No update op is owed**, and the only live question is whether that `add` can obtain the id, which is [item 9](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s trigger and not a further item.
3. **A graph's edge entry becomes an *occurrence* rather than the edge itself**, in three degrees of localness: **(a)** a bare reference, when no terminal of any leg is local; **(b)** partial, when one terminal is local --- so an occurrence may carry **only a subject or only a target**; **(c)** more than one entry, when more than one leg surfaces in the same graph. *(The worked shape: an edge leaving a room, entering an object, running port-to-port inside it, and emerging back into the same room has two occurrences in that room.)*
4. **Occurrence is discoverable in reverse** --- edge to the graphs it occurs in.
5. **Occurrences that carry content must be updated atomically with the edge**, across every graph the reverse index names. **--- Bounded 2026-08-28 by [the resolution](#the-fork-inside-p8-whole-path-or-per-leg): the set is *the legs of one chain*, not every graph any leg touches.** The distinction is the whole of discriminator 1.

**Clauses 4 and 5 are where the cost lives, and their price is set by the fork below rather than by the clauses themselves.**

### The fork inside P8: whole-path or per-leg

**Deliberately undecided, on the user's instruction 2026-08-28.** Both readings satisfy clauses 1--3; they differ on what one record *is*, and that difference re-prices clauses 4 and 5.

| | **A --- whole-path** | **B --- per-leg** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **One record is** | the entire path, all legs, from origin terminal to final terminal | one leg, terminating on nodes or ports, wholly inside one graph |
| **The path is** | a **stored fact** | **composed** --- which is [CC1b](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order)'s join, and the `ludicCache` corpus's edge-stitch/edge-merge |

**Why B is worth taking seriously rather than treating as the weaker half: the shipped design already holds B's position on storage, and holds it load-bearingly.** [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s cover ranges over **nodes only**, and its edge half was **dissolved rather than decided** --- *"a boundary-spanning edge is already two edges terminating at ports, one lying wholly inside each bucket … it is not one edge."*

**PR-9's dissolution is a constraint the resolution satisfies, not an asymmetry between the two options.** It is about **storage and hosting** --- what lies wholly inside one bucket --- so per-leg *hosting* leaves it entirely intact even with a chain-level identity above it. **It rules out A's hosting story and says nothing against a chain identity**, because PR-9 was never asked about identity.

**What discriminates them --- the list the comparison table is for.**

1. **Mutation cost.** **A** fans out to every graph any leg touches, and that set is unbounded. **B** touches one leg's occurrences, often one graph. **This is the runaway the fork exists to avoid**, and it is not a small effect: an unbounded *synchronous* footprint is precisely what this plan traded away deliberately (*"unbounded synchronous becomes bounded synchronous plus unbounded asynchronous --- the right trade precisely because the synchronous path is the player-facing one"*).
2. **What carries *these legs are one edge*.** **A**: the record. **B**: nothing yet --- either it is derived from shared port terminals (CC1b's join) or it needs a grouping key. **Neither A nor B is what is adopted: a third position is, and it is the row the resolution turns on.** **B with a shared key across legs** buys B's mutation locality *and* A's stored path --- see [the resolution](#resolution-the-fork-splits-by-concern-rather-than-resolving-to-a-side-2026-08-28).
3. **[LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption)'s second job**, which is the strongest thing P8 buys at all (see below). **A** inherits the fix outright, the record persisting independently of any graph's perspective. **B inherits it only for legs** --- each leg record survives, but the *linkage* between them is exactly what LC8 warns can be dropped and never learned about again. **So discriminator 3 is downstream of discriminator 2**, and they must not be scored independently.
4. **PR-9's dissolution**, above. B free, A re-opens it.
5. **P7.** B is what CC1b's join presumes. Under A the join is unnecessary, or becomes a materialization step. **The fork therefore bears on an unwritten proposal**, and P7 should not be written as though this were settled.

#### Resolution: the fork splits by concern rather than resolving to a side (2026-08-28)

**Neither A nor B. The two readings were rival answers to what looked like one question, and it is two.** Once *leg* and *chain* are separated, each concern attaches to whichever of them it actually answers to:

- **Identity and reference adhere to the *chain*.** `edgeId` names the chain. **This is the re-tunability argument above**: a leg count moves under re-tuning, so nothing durable may be keyed to it.
- **Hosting, adjacency, and mutation *site* adhere to the *leg*.** A leg lies wholly inside one graph and is what that graph can see, host, and traverse. **This is what keeps [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s dissolution intact and discriminator 1's runaway off the table.**
- **Mutation of a chain is atomic across the legs of that chain**, located through the reverse index on the chain identity --- clause 5, now with a bounded fan-out: **the legs of one chain, not every graph any leg touches.** **Bounded further 2026-08-29, and this is the cheaper reading rather than a looser one:** atomicity holds **at the mutation site**, and does **not** propagate atomically to every abstraction that states the relation. Cross-lattice divergence is resolved by **reconciliation**, not by an atomic write --- see [multi-abstraction wrapping](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28). **Atomic consistency across the lattice is rejected**, which is what the truth-side reverse index in *What it costs* was being bought for.

**No new leg identity is needed, and this is the economy of the split.** A leg is identified by its endpoints, which is what the substrate already stores and what all five structure-as-identity call sites already compare. **A `(chainId, index)` scheme is rejected outright** --- it invents a second identifier for a job structure already does. ~~**It returns in exactly one case:** a chain that visits the same endpoint pair twice, i.e. **self-intersects**. **Recommendation, not a decision: forbid self-intersecting chains explicitly**, since ruling them out is cheaper than supporting them and an unstated rule here surfaces as a bug rather than as a design question.~~ **--- Synced 2026-08-29 by the internal consistency pass: the rejection is *unconditional*, because the one case that would have resurrected a per-leg identifier does not exist.** **A chain is structurally incapable of self-intersecting**, on locked-frame clause 1 alone: a crossing port is **degree-2**, terminals are port-qualified, so a leg is in bijection with a **port-plus-direction** and one leg twice in a run demands one port traversed the same way twice, which **single-use forecloses.** **The word is *structurally impossible*, not *forbidden*** --- the latter implies a guard, an owner, and a rule a later reader must remember. **Full derivation, its falsifier, and the two near-misses that do not bear on it (AB-63's containment cycles, AB-13's diamonds) are at [what it does not resolve](#what-it-does-not-resolve)**, which is where this was worked; this passage previously contradicted it and now does not.

**Findings table.** Rows 1--4 closed by the resolution above; the *decided by* column is what keeps a filled row from reading as a preference.

| Discriminator | A --- whole-path | B --- per-leg | Decided by | Date |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Mutation cost | Unbounded fan-out over every graph any leg touches | Bounded to one leg's occurrences | **Split.** Mutation site is per-leg, so B's bound holds; atomicity is scoped to *the legs of one chain*, which is bounded by the chain rather than by the graph set | 2026-08-28 |
| 2. What carries the path | The record | **A shared `edgeId` at chain scope** --- the third position, adopted | **Split**, and this is the row the resolution turns on. Neither *stored path* nor *derived by join*: the linkage is carried by a chain-scoped identity on per-leg records. **Run against [LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption) 2026-08-29: corroborated, not discriminated, and the difference is worth keeping.** LC8 does **not** separate A from adopted-B --- under A the path is one record and under adopted-B the key carries it, so the linkage is present either way. **What it separates out is *bare* B**, join-derivation with no key. ~~which nobody holds.~~ **--- Corrected 2026-08-29 by [AB-65](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s answer, and the correction matters to how the rest of this cell reads: bare B *is* held, for the *inline unidentified* representation, which is the vast majority of edges.** It is not a rejected option; it is the common mode, with the chain join-derived rather than carried. **So the window below is the normal condition for most edges rather than a transient**, and only an **identified** edge escapes it --- which is what a reader must not generalize from *a key knows on arrival*. What it exhibits is a **window**: between the room's consolidation and the intervening whole's merge, the interior leg is absent, so [CC1b](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order)'s join has nothing to compose through and cannot yet know the two half-edges are one relationship, where a key knows on arrival. **The window is only interesting because reading a cache mid-fold is this case's whole hazard family.** **A first pass claimed more --- that the half-edges' *different* ports rule the join out structurally --- and that was wrong**: a join composes **adjacent** legs, which share a port by construction on a degree-2 crossing port; these two are non-adjacent, which is a missing leg and not a defect in the join. See [`AGENT.designVariant.performance.md`](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.performance.md) | 2026-08-28, corroborated 2026-08-29 |
| 3. LC8's second job | Inherited outright | Inherited, because the linkage now survives | **Downstream of row 2**, as flagged. With chain identity on each leg, a surviving leg carries the fact that it belongs to a chain, so the linkage is not the thing that gets silently dropped. **--- Verdict confirmed 2026-08-29, reason replaced.** The re-grade finds it is **not** chain identity that saves LC8's second job: **clauses 1 and 3(b)** do, by making a half-grounded leg representable and so retainable, and LC8's loss is a **representation gap rather than a reducer-interface one**. Chain identity buys the *legibility* of the pair before the intervening whole merges, which is a different hazard the case raises without naming. **Clause 4 is the wrong index for the recovery and must not be read as discharging it** --- it runs edge-to-graphs, and the case's need runs the other way | 2026-08-28, corpus-confirmed 2026-08-29 |
| 4. PR-9's dissolution | Re-opens it | Free | **Not an asymmetry after all.** PR-9 is about hosting; per-leg hosting satisfies it, and chain identity is not in its scope. See the correction above | 2026-08-28 |
| 5. Consequences for P7 | | | **Open.** Left empty deliberately --- the join's status under a chain-keyed model has not been worked, and P7 is the author's to write | |

### When are two legs the same leg? The comparison rule

**Added 2026-08-28, and it supplies a rule [EA-10](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-10)'s survey was already waiting for**: that survey classifies five call sites as *structure-as-identity* and says of them *"each must become id-aware; none is incoherent"* --- without saying what id-aware comparison **is**. This is that. **Four are live**; `equals` has no non-test caller.

**Chain-scoped identity does not break dedup, and the reason is worth stating because the opposite is the intuitive reading** --- two legs of one chain in the same graph do share an `edgeId`. **That is not a collision, for two independent reasons.** Comparison does not consult `edgeId` today at all ([EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register) made it inert), and two legs of one chain have **different endpoints**, so structural matching already separates them. **Multiple same-`edgeId` legs in one graph is not a defect; it is what a chain crossing a boundary looks like from inside** --- and in the `ludicCache` it is the point, since it is how the cache can know that three legs it holds are one relationship.

**The breakage is real but relocated, and the relocated form is sharper.** Structural dedup is correct *today* precisely because an edge is a value: two structurally identical relations **are** the same relation, so collapsing them loses nothing. **Give edges chain identity and that stops being true.** Two legs with identical endpoints and kind belonging to **different chains** are now different facts, and `addRelationalEdge` will **silently collapse them, destroying one chain's leg.** Same-chain collision is harmless; **cross-chain collision is silent data loss.**

**The rule, as four cases:**

| Both `edgeId`s | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| **Absent** | **Structure decides** --- today's behaviour, preserved unchanged |
| **Present and equal** | **Same leg** |
| **Present and differing** | **Different legs**, though structurally identical. **This is the case the rule exists for** |
| **One present, one absent** | **Different legs. Decided 2026-08-29** --- no automatic absorption. See below |

**The mixed pair is a genuine fork and is not decided here.** An id-less leg means *some relation of this shape*; an id-bearing one means *this chain's leg*. **Call them different** and the same fictional relation is stored twice, once anonymously --- silent duplication, which is the failure the rule exists to prevent, arriving by the other door. **Call them the same** and adding an id-bearing leg **absorbs** the anonymous one, which reads as *naming a relation that was already there*, and makes minting an id a well-defined operation on existing structure rather than a separate write --- **but it is asymmetric**, since absorption changes an existing edge's identity, so *add* stops being purely additive. ~~**Lean recorded as a lean, not as a finding: same, with absorption**, because it makes the anonymous edge the general case and the id a refinement of it, which is the shape the rest of this design already has --- coarse by default, detail minted on demand.~~

**Decided 2026-08-29 against that lean: different legs, and the lean is abandoned rather than narrowed.** **Chain membership becomes an explicit authoring act** --- labelling a path as an instance of a given Edge is something an author does, not something a comparison infers.

- **The asymmetry the lean conceded is wider than it was priced at, and that is the deciding fact.** It was priced at `add` alone. But the rule feeds **four live sites**, and a *same* verdict on a mixed pair reaches all of them: `removeRelationalEdge` would filter out an **anonymous** leg when asked to remove a named one, and `findMatchingEdge` would return an anonymous leg **as** the named one. Absorption is not one function losing additivity; it is *structurally identical is good enough to stand in for the named thing*, everywhere the rule is consulted.
- **And that is the proposition this design exists to deny.** [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register) graduated on *structural keying is a uniqueness constraint on the fiction, not a neutral reference mechanism*, and [EA-C6](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#ea-c6-the-thread-that-jams-in-the-keyhole) is a worked case where structure fails as a continuity witness. **Absorption re-promotes structural matching to identity-conferring** at the one place the rule was written to be careful.
- **The duplication cost is real but the door is already open.** *Present and differing* is **Different legs** whether or not the structures match, so this design already admits visible structural duplicates when identities differ. **The mixed pair is that same rule with one identity unstated**, not a new tolerance.
- **What it costs, accepted rather than argued away: retro-naming stops being free.** Absorption was what made *naming a relation that is already there* well-defined. Without it that needs a **real operation** --- see [item 10](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires), which is the obligation this decision incurs --- because `op` is `add | remove` and a delete-plus-add depends on both halves landing in one window, which EA-C6 is precisely the finding that nothing promises.
- **Where the unverified port-coarseness question now bites.** It no longer bears on the **verdict**, which is the same however coarse ports are; it bears on **how often** a flagged near-collision arises, and so on the price of noticing one. **That relocates the check from this rule to [item 8](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires) rather than discharging it.** **--- And item 8 relocated it once more, 2026-08-30, to [`CE2`](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#recommended-order)**, where it prices the *flag, forbid or stay silent* tier of the mint-time check. **Two relocations and no discharge is the honest record**: the question has never been answered, only moved to where answering it would cost something.

**What decides how urgent this is ---** ~~and it has not been checked~~ **and it is [PQ-14](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused)'s, not this section's.** Cross-chain collision requires two chains to produce **structurally identical legs**, which depends on **how coarse ports are**. If a port is per-crossing, endpoints stay unique and the collision never arises. If ports are **shared** --- many chains crossing one abstraction boundary through one port pair --- two chains from the same node through the same port produce identical legs and it arises immediately. ~~**Instinct is that abstraction ports are the shared kind, which would make this reachable rather than theoretical. Not verified; cheap to verify; do that before pricing the rule.**~~ **--- Synced 2026-08-29 by the internal consistency pass, and the instinct was not unverified so much as *uncited*.** **[PQ-14](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) has held this question since 2026-08-10** --- *does single-use survive multi-level chains, i.e. may one port carry more than one interior edge?* --- **so P8 restated a live row as an open question of its own.** **What PQ-14 supplies is narrower than the blanket form asked here, and the narrowing is what matters:** single-use's payoffs depend on a port having **one egress**, not one *interior* edge, so the live candidate is **interior fan-in under a shared lifecycle**, not shared ports generally. **Under that candidate the legs are `(H#{p}, A)` and `(H#{p}, B)` --- distinct --- so the collision this paragraph feared does not follow from the relaxation actually on the table.** **Two consequences for this rule, and neither changes the verdict.** The **mixed-pair verdict does not turn on it** at all: *different legs* holds however coarse ports are. What it prices is **how often a near-collision is worth flagging**, which is [item 8](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s business --- so the check is **relocated, not discharged**, and it is PQ-14's to answer rather than this section's to re-ask.

### What it buys

- **It closes [LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption)'s second job, which is the highest-severity unworked concern in that corpus** --- *sub-graphing drops things*, and the bad reading is that a discarded edge is *"unrecoverable by any later step, and no re-merge and no ordering rescues it."* LC8 flags that as unusual: not fast/slow but **permanently missing an edge it can never learn about.** **An edge whose existence does not depend on any graph's perspective cannot have that failure** --- at worst it is unresolved, which is recoverable. **Subject to fork discriminator 3.**
- **It answers [EA-10](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#ea-10)'s requirement in the vocabulary that requirement was missing.** *The terminal of an edge must be able to be another edge* is ambiguous once leg and chain are told apart, and **it means leg to *chain*.** A terminal pointing at a leg points at a tuning artifact; a terminal pointing at a chain points at something the fiction can keep --- and a chain is referenceable **with no leg present**, which is what makes the terminal expressive rather than merely legal. **Added 2026-08-29**; see [multi-abstraction wrapping](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28).
- **Edge co-membership becomes possible at all.** Overlapping abstractions sharing a node set can hold the edges *between* those nodes as shared members rather than as duplicates, with a stated consistency mechanism instead of an implicit one.
- **It dissolves the asymmetry that generated it**, and that is a consequence worth stating against the lane it came from: the reason [EP-2](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#proposal-ep-2-one-abstraction-structure-used-at-different-weights) cannot multi-host edges is that edges are values. **Under P8 both EP-1 and EP-2 can**, so **the EP-1/EP-2 fork may not be graded on edge multi-hosting while P8 is open.**

### What it costs

- **A truth-side reverse index, and [AB-52](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s does not subsidize it.** AB-52's leading candidate is measured into `ludicCache` and takes [P6](#proposal-p6-ludiccache-as-the-attention-scoped-reference-structure) clause 4's licence --- *an error costs slow, never wrong*. **Clause 5 gates a write, which cannot take that licence**, and AB-52 says so about itself: *"a **write** path needing upward resolution would break it, since a write cannot depend on cache warmth."* **Re-priced 2026-08-29 and it is the largest cost this proposal has shed:** with atomic consistency across the lattice rejected, clause 5's write no longer has to reach every abstraction stating the relation, so the index it needs is **at the mutation site**, not truth-side over the whole lattice. **What remains is reconciliation**, which is a read-side job and *can* take P6 clause 4's licence. **The remainder of this bullet prices the *atomic* reading and is retained as the cost of the option not taken.** **These are two different indexes.** The truth-side one is the denormalization this plan has rejected twice, most recently as *position 3 growing back*, so it owes [PQ-1](#the-port-record-split-complementary-with-the-interior-authoritative-settled-2026-08-06)'s *what does it save* bar on its own evidence.
- **Resolution must land in grounding.** `computeStepSequenceFootprint` runs once and `MultiKeyUpdate` cannot be re-entered, so the reverse-index read happens before the footprint is computed or it is *a second round trip wearing one's clothes*.
- **An unbounded synchronous footprint under fork A**, per discriminator 1.
- **No change to a graduated row, and this is a cost the proposal does not incur.** Clause 2 **discharges** an obligation [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register) booked rather than amending it, so nothing in the Settled register is re-opened; [see the discharge note](#what-ea-8-already-booked-and-what-p8-discharges). **The cost that replaces it is smaller and belongs to nobody yet:** something must notice a structural collision **at mint time**, and nothing does.
- **`edgesMatch` needs a rule it does not have.** If a graph can hold a full edge *and* an occurrence of a structurally identical edge elsewhere, `addRelationalEdge`'s dedup and `removeRelationalEdge`'s filter are both ambiguous about whether those are one member or two. **First place P8 touches shipped code, and it touches it at dedup semantics rather than at storage.** **--- Supplied 2026-08-28: [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule)**, in three cases of four. **--- Discharged 2026-08-29: the mixed pair reads *different legs*, so the rule is complete in all four cases and `edgesMatch` has the rule it lacked.** **What replaces the cost is smaller and named:** no comparison mutates anything, and the retro-naming absorption would have handled is now [item 10](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s explicit operation.
- **A second identity field on the leg --- `chainId` beside `edgeId` --- priced 2026-08-29 as a cost of graduating P8 to a prototype.** **The framing that produced it: there are two identities wanted by two different holders, and one field was being asked to serve both.** The **reasoning** engine needs the *semantic fact* as a unit, however it is routed and however many chains it is present as; **addressing** needs the *route* that reaches it, and per [leg and chain](#leg-and-chain-and-why-the-corpus-does-not-reliably-distinguish-them) a route is **never identifying**. **Neither is derivable from the other, so neither can be dropped**, and the long-running *what does `edgeId` identify?* question is ill-posed rather than open --- it presumes one answer where two holders each have one. **Form: sibling optional fields, not a port-style compound key.** The compound reading (`EDGE#...#{chainId}`, one field, no retyping) was considered and set aside on the shipped precedent it appealed to: a port-qualified terminal is **not** a `#`-joined string but a structured `EphemeraLudicPortAddress` --- `{ owner, port }` with `ephemeraLudicTerminalOwner` stripping the qualifier --- so the codebase's own answer to *identity plus a qualifier, where consumers need both the whole and the base* is **two fields and an accessor**. **Priced against a deferred migration, the sibling add is the cheaper of the two now as well as later.** **What it costs, in full:** `chainId?: string` beside `edgeId?: string` on `EphemeraLudicRelationalEdgeBase`, one line in the guard, and the carrier sites that `removeRelationalEdge`'s re-serialisation composes --- which the round-trip test in `ludicGraph/index.test.ts` was written to catch a half-done carry of. **Both fields stay optional, so [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)'s mixed population is undisturbed and nothing's behavior changes on the add.** **What it does *not* buy, stated because it looks as though it should:** it does not decide whether a second author drawing an already-existing relationship by a different route **finds** that `edgeId` or **mints** a new one. **The split localizes that fork to one call site; it does not close it.** **--- Closed 2026-08-29 by [item 10](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s assign operation, as a side effect of the mixed-pair verdict rather than by being worked: *finds*.** An Edge record carries **endpoints and N chains**, so the second route is a **new chain on the existing Edge**, and the operation's endpoint check is the guard that keeps a mistaken find from attaching a chain to the wrong relationship. **Minting a second `edgeId` for the same relationship is now the thing an author does *wrongly*, not a permitted alternative.**

### What it changes conceptually

**Edges stop being values and become things with continuity.** *Where an edge lives* stops being a fact about storage and becomes a fact about **occurrence** --- an edge is *in* a graph the way a node is, by reference, and the graph's entry describes how much of it is locally visible. **The clean/dirty test applies and P8 passes it in the direction that keeps the model honest:** the edge record is **truth**, and graph-side occurrences are **materialization** --- *the clean answer, computed faster* --- rather than a second model wearing dirt's clothing.

### What it does not resolve

- **The leg-granularity fork, closed by splitting on concern** --- four discriminator rows filled. ~~**What remains of it is two items: row 5 (`P7`), and the mixed-pair case of [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule).**~~ **--- One item as of 2026-08-29: row 5 (`P7`) alone.** [The comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule)'s mixed pair reads **different legs**, so the rule is complete in all four cases; **what the decision left behind is not a residue of the fork but a build obligation**, [item 10](#what-graduating-the-leg-field-requires)'s assign/strip operations.
- ~~**Whether ports are per-crossing or shared**, which decides whether cross-chain leg collision is reachable at all. **Unchecked, and cheap to check.**~~ **Struck 2026-08-29: it was not unchecked, it was uncited.** [**PQ-14**](#open-questions-ab-34-sub-questions-ids-stable-never-reused) has held this since 2026-08-10 --- *does single-use survive multi-level chains, i.e. may one port carry more than one interior edge?* --- with a worked position P8 restated as an open question without noticing. **What PQ-14 supplies, and it is narrower than the blanket form asked here:** single-use's payoffs depend on a port having **one egress**, not one *interior* edge, so the live candidate is **interior fan-in under a shared lifecycle**, not shared ports generally. **Nothing in P8 turns on it** --- see the bullet below, which is the reason.
- ~~**Whether chains may self-intersect.** Recommended forbidden; **not decided**, and it is the only case that would resurrect a per-leg identifier.~~ **Resolved 2026-08-29: a chain is structurally incapable of self-intersecting, and *structurally impossible* rather than *forbidden* is the load-bearing word.** **The derivation, from [locked-frame](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) clause 1 alone.** A crossing port is **degree-2** --- one interior end, one exterior end, [no fan-in and no fan-out](#the-fan-out-cost-stated-precisely-because-it-is-the-schemes-real-risk) --- and a chain is a start node, N crossing ports, an end node. Terminals are port-qualified, so `H#{p1}` and `H#{p3}` are **different terminals in the same graph**. A leg is therefore in bijection with a **port-plus-direction**, and self-intersection --- one leg twice in one run --- **requires the same port traversed the same way twice**, which single-use forecloses. **[The cross-chain bullet above already stated it](#what-it-does-not-resolve)**: *"if a port is per-crossing, endpoints stay unique and the collision never arises."* **The near-miss that makes this look open, foreclosed explicitly because it is what the recent cases produce.** A **containment cycle** ([AB-63](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)) would let a run revisit a **graph** --- and revisiting a graph **is not self-intersection**, because the return arrives through a *different* port and the legs differ. Likewise [AB-13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only)'s multi-parent DAG: a diamond yields **two chains to one referent**, never one chain twice. **So neither AB-63 nor the multi-hosting attested all over the corpus bears on this**, and P8 takes no dependency on either. **Why the word matters, and the plan has settled this distinction twice before.** *Forbidden* implies an enforcement point and an owner --- a guard, a validator, a check at mint time --- and it is a rule a later reader must remember and apply. *Structurally impossible* needs none of that and cannot drift. This is [C18](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c18-three-place-settings-and-only-two-of-them-may-have-a-napkin) Finding 4's move --- *"unrepresentable rather than prohibited, which is the difference between an invariant and a warning"* --- and [C19](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c19-filling-out-the-place-setting-and-the-tavern-that-ran-out-of-napkins) Finding 4 calls that pattern's third arrival the reason to trust it. **This is its fourth.** **What replaces the owner is a falsifier, and it is a single condition:** this resolution fails **if and only if a port ever admits two crossings in the *same direction*.** **PQ-14's live pressure is not that** --- interior fan-in gives legs `(H#{p}, A)` and `(H#{p}, B)`, which are distinct --- so the relaxation currently under consideration cannot reopen this, and a future one must be read against this sentence before it is adopted. **What this deletes rather than resolves.** *The only case that would resurrect a per-leg identifier* is withdrawn: **nothing resurrects it**, so [the rejection of a `(chainId, index)` scheme](#the-fork-inside-p8-whole-path-or-per-leg) stops being conditional and holds outright. **Recorded because the first pass at this bullet argued the opposite** --- that self-intersection was *"a property of the run"* reachable through AB-63 --- and offered to write that dependency in; it could not produce a four-node instance because none exists. See [`AGENT.designVariant.performance.md`](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.performance.md).
- **The [EP-1/EP-2](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#how-ep-1-and-ep-2-discriminate--what-ea-c5-must-show) fork.** P8 is **upstream** of it and decides neither. What survives that fork under P8 is narrower: *does the membership list need its own component type, or is it a field on the whole's existing graph?*
- ~~**Whether the demand is witnessed at all.** Node multi-membership is attested ([LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption)). **Edge multi-membership --- one relation as a member of two abstractions --- is asserted nowhere in the corpus**, and the argument for it here is **symmetry, not fiction**. **A corpus search is the cheap decisive move and has not been run.** If it comes back empty, P8's motivating demand is unwitnessed and the proposal should say so in its Status rather than quietly keep its clauses.~~ **Struck 2026-08-29: the search was run, the demand is witnessed, and *symmetry, not fiction* is retired.** **[AB-54](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#settled-register) supplies the test and it is already settled** --- *hosting kinds put the subordinate node in the superior's own shard* --- so membership **is shard residency**, and the question is mechanical: **which shards hold a leg of this edge?** **Three witnesses, and the first two are in the corpus written for this subject.** **(i)** [EA-C1](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#ea-c1-the-power-cord-and-the-flashlight)'s `StrippedEnd -[SolderedTo]-> BatteryCase` --- `StrippedEnd` is a part of `PowerCord`, `BatteryCase` is `PartOf` `Flashlight`, **so one relation has a leg in each of two abstractions' shards**, and the case's own table records it in a column headed *crossing*. **(ii)** [EA-C2](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.corpus.planning.md#ea-c2-the-clamp-and-the-second-contact-point)'s `Clamp -[Secures]-> Cord`, the same two abstractions in the opposite orientation. **(iii)** [C6](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c6-the-rope-the-candle-and-the-impromptu-timer)'s candle-`Under`-span, the strongest of the three because [AB-13](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#open-decisions-design--plan-only) already certifies its claimants as different **in kind** --- *"neither containing the other"*, where *"a tree cannot express it at all."* **The test discriminates rather than admitting everything, and here is the negative case:** [PR-C1](AGENT.presence.corpus.planning.md#pr-c1-the-contraption-in-two-rooms-and-whether-covers-nest)'s `CableSpan1`--`CableSpan2` sits in **one** graph and crosses only a **bucket** boundary --- *"bucket boundaries and graph boundaries are not the same thing"* --- so it is **not** a witness. **What this changes and what it does not.** P8 keeps its clauses, and the ground under them shifts from symmetry to attestation. **It does not license the layering question** --- EA-C2's `SolderedTo`-plus-`Secures` fusing to `SecurelyThreadsInto` is one coarse edge as the view of a set, which is [EA-2](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)'s and not this demand. **One ruling is owed a re-read rather than reversed here:** [the candlestick disqualification](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28) turns on *stated in* versus *part of*, which is a claim about what the fiction treats as constitutive; **AB-54's test is leg-residency instead**, and the two need not agree. **Whoever owns that ruling should re-read it; it is not overturned by this bullet.** **The method finding, kept because the first run of this search returned the wrong answer.** It was run as a **phrasing search** --- looking for cases whose authors had already written *this edge is a member of these abstractions* --- and the corpus was never writing about edge membership, so it could not have returned anything. **It was amplified by a disqualifier invented for the sweep and never checked against the definition:** *multi-hosted is not multi-member*, **a line AB-54 does not draw.** **A sweep reported empty owes the definition it applied and one concrete setup that definition fails on**; see the entry in [`AGENT.designVariant.performance.md`](../../../../AGENT.designVariant.performance.md).
- **Whether an Edge entity needs presence.** Making an edge a stored thing gives it a stored thing's properties, and [PR-9](AGENT.presence.planning.md#settled-register)'s cover is **node-only**. [EP-1](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#the-proposal) clause 1 shows the shape of the work this implies --- four unions, and admission to the top of the stack buys nothing on its own.
- ~~**The lock check.** Clause 3's multi-leg occurrence must be read against [locked-frame](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) clause 2 through [the stated re-open procedure](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause), **not by assuming compatibility.** First reading is that it restates the lock rather than violating it --- the legs still cross via ports; the record merely spans them --- **and a first reading is not the procedure.**~~ **Run 2026-08-29 against [locked-frame](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#locked-frame-parts-and-ports-2026-08-06) clause 2: the verdict is *restates*, and the first reading's conclusion was right for a reason it did not give.** **(1) A proposal is not an admissible ground, so the obligation was mis-named.** [The procedure](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#what-would-re-open-a-locked-clause) admits exactly two things --- a corpus case a clause cannot represent (the [C7](AGENT.abstractionLayers.corpus.planning.md#c7-ariadnes-thread) standard), or a demonstration that clause 2's encapsulation fails **where it was bought**, meaning an interior repartitioning that still breaks external references --- and it expressly excludes *"a rival proposal that happens to also work."* **What P8 owed was a compatibility demonstration, not a re-open argument**; the two are different obligations, and only the second is what a proposal can even attempt. **The corpus route stays live and is [LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption)'s**, so nothing here pre-empts it. **(2) The durable text of clause 2 is narrower than this plan's copy of it, and the difference is the whole of the check.** The lock graduated to [`AGENT.concepts.md`](../../../../../lambda/ephemera/dataSource/positions/AGENT.concepts.md#wholes-parts-and-ports), which is *"now the durable statement"*, and there clause 2 carries an amendment this file's two-sentence copy does not: *"**'Owns' is about the *binding*, not about every value recorded on it** … This clause says a port exists only because its interior minted it and cannot be conjured from outside; it does **not** say the interior wins every disagreement between what a port records and what the exterior refers to,"* with *"reading an authority claim out of an ownership claim is the specific error to avoid here."* **Clause 2 is an ownership claim about minting, not an authority claim about content.** The reading on which P8 conflicts with it --- *edge content living in a record no single interior owns* --- is precisely the authority reading clause 2 disclaims, and the concepts entry records that it has been *"read away once"* already. **P8 mints no port from outside and claims no interior override**, and a chain record is an exterior-facing fact of exactly the kind that entry already says *"defer[s] to the exterior reference."* **Rows here argue from the short copy; the durable text is the one that governs.** **(3) Compatibility, checked per degree rather than in the aggregate, because the aggregate is what the first reading did.** **(a)** A bare reference names the chain, and **no crossing occurs at all** --- a citation is not a traversal. **(b)** A partial occurrence's non-local end is a **port-qualified terminal**, which *is* the explicit binding clause 2 requires; nothing names a node behind it, per the concepts entry's *"a port id is not a name for the interior node behind it."* **(c)** The worked two-occurrence shape is the decisive one: an edge leaving a room, running port-to-port inside an object and re-emerging, gives the room two legs **both terminating on the object's ports**. **No degree addresses an interior node from outside**, which is the clause verbatim. **(4) The second test is vacuous today, and P8 is what would make it live --- in the direction that supports the lock.** *An interior repartitioning that still breaks external references* has no instance to test, because **nothing holds an external reference to an edge**: [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)'s `edgeId` is optional, minted by nobody and consulted by nobody. P8 creates the first such references and creates them in the one form that **survives** repartitioning --- identity held apart from the route. **So clause 3 supplies clause 2's encapsulation claim its first testable case rather than threatening it.** **The falsifier, since (3) rests on *a citation is not a crossing* and that reading would be toothless if anything could be recast as citation:** this verdict fails **if a bare reference ever resolves into a traversal that lands on an interior node without a port hop.** Reverse-index resolution returns records, not routes, and must keep doing so.

### What EA-8 already booked, and what P8 discharges

**[EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register) needs no amendment from this proposal, and the citation runs upward: P8 discharges an obligation that row already booked.**

**Read the row, not the type comment --- they say different things and only one of them is the verdict.** The comment's *"a mixed population… behaves today exactly as an all-id-less one does"* is the **safety property that let the field ship ahead of its consumer**, not the row's justification, and **inertness was always meant to end** --- that is what an option is for. **EA-8 did not merely leave use to later constructors; it named the obligation, the sites, and the decision:**

> *"It **does not lift the uniqueness constraint**: `addRelationalEdge` still refuses structural duplicates, so two connections alike in every recorded respect stay unrepresentable --- and lifting that guard is **not a single-site change**, because `removeRelationalEdge`, `applyRelationalPatch` and `findMatchingEdge` must become id-aware in the same change or a structural remove deletes edges it was not aimed at."*
>
> *"**The first constructor that mints an id must decide whether id or structure wins**, and that test is what forces the decision instead of letting it be discovered."*

**So [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule) is that decision, arriving on schedule** --- three cases of four answered, **the mixed pair being the residue**, and the mixed pair is precisely *whether id or structure wins* in the one case where they conflict. **EA-8's re-open trigger is untouched:** its claim is *optionality*, failing only if some site cannot carry a mixed population, and nothing here bears on that.

**P8 supplies EA-8 no new justification, and the one that looks new is EA-8's own.** *Two structurally identical relations may be different facts* is the row's graduation principle, in these words: *"structural keying is a uniqueness constraint on the fiction, not a neutral reference mechanism, so identity is required exactly where that constraint must be violated."* **Anything reading like fresh support for EA-8 should be checked against that sentence first**, which is what [Getting Started](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#getting-started) rule 4 warns about from the other direction.

**What genuinely remains open, and it is one thing.** EA-8 assigns the minting constructor the *id-versus-structure* decision, but **nothing is assigned the job of noticing that a collision exists** at mint time --- and because the field is optional, nothing forces the question to be asked. **That is unowned rather than owed by EA-8**, and it should not be filed on a Settled row, which would make a closed row read as carrying a debt. **A new open row is the right home; none is proposed here.** Two lesser residues sit with it: **that both members of a colliding pair need ids** (one id-bearing plus one anonymous merges under absorption), which sharpens EA-8's delegated mint rule rather than changing it, and the mixed-pair case above.

### What graduating the leg field requires

**Retitled 2026-08-30 from *What graduating **this** requires*, because the old title over-claimed and had done since it was written.** *This* read as **P8**, and P8 is not what the list graduates --- the destination line below scopes it to the leg field and excludes the Edge record, the reverse index and presence by name. **A reader who finished ten items under the old heading would reasonably conclude the proposal was graduated; it is not, and only the field is.**

**Where the mismatch came from, checked against history rather than recalled: the narrowing and the list arrived in the same commit** (`ab754e96e`, 2026-08-29) --- neither is in its parent. **The list was asked for as *what will it take to graduate this proposal* and was written as a list for graduating part of it, with the narrowing carried as a premise inside the answer rather than raised as a decision.** That is the error the retitle addresses; the title was the symptom. **A later account attributing this to a naming choice made afterwards is wrong and is corrected here** --- the scope did not pre-date the list. **The retitle changes no scope and adds no item;** what it removes is a heading that let a partial list read as a whole one.

**The destination is [Prototype](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11), not Settled, and the build surface is one thing:** `chainId?: string` beside `edgeId?: string`, with the three decided cases of [the comparison rule](#when-are-two-legs-the-same-leg-the-comparison-rule) wired into `edgesMatch`. **No Edge record, no reverse index, no presence for edges** --- those stay proposal-side, and they are why this list is short. **A field with no consumer is not a candidate target**: the tier exists to buy evidence, and nothing reads a field nothing reads. **That is what puts the mixed pair in scope** rather than deferring behind [EA-8](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.planning.md#settled-register)'s ship-ahead-of-consumer precedent.

**Items 1--3 are the discovery steps and are scheduled first because they are the only ones that can change the answer.** Each has a **closed outcome space**: it survives, which finishes the item, or it falsifies a clause, which returns P8 to open and **ends** this list rather than extending it. ~~**Neither branch can find work outside P8's own clauses**, which is the reason the list does not grow.~~ **Falsified by item 2 on 2026-08-29, and the correction is recorded rather than the claim softened.** The re-grade added **item 9**, and the reason it could is that the non-growth argument was drawn from *this proposal's clauses and build surface* and **never read the destination tier's own entry conditions** --- so a third branch existed that the closed-outcome argument did not cover: an item sourced from **Prototype** rather than from P8. **The repaired claim, and it is narrower:** the tier's conditions are **enumerable and have now been enumerated** --- [three of them](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11), two of which fold into item 9's third --- so that source is closed by exhaustion rather than by argument. **It stands, and it briefly did not.** Item 3 was written up as falsifying it a second time --- a tenth item, sourced from neither a failed clause nor the tier, on the ground that the outcome space was missing *survives, and owes*. **That item was withdrawn as unfounded and this claim is restored with it**, since nothing else had ever instanced the third branch. **Recorded rather than reverted silently, because it is the clearest available measure of what a manufactured finding costs:** one invented residue retired a live claim two paragraphs away, and the retirement read as the more rigorous move. **The per-item discipline it proposed is kept anyway, being cheap and independently useful: each item states whether it grew the list and from where.** ~~**Items 4--8 are transcription plus one small change against a test that already exists, and writing them cannot surface anything new.**~~ **Falsified by item 4 on 2026-08-29, plainly and without needing a general lesson drawn from it:** item 4 was a **decision**, not a transcription, and deciding it against the recorded lean incurred **item 10**. **The band is *items 5--8*; item 4 belonged with the discovery steps and was mis-sorted because its lean was already written down**, which reads as settled work when it is a decision not yet taken.

**Excluded, each with its reason, because an unstated exclusion is indistinguishable from an oversight.**

- **Discriminator row 5 (`P7`).** The author's to write, and the concern-split means P8 no longer turns on it. **What is owed is the negative:** P7 must not be written as though this were settled.
- **[The candlestick disqualification](#multi-abstraction-wrapping-a-node-in-two-abstractions-and-what-chain-means-after-it-2026-08-28).** The row owner's call. It can change that ruling; **it cannot change P8's clauses.**
- **The corpus-wide leg/chain vocabulary sweep.** P8 defines both words locally, so the sweep buys other files' readability rather than this verdict. Its four concentrated risk sites are already named.
- **The reverse index, an Edge record, and presence for edges.** All downstream of storage this prototype does not build.
- **A corpus case that could have gone the other way. Added 2026-08-29 from [item 2](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption), and it is the exclusion most worth arguing with.** LC8 **defuses** rather than tests --- clause 3(b) dissolves its hazard --- so P8's evidence is still argument plus one against-code read plus one confirmation. **That is a bar for [Locked](AGENT.abstractionLayers.planning.md#graduation-tiers-when-a-decision-licenses-code-2026-08-11)** --- *corpus-grounded rather than merely argued* --- **and the destination here is Prototype, whose entire premise is that building is the affordable way to buy the evidence.** **What it costs to exclude, stated rather than waved off:** the slice must not be read as promoting P8, and item 9's rollback trigger is doing the work a falsification case would otherwise do.
- **[LC8](AGENT.abstractionLayers.ludicCache.corpus.planning.md#lc8-the-spring-in-the-box-and-in-the-contraption)'s *first* job --- whether a bare port-to-port leg exists at all.** Clause 3(c)'s worked example presumes one. **It bears on an illustration, not on a clause**, so P8 is not gated on it; if it comes back against the bare shape, the example is rewritten and the clauses stand.
- **The [EP-1/EP-2](AGENT.edgeAbstractions.proposals.planning.md#how-ep-1-and-ep-2-discriminate--what-ea-c5-must-show) fork.** P8 is upstream and decides neither --- **but the pressure runs the other way and is worth stating as urgency rather than as scope:** that fork may not be graded on edge multi-hosting while P8 is open, so **P8 sitting open is blocking a sibling lane.**
